theorem padicLogShell_isCompact : IsCompact (padicLogShell p)

IUT III Proposition 1.2 (v) (a_non), page 31, for the base field ℚ_p: the log-shell is compact.

IUT.Prerequisites.PadicLog.padicLogUnit_multheorem

theorem padicLogUnit_mul (u v : ℤ_[p]ˣ) :
  padicLogUnit p (((u * v : ℤ_[p]ˣ) : ℤ_[p]) : ℚ_[p]) =
    padicLogUnit p ((u : ℤ_[p]) : ℚ_[p]) + padicLogUnit p ((v : ℤ_[p]) : ℚ_[p])

log on units is a homomorphism ℤ_p^× → ℚ_p.

IUT.Prerequisites.PositiveRationalsZhatUnits

IUT/Prerequisites/PositiveRationalsZhatUnits.lean

The elementary fact ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1}

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Example 5.1 (v), PDF page 127 (rendered and read), where the injectivity of a Kummer map is deduced "from the elementary observation that, relative to the natural inclusion ℚ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ, ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1}". The same fact is invoked in Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 2.3.3 (vi), (vii), (viii), PDF pages 81 to 83 (text only, from the extracted text), and in Remark 3.12.1 (iii), PDF pages 186 and 187 (rendered and read), where it is described as playing a key role in the multiradial algorithms for cyclotomic rigidity isomorphisms in the number field case, and as an immediate consequence of an interpretation of the product formula.

This file is not a formalization of Example 5.1; it proves only the elementary arithmetic fact quoted above.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • Mathlib has no Ẑ. We model Ẑ as the product ∏_p ℤ_p, written Zhat, indexed by the nonzero primes of ℤ (IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum ℤ), with ℤ_p the ring v.adicCompletionIntegers ℚ of Mathlib.
  • Ẑ ⊗ ℚ is modeled by Mathlib's finite adele ring IsDedekindDomain.FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ, the restricted product of the ℚ_p with respect to the ℤ_p. The isomorphism Ẑ ⊗ ℚ ≅ 𝔸_f is standard but is not proved here. The inclusion ℚ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ is Mathlib's algebraMap ℚ (FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ), and Ẑ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ is the ring map Zhat.toFiniteAdeles defined below (the restricted product contains the full product of the integer rings).
  • ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× is read as the set of positive rationals whose image in 𝔸_f is the image of a unit of Ẑ. The main theorem posRat_inter_zhatUnits says this set is {1}.

The core of the proof is eq_one_of_forall_valuation_eq_one: a positive rational number that is a unit at every prime is 1. It uses Mathlib's IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum.mem_integers_of_valuation_le_one.

Public declarations

IUT.Prerequisites.Zhatabbrev

abbrev Zhat : Type := Π v : HeightOneSpectrum ℤ, v.adicCompletionIntegers ℚ

Ẑ, modeled as the product ∏_p ℤ_p of the rings of p-adic integers over the nonzero primes of ℤ.

IUT.Prerequisites.Zhat.toFiniteAdelesdef

noncomputable def Zhat.toFiniteAdeles : Zhat →+* FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ where
  toFun x := ⟨fun v => (x v : v.adicCompletion ℚ), Filter.Eventually.of_forall fun v => (x v).2⟩
  map_one' := rfl
  map_mul' _ _ := rfl
  map_zero' := rfl
  map_add' _ _ := rfl

The natural ring map Ẑ → Ẑ ⊗ ℚ, with Ẑ ⊗ ℚ modeled by the finite adeles of ℚ: an element of ∏_p ℤ_p is integral at every prime, so it lies in the restricted product.

IUT.Prerequisites.Zhat.toFiniteAdeles_applytheorem

@[simp]
theorem Zhat.toFiniteAdeles_apply (x : Zhat) (v : HeightOneSpectrum ℤ) :
    Zhat.toFiniteAdeles x v = (x v : v.adicCompletion ℚ)

IUT.Prerequisites.eq_one_of_forall_valuation_eq_onetheorem

theorem eq_one_of_forall_valuation_eq_one {q : ℚ} (hq : 0 < q)
  (h : ∀ v : HeightOneSpectrum ℤ, v.valuation ℚ q = 1) : q = 1

A positive rational number whose v-adic valuation is 1 at every nonzero prime v of ℤ is equal to 1.

IUT.Prerequisites.posRat_inter_zhatUnitstheorem

theorem posRat_inter_zhatUnits :
  {q : ℚ | 0 < q ∧ ∃ u : Zhatˣ,
    Zhat.toFiniteAdeles (u : Zhat) = algebraMap ℚ (FiniteAdeleRing ℤ ℚ) q} = {1}

**ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1}** (IUT I, page 127), relative to the natural inclusion ℚ ↪ Ẑ ⊗ ℚ = 𝔸_f: the positive rationals whose image in the finite adeles is the image of a unit of Ẑ = ∏_p ℤ_p are exactly {1}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ProductFormula

IUT/Prerequisites/ProductFormula.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.ArithmeticDivisor

Normalized absolute values and the product formula over 𝕍(F)

Module documentation

Sources:

  • Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Definition 1.9, PDF pages 21 and 22 (rendered and read): the set 𝕍(F) of valuations of a number field F, as the disjoint union of the nonarchimedean and archimedean valuations, formalized in IUT/PaperIV/ArithmeticDivisor.lean as IUT.PaperIV.Valuations F.
  • Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 3.12.1 (iii), PDF pages 186 and 187 (rendered and read), which describes the fact ℚ_{>0} ∩ Ẑ^× = {1} as an immediate consequence of an interpretation of the product formula.

This file is not a formalization of any numbered statement of the papers. It transports the product formula of Mathlib (NumberField.prod_abs_eq_one, in Mathlib/NumberTheory/NumberField/ProductFormula.lean) to the index set IUT.PaperIV.Valuations F used for arithmetic divisors in this project, in multiplicative and in additive (logarithmic) form.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • The normalized absolute value ‖x‖_v at a nonarchimedean v is Mathlib's NumberField.FinitePlace.mk v x, which equals q_v ^ (- ord_v x) with q_v the cardinality of the residue field (see the module docstring of the Mathlib file above).
  • At an archimedean v it is |x|_v ^ m_v, with m_v = 1 for a real place and m_v = 2 for a complex place (NumberField.InfinitePlace.mult). The papers do not fix this normalization on the pages read; it is the one for which the product formula holds with all multiplicities one. Note that the degree map of IUT IV Definition 1.9 (i) gives weight 1 to every archimedean valuation, real or complex; no relation between the two conventions is asserted here.
Public declarations
variable {F : Type*} [Field F] [NumberField F]

IUT.Prerequisites.finprod_sum_typetheorem

theorem finprod_sum_type {α β M : Type*} [CommMonoid M] (f : α ⊕ β → M)
  (hf : (Function.mulSupport f).Finite) :
  ∏ᶠ i, f i = (∏ᶠ a, f (.inl a)) * ∏ᶠ b, f (.inr b)

A finite product over a sum type splits into the two partial products.

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbsdef

noncomputable def normAbs : Valuations F → F → ℝ
  | .inl v, x => FinitePlace.mk v x
  | .inr w, x => w x ^ w.mult

The normalized absolute value ‖x‖_v at a valuation v ∈ 𝕍(F): Mathlib's finite place absolute value (q_v ^ (- ord_v x)) at a nonarchimedean v, and |x|_v ^ m_v at an archimedean v, where m_v ∈ {1, 2} is 1 for a real place and 2 for a complex place.

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbs_nonnegtheorem

theorem normAbs_nonneg (v : Valuations F) (x : F) : 0 ≤ normAbs v x

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbs_postheorem

theorem normAbs_pos (v : Valuations F) {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) : 0 < normAbs v x

IUT.Prerequisites.normAbs_mulSupport_finitetheorem

theorem normAbs_mulSupport_finite {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) :
  (Function.mulSupport fun v : Valuations F => normAbs v x).Finite

For x ≠ 0, ‖x‖_v = 1 for all but finitely many v ∈ 𝕍(F).

IUT.Prerequisites.prod_normAbs_eq_onetheorem

theorem prod_normAbs_eq_one {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) : ∏ᶠ v : Valuations F, normAbs v x = 1

**Product formula over 𝕍(F)**: for x ∈ F nonzero, ∏_{v ∈ 𝕍(F)} ‖x‖_v = 1.

IUT.Prerequisites.sum_log_normAbs_eq_zerotheorem

theorem sum_log_normAbs_eq_zero {x : F} (hx : x ≠ 0) :
  ∑ᶠ v : Valuations F, Real.log (normAbs v x) = 0

**Product formula over 𝕍(F), additive form**: for x ∈ F nonzero, ∑_{v ∈ 𝕍(F)} log ‖x‖_v = 0.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction

IUT/Prerequisites/ThetaFunction.lean

The classical theta series on a Tate curve and its values at the points ±√−1 · q_X^{j/2}

Module documentation

Sources. Pages read for this file; every formula used was checked against a rendered image:

  • Mochizuki, The Étale Theta Function and its Frobenioid-theoretic Manifestations, Section 1, pages 18 to 21: the coordinates U and Ü (pages 19 and 20), Proposition 1.4 with its proof (pages 20 and 21).
  • Mochizuki, Comments on "The Étale Theta Function and its Frobenioid-theoretic Manifestations" (March 2022): all items read; pages 3, 4 and 6, which hold the items on Section 1, checked against rendered images. None corrects the series or the functional equations of Proposition 1.4. Item (xvii) replaces "∈" by "⊆" for the sets of classes in Proposition 1.4 (iii), and item (xxx) adds references for the classical theory of theta functions on Tate curves; neither concerns a statement formalized here.
  • Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory II, Remark 2.5.1, pages 72 and 73, and page 24 (part (iv) of Remark 1.1.1, which runs over pages 21 to 25; only page 24 was read), all rendered.
The series

Proposition 1.4 (page 20) defines Θ̈(Ü) = q_X^{−1/8} · ∑_{n ∈ ℤ} (−1)^n · q_X^{(1/2)(n + 1/2)²} · Ü^{2n+1}. Since (1/2)(n + 1/2)² − 1/8 = n(n + 1)/2 is an integer, no fractional power of q_X occurs: Θ̈(Ü) = ∑_{n ∈ ℤ} (−1)^n · q_X^{n(n+1)/2} · Ü^{2n+1}. The file fixes an element r with r² = q_X (the q_X^{1/2} of the quasi-periodicity) and defines thetaSeries r u = ∑_{n ∈ ℤ} (−1)^n · r^{n(n+1)} · u^{2n+1}; zpow_mul_succ_eq_sq_zpow records r^{n(n+1)} = (r²)^{n(n+1)/2}, so thetaSeries r u is exactly Θ̈(u) for q_X = r², evaluated at a point u of the field K rather than regarded as a function on the formal scheme.

Setting: K a complete normed field and 0 < ‖r‖ < 1. Summability (summable_thetaTerm, for every u ≠ 0) uses the ratio test and does not need the ultrametric inequality; only the nonvanishing of Θ(√−1) uses IsUltrametricDist K. The functional equations are identities of tsum that hold for all arguments; they carry content where the series converges.

What is proved
  1. Proposition 1.4 (ii): Θ(u) = −Θ(u⁻¹) (thetaSeries_inv), Θ(−u) = −Θ(u) (thetaSeries_neg), and Θ(r^a u) = (−1)^a · r^{−a²} · u^{−2a} · Θ(u) for a ∈ ℤ, u ≠ 0 (thetaSeries_zpow_mul), i.e. Θ̈(q_X^{a/2} Ü) = (−1)^a q_X^{−a²/2} Ü^{−2a} Θ̈(Ü).
  2. The zeros Θ(±1) = 0 (stated in the proof, page 20) in every characteristic, and hence Θ(±r^a) = 0 for all a ∈ ℤ (thetaSeries_zpow). That these are all the zeros, that they are simple, and the divisor of poles (Proposition 1.4 (i)) are not formalized.
  3. The values used in IUT II, Remark 2.5.1 (ii), page 73: at c with c² = −1, Θ(r^j · c) = r^{−j²} · Θ(c) (thetaSeries_zpow_mul_of_sq_eq_neg_one), i.e. Θ̈(±√−1 · q_X^{j/2}) = q_X^{−j²/2} · Θ̈(±√−1); the sign (−1)^j cancels against c^{−2j} = (−1)^j. In a complete ultrametric field, Θ(c) = 2c · ∑_{n ≥ 0} r^{n(n+1)} and ‖Θ(c)‖ = ‖2‖, so Θ(c) ≠ 0 when 2 ≠ 0 in K, and the ratio Θ(r^j c)/Θ(c) equals r^{−j²} (thetaSeries_div_of_sq_eq_neg_one). Normalizing by Θ(c) is a choice of this file: the pages read state no normalization of Θ̈ at √−1.
  4. Relation to IUT.PaperII.Labels. That module attaches to j ∈ {0, 1, …, l^⋇} the exponent j² of qq_v, a chosen 2l-th root of the Tate parameter (as recorded in that module, from IUT I, Example 3.2 (iv); that page was not read for this file). With qq an element such that qq^l = r (so qq^{2l} = q_X), (qq^{j²})^l · Θ(r^j c) = Θ(c) (pow_mul_thetaSeries_zpow_mul): the theta value at ±√−1 · q_X^{j/2}, relative to Θ(c), is the reciprocal of the l-th power of qq^{j²}, as Remark 2.5.1 (ii) says. Moreover, for x in K, x^{2l} · R_j² = 1 with R_j = Θ(r^j c)/Θ(c) if and only if x lies in the μ_{2l}(K)-orbit of qq^{j²} (pow_mul_thetaRatio_sq_eq_one_iff). Interpretation: the identification of q_X with the Tate parameter q_v is read off from Remark 2.5.1 (ii) and is not proved. On page 24 of IUT II (Remark 1.1.1 (iv)), the running text speaks of a Kummer class of a product of integral powers of (Ü)² and q_X^{l/2} (exponent l/2 as printed, rendered at 300 dpi), citing Proposition 1.4 (ii); that passage plays no role here.
What is not formalized

The étale theta function itself (the Kummer classes of Propositions 1.3 and 1.4 (iii)), the tempered coverings Ÿ and their cusps, the evaluation points on the tempered covering, the group-theoretic evaluation of IUT II, Corollary 2.5, the cyclotomic rigidity and Kummer map of Remark 2.5.1 (i), and hence the statement that θ^j(Π_v) is the μ_{2l}-orbit of qq_v^{j²}: only the classical identity behind it is proved. The property in Remark 2.5.1 (ii) that Θ̈(c)/Θ̈(c′) is a root of unity when c′ arises from c by an automorphism of Π_v with c′/c a unit, and Remark 2.5.1 (iii), are not treated. The orbit criterion of item 4 is a reformulation made in this file, not a statement of the paper.

Instance

Over ℚ_[p] with r = p (p is not a square in ℚ_[p], so q_X = p²): summability, the zeros Θ(±p^a) = 0, and ‖Θ(p^j c)/Θ(c)‖ = p^{j²} for p odd and c² = −1; such a c exists in ℚ_[5] (Hensel's lemma, exists_sq_eq_neg_one_padic_five).

variable {K : Type*} [NormedField K] [CompleteSpace K] [IsUltrametricDist K]
variable {p : ℕ} [Fact p.Prime]

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaTermdef

def thetaTerm (r u : K) (n : ℤ) : K := (-1) ^ n * r ^ (n * (n + 1)) * u ^ (2 * n + 1)

The n-th term (−1)^n · r^{n(n+1)} · u^{2n+1} of the theta series, with r in the role of q_X^{1/2}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeriesdef

noncomputable def thetaSeries (r u : K) : K := ∑' n : ℤ, thetaTerm r u n

The theta series Θ(u) = ∑_{n ∈ ℤ} (−1)^n · r^{n(n+1)} · u^{2n+1}. With r² = q_X and u = Ü this is the series Θ̈(Ü) of Proposition 1.4 of the paper on the étale theta function (see the module docstring for the computation of the exponents).

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.zpow_mul_succ_eq_sq_zpowtheorem

theorem zpow_mul_succ_eq_sq_zpow (r : K) (n : ℤ) :
  r ^ (n * (n + 1)) = (r ^ 2) ^ (n * (n + 1) / 2)

The exponent of r is even, so each term depends on r only through q_X = r²: r^{n(n+1)} = (r²)^{n(n+1)/2}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaTerm_neg_succtheorem

theorem thetaTerm_neg_succ (r u : K) (n : ℤ) :
  thetaTerm r u (-(n + 1)) = -thetaTerm r u⁻¹ n

Reflection of the index n ↦ −(n + 1) relates the terms at u and at u⁻¹.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.negSuccEquivdef

def negSuccEquiv : ℤ ≃ ℤ where
  toFun n := -(n + 1)
  invFun n := -(n + 1)
  left_inv n := by simp only; omega
  right_inv n := by simp only; omega

The involution n ↦ −(n + 1) of ℤ.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_invtheorem

theorem thetaSeries_inv (r u : K) : thetaSeries r u = -thetaSeries r u⁻¹

Proposition 1.4 (ii), first formula: Θ(u) = −Θ(u⁻¹).

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_negtheorem

theorem thetaSeries_neg (r u : K) : thetaSeries r (-u) = -thetaSeries r u

Proposition 1.4 (ii), second formula: Θ(−u) = −Θ(u).

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaTerm_zpow_multheorem

theorem thetaTerm_zpow_mul {r u : K} (hr : r ≠ 0) (hu : u ≠ 0) (a n : ℤ) :
  thetaTerm r (r ^ a * u) n =
    (-1) ^ a * r ^ (-a ^ 2) * u ^ (-2 * a) * thetaTerm r u (n + a)

The term identity behind the quasi-periodicity: term(r^a u, n) = (−1)^a · r^{−a²} · u^{−2a} · term(u, n + a).

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_zpow_multheorem

theorem thetaSeries_zpow_mul {r u : K} (hr : r ≠ 0) (hu : u ≠ 0) (a : ℤ) :
  thetaSeries r (r ^ a * u) = (-1) ^ a * r ^ (-a ^ 2) * u ^ (-2 * a) * thetaSeries r u

Proposition 1.4 (ii), third formula: for a ∈ ℤ, Θ(r^a · u) = (−1)^a · r^{−a²} · u^{−2a} · Θ(u), i.e. Θ̈(q_X^{a/2} Ü) = (−1)^a · q_X^{−a²/2} · Ü^{−2a} · Θ̈(Ü) with r = q_X^{1/2}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.norm_thetaTermtheorem

theorem norm_thetaTerm (r u : K) (n : ℤ) :
  ‖thetaTerm r u n‖ = ‖r‖ ^ (n * (n + 1)) * ‖u‖ ^ (2 * n + 1)

‖term(n)‖ = ‖r‖^{n(n+1)} · ‖u‖^{2n+1}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.summable_thetaTerm_nattheorem

theorem summable_thetaTerm_nat {r u : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1) (hu : u ≠ 0) :
  Summable fun n : ℕ => thetaTerm r u n

The terms with index n ≥ 0 are summable when 0 < ‖r‖ < 1 and u ≠ 0 (ratio test).

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.summable_thetaTermtheorem

theorem summable_thetaTerm {r u : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1) (hu : u ≠ 0) :
  Summable (thetaTerm r u)

Summability of the theta series on ℤ for every u ≠ 0 when 0 < ‖r‖ < 1.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_onetheorem

theorem thetaSeries_one {r : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1) : thetaSeries r 1 = 0

Θ(1) = 0 (stated in the proof of Proposition 1.4), in every characteristic: the terms of index n and −(n + 1) cancel.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_neg_onetheorem

theorem thetaSeries_neg_one {r : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1) : thetaSeries r (-1) = 0

Θ(−1) = 0.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_zpowtheorem

theorem thetaSeries_zpow {r : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1) (a : ℤ) :
  thetaSeries r (r ^ a) = 0 ∧ thetaSeries r (-r ^ a) = 0

The zeros Θ(±r^a) = 0, a ∈ ℤ, i.e. Θ̈(±q_X^{a/2}) = 0: obtained from Θ(±1) = 0 and the quasi-periodicity.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaTerm_of_sq_eq_neg_onetheorem

theorem thetaTerm_of_sq_eq_neg_one {r c : K} (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) (n : ℤ) :
  thetaTerm r c n = c * r ^ (n * (n + 1))

At a point c with c² = −1 the signs cancel: term(c, n) = c · r^{n(n+1)}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_zpow_mul_of_sq_eq_neg_onetheorem

theorem thetaSeries_zpow_mul_of_sq_eq_neg_one {r c : K} (hr : r ≠ 0) (hc : c ^ 2 = -1)
  (j : ℤ) : thetaSeries r (r ^ j * c) = r ^ (-j ^ 2) * thetaSeries r c

The theta values at the points r^j · c, c² = −1, separated by periods from c: Θ(r^j · c) = r^{−j²} · Θ(c), i.e. Θ̈(±√−1 · q_X^{j/2}) = q_X^{−j²/2} · Θ̈(±√−1). The sign (−1)^j and the factor c^{−2j} = (−1)^j of the quasi-periodicity cancel.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.pow_mul_thetaSeries_zpow_multheorem

theorem pow_mul_thetaSeries_zpow_mul {r c qq : K} (hr : r ≠ 0) (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) {l : ℕ}
  (hqq : qq ^ l = r) (j : ℤ) :
  (qq ^ (j ^ 2)) ^ l * thetaSeries r (r ^ j * c) = thetaSeries r c

The same identity in terms of a chosen l-th root qq of r, so that qq^{2l} = r² = q_X (the root written as q with a double underline in IUT): (qq^{j²})^l · Θ(r^j · c) = Θ(c). That is, Θ(r^j · c) is Θ(c) times the reciprocal of the l-th power of qq^{j²}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_of_sq_eq_neg_onetheorem

theorem thetaSeries_of_sq_eq_neg_one {r c : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1) (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) :
  thetaSeries r c = 2 * c * ∑' n : ℕ, r ^ (n * (n + 1))

At c with c² = −1, pairing the indices n and −(n + 1): Θ(c) = 2c · ∑_{n ≥ 0} r^{n(n+1)}.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.norm_thetaSeries_of_sq_eq_neg_onetheorem

theorem norm_thetaSeries_of_sq_eq_neg_one {r c : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1)
  (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) : ‖thetaSeries r c‖ = ‖(2 : K)‖

‖Θ(c)‖ = ‖2‖ for c² = −1, in a complete ultrametric field with 0 < ‖r‖ < 1.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_ne_zero_of_sq_eq_neg_onetheorem

theorem thetaSeries_ne_zero_of_sq_eq_neg_one {r c : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1)
  (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) (h2 : (2 : K) ≠ 0) : thetaSeries r c ≠ 0

Θ(c) ≠ 0 for c² = −1 when moreover 2 ≠ 0 in K.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.thetaSeries_div_of_sq_eq_neg_onetheorem

theorem thetaSeries_div_of_sq_eq_neg_one {r c : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1)
  (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) (h2 : (2 : K) ≠ 0) (j : ℤ) :
  thetaSeries r (r ^ j * c) / thetaSeries r c = r ^ (-j ^ 2)

The ratio form: Θ(r^j · c)/Θ(c) = r^{−j²}, with the denominator nonzero.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.pow_mul_thetaRatio_sq_eq_one_ifftheorem

theorem pow_mul_thetaRatio_sq_eq_one_iff {r c qq : K} (hr0 : r ≠ 0) (hr : ‖r‖ < 1)
  (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) (h2 : (2 : K) ≠ 0) {l : ℕ} (hqq : qq ^ l = r) (j : ℤ) (x : K) :
  x ^ (2 * l) * (thetaSeries r (r ^ j * c) / thetaSeries r c) ^ 2 = 1 ↔
    ∃ ζ : K, ζ ^ (2 * l) = 1 ∧ x = ζ * qq ^ (j ^ 2)

Recovering the μ_{2l}-orbit of qq^{j²} from the ratio R_j = Θ(r^j · c)/Θ(c): for x in K, x^{2l} · R_j² = 1 (that is, x^{−l} = ±R_j for x ≠ 0) holds if and only if x = ζ · qq^{j²} for some ζ ∈ K with ζ^{2l} = 1. Here qq^l = r, so qq^{2l} = q_X.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.padic_summable_thetaTermtheorem

theorem padic_summable_thetaTerm {u : ℚ_[p]} (hu : u ≠ 0) :
  Summable (thetaTerm (p : ℚ_[p]) u)

The theta series over ℚ_[p] with r = p converges at every u ≠ 0.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.padic_thetaSeries_zpowtheorem

theorem padic_thetaSeries_zpow (a : ℤ) :
  thetaSeries (p : ℚ_[p]) ((p : ℚ_[p]) ^ a) = 0 ∧ thetaSeries (p : ℚ_[p]) (-(p : ℚ_[p]) ^ a) = 0

The zeros Θ(±p^a) = 0 over ℚ_[p].

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.padic_norm_thetaRatiotheorem

theorem padic_norm_thetaRatio {c : ℚ_[p]} (hc : c ^ 2 = -1) (j : ℤ) :
  ‖thetaSeries (p : ℚ_[p]) ((p : ℚ_[p]) ^ j * c) / thetaSeries (p : ℚ_[p]) c‖ =
    (p : ℝ) ^ (j ^ 2)

The norm of the ratio Θ(p^j · c)/Θ(c) = p^{−j²} over ℚ_[p] is p^{j²}, for any c with c² = −1.

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.exists_sq_eq_neg_one_padic_fivetheorem

theorem exists_sq_eq_neg_one_padic_five (hp : p = 5) : ∃ c : ℚ_[p], c ^ 2 = -1

Nonvacuity of the hypothesis c² = −1: ℚ_[5] contains a square root of −1 (Hensel's lemma applied to X² + 1 at 2).

IUT.Prerequisites.ThetaFunction.padic_five_norm_thetaRatiotheorem

theorem padic_five_norm_thetaRatio (hp : p = 5) :
  ∃ c : ℚ_[p], c ^ 2 = -1 ∧ ∀ j : ℤ,
    ‖thetaSeries (p : ℚ_[p]) ((p : ℚ_[p]) ^ j * c) / thetaSeries (p : ℚ_[p]) c‖ =
      (p : ℝ) ^ (j ^ 2)

An unconditional value over ℚ_[5] with r = 5: there is c with c² = −1, and for it ‖Θ(5^j · c)/Θ(c)‖ = 5^{j²} for every j ∈ ℤ.

IUT I

Construction of Hodge theaters.

IUT.PaperI.InitialThetaData

IUT/PaperI/InitialThetaData.lean

IUT I, Definition 3.1: the conditions on initial Θ-data that Mathlib can state today

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Definition 3.1, pages 61 to 63, with Remarks 3.1.1 to 3.1.7 (pages 63 to 69, text only) for context. Pages 61, 62, 63 were read as rendered images; every condition transcribed below was checked against those images.

This file is not a formalization of Definition 3.1. Initial Θ-data is a tuple (F̄/F, X_F, l, C_K, V, V^bad_mod, ε) subject to conditions (a) to (f). Most of the objects involved (hyperbolic orbicurves of type (1, l-tors)±, K-cores, étale fundamental groups of orbicurves, the field of moduli of X_F in the sense of [AbsTopIII], Definition 5.1, (ii), the q-parameters of a Tate curve, cusps arising from the quotient Q of [EtTh], Definition 2.1) have no counterpart in Mathlib. This file states, as separate named predicates on an actual number field F and an actual Weierstrass curve W over F, exactly those clauses whose every term exists in Mathlib with its intended meaning, and proves a few small facts.

What is stated here
  • SqrtNegOneMem F: the clause √−1 ∈ F of condition (a).
  • AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause l: only the first clause "l is a prime number ≥ 5" of condition (c), not the whole condition.
  • TorsionRational W n: every n-torsion point of E_F over an algebraic closure is F-rational. With n = 6 this is the clause "the 2 · 3-torsion points of E_F are rational over F" of condition (b).
  • SemistableAtFinitePlaces W: the clause "X_F admits stable reduction over all v ∈ 𝕍(F)^non" of condition (b), read as described below.
  • Relative to a number field k with F an algebra over k, standing in for F_mod: BadSetOddNonempty, MultiplicativeOverBadSet, GaloisOfDegreePrimeTo, PrimeToBadResidueChars. These state the clauses of (b) and (c) that mention F_mod and 𝕍^bad_mod, for an arbitrary such k; the requirement that k be the field of moduli of X_F is not stated, because the field of moduli is not defined here.
  • PartialInitialThetaConditions: the conjunction of the predicates above. It is a partial collection, not Definition 3.1; its docstring lists what is missing.
Interpretation choices, recorded
  • F̄ is taken to be Mathlib's AlgebraicClosure F. The rationality of torsion points does not depend on the choice of algebraic closure; that independence is not proved here.
  • E_F is represented by a Weierstrass curve W with W.IsElliptic. Points of E_F over a field L are (W⁄L).toAffine.Point (the point at infinity together with the affine nonsingular points), with Mathlib's group law.
  • Stable reduction of the once-punctured elliptic curve X_F at a finite place is read as: a minimal Weierstrass model of E_F over the valuation ring of F at that place has good or multiplicative reduction. This identification of "stable reduction of X_F" with "semistable reduction of E_F" is the standard one but is not argued on the pages read; it is a reading of page 61, not a transcription. Mathlib's WeierstrassCurve.minimal is a chosen minimal model; the reduction type does not depend on the choice, but that is not proved here.
  • "Bad (i.e., multiplicative) reduction" at the places over 𝕍^bad_mod is page 61's own gloss and is stated with HasMultiplicativeReduction.
  • "Odd residue characteristic" at a prime w of 𝓞 k is stated as (2 : 𝓞 k) ∉ w, and "l prime to the residue characteristic of w" as (l : 𝓞 k) ∉ w.
  • A place of F lies over a place w of k when the prime ideals satisfy v ∩ 𝓞 k = w.
Not statable today (see docs/INITIAL_THETA_DATA.md)

The field of moduli F_mod of X_F; the image of G_F → GL₂(𝔽_l) containing SL₂(𝔽_l) and the field K it cuts out (Mathlib has the group law on E(F̄) and the Galois action can be written with WeierstrassCurve.Affine.Point.map, but the structure theorem E[l](F̄) ≅ (ℤ/lℤ)² that gives the representation its meaning is not in Mathlib); the orders of the q-parameters at the places of 𝕍(F)^bad; the orbicurves C_F, C_K, X_K and their types; K-cores; the étale and tempered fundamental groups Π, Δ; the section V ⊆ 𝕍(K) (statable once K is constructed, omitted here); the cusp ε and the condition at v ∈ V^bad that C_v be of type (1, ℤ/lℤ)±.

No claim of the form l ≠ 5 is made: it would need the mod l Galois representation.

Public declarations
variable {F : Type*} [Field F] [NumberField F] (W : WeierstrassCurve F) [W.IsElliptic]

IUT.PaperI.SqrtNegOneMemdef

def SqrtNegOneMem (F : Type*) [Field F] : Prop :=
  ∃ x : F, x ^ 2 = -1

Definition 3.1 (a), first clause, page 61: the number field F contains a square root of −1.

IUT.PaperI.not_sqrtNegOneMem_rattheorem

theorem not_sqrtNegOneMem_rat : ¬ SqrtNegOneMem ℚ

The clause of condition (a) is not automatic: it fails for ℚ.

IUT.PaperI.AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClausedef

def AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause (l : ℕ) : Prop :=
  l.Prime ∧ 5 ≤ l

Definition 3.1 (c), first clause only, page 62: l is a prime number with l ≥ 5. Condition (c) also requires that the image of G_F → GL₂(𝔽_l) contain SL₂(𝔽_l) and that l be prime to the residue characteristics of 𝕍^bad_mod and to the orders of the q-parameters at the places of 𝕍(F)^bad; this predicate does not state those clauses.

IUT.PaperI.auxiliaryPrimeFirstClause_fivetheorem

theorem auxiliaryPrimeFirstClause_five : AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause 5

The clause of condition (c) is satisfiable: 5 is a prime ≥ 5.

IUT.PaperI.AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause.coprime_sixtheorem

theorem AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause.coprime_six {l : ℕ} (h : AuxiliaryPrimeFirstClause l) : Nat.Coprime l 6

A prime l ≥ 5 is prime to 6, as the hypotheses of §1 (page 37) require of l.

IUT.PaperI.TorsionRationaldef

def TorsionRational (n : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ P : (W⁄(AlgebraicClosure F)).toAffine.Point, n • P = 0 →
    P ∈ (Affine.Point.baseChange (W' := W) F (AlgebraicClosure F)).range

Every n-torsion point of E_F over F̄ (here AlgebraicClosure F) is F-rational, that is, lies in the image of the base change map E(F) → E(F̄). With n = 6 this is the clause of Definition 3.1 (b), page 62: "the 2 · 3-torsion points of E_F are rational over F".

IUT.PaperI.TorsionRational.of_dvdtheorem

theorem TorsionRational.of_dvd {m n : ℕ} (hmn : m ∣ n) (h : TorsionRational W n) :
  TorsionRational W m

Rationality of the n-torsion implies rationality of the m-torsion for m ∣ n.

IUT.PaperI.TorsionRational.two_of_sixtheorem

theorem TorsionRational.two_of_six (h : TorsionRational W 6) : TorsionRational W 2

The 2-torsion part, used on page 63 (Definition 3.1 (e)) and page 71 (Example 3.2 (iv)): rationality of the 6-torsion gives rationality of the 2-torsion.

IUT.PaperI.SemistableAtFinitePlacesdef

def SemistableAtFinitePlaces : Prop :=
  ∀ v : HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F),
    (W.minimal (v.valuation F).valuationSubring).HasGoodReduction
        (v.valuation F).valuationSubring ∨
      (W.minimal (v.valuation F).valuationSubring).HasMultiplicativeReduction
        (v.valuation F).valuationSubring

Definition 3.1 (b), page 61: X_F admits stable reduction at every nonarchimedean valuation of F, read as: at every nonzero prime v of 𝓞 F, a minimal model of E_F over the valuation ring of v has good or multiplicative reduction. See the module docstring for this interpretation.

IUT.PaperI.BadSetOddNonemptydef

def BadSetOddNonempty (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) : Prop :=
  S.Nonempty ∧ ∀ w ∈ S, (2 : 𝓞 k) ∉ w.asIdeal

Definition 3.1 (b), page 61, relative to a number field k standing in for F_mod: S (standing in for 𝕍^bad_mod) is a nonempty set of nonarchimedean valuations of k of odd residue characteristic.

IUT.PaperI.MultiplicativeOverBadSetdef

def MultiplicativeOverBadSet (W : WeierstrassCurve F) (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) :
    Prop :=
  ∀ v : HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F), ∀ w ∈ S,
    v.asIdeal.comap (algebraMap (𝓞 k) (𝓞 F)) = w.asIdeal →
      (W.minimal (v.valuation F).valuationSubring).HasMultiplicativeReduction
        (v.valuation F).valuationSubring

Definition 3.1 (b), page 61, relative to k: E_F has bad, that is multiplicative, reduction at every nonarchimedean valuation of F lying over an element of S.

IUT.PaperI.GaloisOfDegreePrimeTodef

def GaloisOfDegreePrimeTo (l : ℕ) : Prop :=
  IsGalois k F ∧ Nat.Coprime (Module.finrank k F) l

Definition 3.1 (b), pages 61 and 62, relative to k: the extension F/k is Galois of degree prime to l.

IUT.PaperI.PrimeToBadResidueCharsdef

def PrimeToBadResidueChars (l : ℕ) (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) : Prop :=
  ∀ w ∈ S, (l : 𝓞 k) ∉ w.asIdeal

Definition 3.1 (c), page 62, relative to k: l is prime to the residue characteristics of the elements of S.

IUT.PaperI.PartialInitialThetaConditionsstructure

structure PartialInitialThetaConditions (W : WeierstrassCurve F) [W.IsElliptic] (l : ℕ)
  (S : Set (HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 k))) : Prop where

A partial collection of the conditions of Definition 3.1 (pages 61 to 63): the clauses that are statable with Mathlib today, for a number field F, an elliptic curve W standing for E_F, a natural number l, a number field k with F a k-algebra standing in for F_mod, and a set S standing in for 𝕍^bad_mod. This is not initial Θ-data. Missing, relative to Definition 3.1: * (a) nothing beyond the choice F̄ = AlgebraicClosure F; * (b) that X_F is the once-punctured curve E_F \ {0} (implicit in using W), the orbicurve C_F, the requirement that k be the field of moduli F_mod of X_F, the field F_sol, and the fundamental groups Π_{X_F} ⊆ Π_{C_F}, Δ_X ⊆ Δ_C; * (c) that the image of G_F → GL₂(𝔽_l) contain SL₂(𝔽_l), the field K, and that l be prime to the orders of the q-parameters of E_F at the places of 𝕍(F)^bad; * (d) the orbicurve C_K of type (1, l-tors)± with K-core C_F ×_F K, and X_K; * (e) the section V ⊆ 𝕍(K) of 𝕍(K) → 𝕍_mod and the type (1, ℤ/lℤ)± condition at v ∈ V^bad; * (f) the cusp ε and its normalization at v ∈ V^bad. Open obligation: no inhabitant is exhibited. This file does not construct any (F, W, l, k, S) satisfying these conditions, so it does not show that the conjunction is consistent. A witness needs at least: a number field F containing a primitive fourth root of unity and the whole 6-torsion of E_F (hence, by the Weil pairing, also a primitive cube root of unity, so F ⊇ ℚ(ζ₁₂); for example F = k(E[6], √−1) for a curve over a number field k); semistable reduction of E_F at every finite place, checked on minimal models; a nonempty set S of odd places of k at which E_F has multiplicative reduction above S; F/k Galois of degree prime to l; and a prime l ≥ 5 prime to the residue characteristics in S. None of these has been verified for a specific curve.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphism

IUT/PaperI/PolyMorphism.lean

IUT I, Section 0: poly-morphisms and capsules

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Section 0 ("Notations and Conventions"), subsection "Monoids and Categories", pages 33 and 34. Both pages were read as rendered images for this file; every definition below was checked against those images.

These are the unnumbered definitions of poly-morphisms and capsules that the later sections of IUT I to IV use throughout (for example, the radial data of IUT II, Example 1.8 (i), page 35, read as a rendered image, include a full poly-isomorphism of topological groups Π/Δ ⥲ G). They are stated for an arbitrary category C of Mathlib. The definitions of page 33 (poly-morphisms, poly-isomorphisms, poly-automorphisms, full poly-isomorphisms, composites) are stated in full. Of page 34 the file supplies only capsules, morphisms of capsules with their identities and composition, and the set of capsule morphisms attached to a fixed injection of index sets. Open, and not supplied here:

  • the category laws for morphisms of capsules (identity and associativity), hence the category Capsule(C) of page 34 itself;
  • capsule-full poly-morphisms as poly-morphisms in Capsule(C), which needs that category;
  • capsule-full poly-isomorphisms as a notion: IsCapsuleFullPolyIso tests only that the chosen injection of index sets is bijective.
Definitions of page 33, stated in full
  • A poly-morphism A → B is a collection of morphisms A → B, that is, a subset of the set of morphisms A → B (PolyMorphism). The page says "a subset"; the empty subset is allowed and no nonemptiness is imposed.
  • It is a poly-isomorphism if all of its members are isomorphisms (IsPolyIso), and a poly-automorphism if moreover A = B (IsPolyAut).
  • The full poly-isomorphism A ⥲ B is the poly-morphism consisting of all isomorphisms A ⥲ B (fullPolyIso).
  • The composite of {fᵢ : A → B}_{i ∈ I} with {g_j : B → C}_{j ∈ J} is the set {g_j ∘ fᵢ}_{(i,j) ∈ I × J}, multiplicities ignored (PolyMorphism.comp). Mathlib writes composition in diagrammatic order, so g_j ∘ fᵢ is fᵢ ≫ g_j. Using sets realizes "the set [where multiplicities are ignored]".
Definitions of pages 33 and 34
  • A capsule of objects of C is a finite collection {A_j}_{j ∈ J} of objects indexed by a finite index set J; it is a |J|-capsule, and π₀({A_j}) = J (Capsule). The index set is a type with a Fintype instance.
  • A morphism of capsules {A_j}_{j ∈ J} → {A'_{j'}}_{j' ∈ J'} is an injection ι : J ↪ J' together with a morphism A_j → A'_{ι(j)} for each j (Capsule.Hom). Composition and identities are given (Capsule.Hom.comp, Capsule.Hom.id). Not done here: the page states that capsules form a category Capsule(C); the category laws are not proved in this file, so no Category instance is given.
  • A capsule-full poly-morphism associated to a fixed injection ι : J ↪ J' is the set of capsule morphisms with that injection whose components are isomorphisms A_j ⥲ A'_{ι(j)} (capsuleFullPolyMorphism); it is a capsule-full poly-isomorphism when ι is a bijection. IsCapsuleFullPolyIso states only this bijectivity condition on ι, not the whole notion.

Nothing in this file is a theorem of the papers; the lemmas only record elementary properties of the definitions.

Public declarations
variable {C : Type u} [Category.{v} C]

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphismabbrev

abbrev PolyMorphism (A B : C) : Type v := Set (A ⟶ B)

IUT I, page 33: a poly-morphism A → B is a collection of morphisms A → B, that is, a subset of the set of morphisms A → B.

IUT.PaperI.IsPolyIsodef

def IsPolyIso {A B : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) : Prop :=
  ∀ f ∈ S, IsIso f

IUT I, page 33: a poly-morphism is a poly-isomorphism if all of the morphisms in the collection are isomorphisms.

IUT.PaperI.IsPolyAutdef

def IsPolyAut {A : C} (S : PolyMorphism A A) : Prop :=
  IsPolyIso S

IUT I, page 33: a poly-isomorphism A ⥲ A (with A = B) is a poly-automorphism.

IUT.PaperI.fullPolyIsodef

def fullPolyIso (A B : C) : PolyMorphism A B :=
  {f | IsIso f}

IUT I, page 33: the full poly-isomorphism A ⥲ B is the poly-morphism given by the collection of all isomorphisms A ⥲ B.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphism.compdef

def PolyMorphism.comp {A B D : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) (T : PolyMorphism B D) :
    PolyMorphism A D :=
  Set.image2 (fun f g => f ≫ g) S T

IUT I, page 33: the composite of a poly-morphism {fᵢ : A → B} with a poly-morphism {g_j : B → C} is the set {g_j ∘ fᵢ} (multiplicities ignored). In Mathlib's diagrammatic notation g_j ∘ fᵢ is fᵢ ≫ g_j.

IUT.PaperI.isPolyIso_fullPolyIsotheorem

theorem isPolyIso_fullPolyIso (A B : C) : IsPolyIso (fullPolyIso A B)

The full poly-isomorphism is a poly-isomorphism.

IUT.PaperI.isPolyIso_iff_subsettheorem

theorem isPolyIso_iff_subset {A B : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) :
  IsPolyIso S ↔ S ⊆ fullPolyIso A B

A poly-morphism is a poly-isomorphism if and only if it is contained in the full poly-isomorphism.

IUT.PaperI.IsPolyIso.comptheorem

theorem IsPolyIso.comp {A B D : C} {S : PolyMorphism A B} {T : PolyMorphism B D}
  (hS : IsPolyIso S) (hT : IsPolyIso T) : IsPolyIso (S.comp T)

The composite of two poly-isomorphisms is a poly-isomorphism.

IUT.PaperI.PolyMorphism.comp_assoctheorem

theorem PolyMorphism.comp_assoc {A B D E : C} (S : PolyMorphism A B) (T : PolyMorphism B D)
  (U : PolyMorphism D E) : (S.comp T).comp U = S.comp (T.comp U)

Composition of poly-morphisms is associative.

IUT.PaperI.Capsulestructure

structure Capsule where

IUT I, pages 33 and 34: a capsule of objects of C is a finite collection {A_j}_{j ∈ J} of objects of C indexed by a finite index set J.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.π₀abbrev

abbrev π₀ (A : Capsule C) : Type := A.index

IUT I, page 34: π₀({A_j}_{j ∈ J}) = J.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.IsNCapsuledef

def IsNCapsule (A : Capsule C) (n : ℕ) : Prop :=
  Fintype.card A.index = n

IUT I, page 34: a capsule with index set J is a |J|-capsule.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Homstructure

structure Hom (A A' : Capsule C) where

IUT I, page 34: a morphism of capsules {A_j}_{j ∈ J} → {A'_{j'}}_{j' ∈ J'} consists of an injection ι : J ↪ J' and, for each j ∈ J, a morphism A_j → A'_{ι(j)}.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Hom.iddef

def Hom.id (A : Capsule C) : Hom A A :=
  ⟨Function.Embedding.refl _, fun j => 𝟙 (A.obj j)⟩

The identity morphism of a capsule.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.Hom.compdef

def Hom.comp {A A' A'' : Capsule C} (φ : Hom A A') (ψ : Hom A' A'') : Hom A A'' :=
  ⟨φ.ι.trans ψ.ι, fun j => φ.app j ≫ ψ.app (φ.ι j)⟩

Composition of morphisms of capsules: compose the injections and the component morphisms.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.capsuleFullPolyMorphismdef

def capsuleFullPolyMorphism (A A' : Capsule C) (ι : A.index ↪ A'.index) : Set (Hom A A') :=
  {φ | φ.ι = ι ∧ ∀ j, IsIso (φ.app j)}

IUT I, page 34: the capsule-full poly-morphism {A_j} → {A'_{j'}} associated to a fixed injection ι : J ↪ J' is the set of capsule morphisms with injection ι whose components A_j → A'_{ι(j)} are isomorphisms.

IUT.PaperI.Capsule.IsCapsuleFullPolyIsodef

def IsCapsuleFullPolyIso {A A' : Capsule C} (ι : A.index ↪ A'.index) : Prop :=
  Function.Bijective ι

IUT I, page 34: a capsule-full poly-morphism is a capsule-full poly-isomorphism when the associated injection between index sets is a bijection. This predicate records only that condition: it tests whether the chosen injection ι is bijective, and it does not construct the capsule-full poly-isomorphism as a poly-morphism in Capsule(C), a category that is not constructed in this file.

IUT.PaperI.Temperoid

IUT/PaperI/Temperoid.lean

IUT I, Section 0: tempered groups and connected temperoids

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, Section 0 ("Notations and Conventions"), subsection "Monoids and Categories", pages 33 to 35, and Remark 2.5.3 (i), (T1), page 52. Pages read for this file: 33, 34, 35 and 52, all as rendered images; every definition below was checked against those images. Also read, as a rendered image: page 4 of Mochizuki's Comments on "Semi-graphs of Anabelioids", item (12), whose (T1) has the same wording as IUT I, Remark 2.5.3 (i), (T1). Not read for this file: [SemiAnbd] itself (Definition 3.1 (i) to (iii), Proposition 3.2) and [FrdI], §0.

Galois-countability and the corrected theory

IUT I, Remark 2.5.3 (i), page 52 (and item (12) of the comments on [SemiAnbd]) notes that pathologies occur in the theory of tempered fundamental groups without countability hypotheses, and introduces in (T1): a tempered group is Galois-countable if its topology admits a countable basis; a connected temperoid is Galois-countable if it arises from a Galois-countable tempered group. The correction does not change the definitions of a tempered group, of B^temp(Π) or of a connected temperoid: it adds Galois-countability as a hypothesis to results (by (E7) of the comments, to [SemiAnbd] 3.2 to 3.9). IUT I, pages 34 and 35, keeps the uncorrected definitions and puts "Galois-countable" only in the reconstruction of Π and in the bijections of page 35, in agreement with the correction. This file therefore formalizes the definitions as stated on page 34 (IsTemperedGroup, TemperedSetCategory, IsConnectedTemperoid, with no countable basis condition) and states Galois-countability separately (IsGaloisCountableTemperedGroup, IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid); no discrepancy between the two sources was found on the pages read.

Definitions of pages 34 and 35, as stated here

The group written Π on the page is named P in the Lean code (Π is a reserved token).

  • Tempered topological group (page 34): a topological group Π is tempered if it may be written as an inverse limit of an inverse system of surjections of countable discrete topological groups (IsTemperedGroup). The inverse system is spelled out: an index preorder I, groups G i that are countable and carry the discrete topology, surjective transition homomorphisms G j →* G i for i ≤ j satisfying the identity and composition laws, and an isomorphism of topological groups from Π onto the subgroup of compatible families of ∀ i, G i with the subspace topology of the product topology (compatibleFamilies). Interpretation: the page states no condition on the index set. We take an arbitrary nonempty directed preorder (the usual meaning of "inverse system"), with no countability condition, in the universe of Π; the groups G i live in the same universe.
  • **B^temp(Π)** (page 34): the category whose objects are countable discrete sets with a continuous Π-action and whose morphisms are morphisms of Π-sets (TemperedSetCategory). It is the full subcategory of Mathlib's category Action (Type u) Π of Π-sets and equivariant maps cut out by IsCountableDiscreteContinuous: the underlying set is countable and the action map Π × X → X is continuous for the discrete topology on X. "Countable" is Mathlib's Countable (cardinality at most that of ℕ), as on the page.
  • Connected temperoid (page 34): a category equivalent to B^temp(Π) for a tempered topological group Π (IsConnectedTemperoid).
  • Galois-countable (page 52, (T1)): a tempered group whose topology has a countable basis, Mathlib's SecondCountableTopology (IsGaloisCountableTemperedGroup); a connected temperoid equivalent to B^temp(Π) for such a Π (IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid). "Arises from" is read as "is equivalent to B^temp of". The notions for temperoids, quasi-temperoids and semi-graphs of anabelioids in (T1) and (T2) are not stated.
  • Morphism of connected temperoids (page 35): a morphism C₁ → C₂ is an isomorphism class of functors C₂ → C₁ that preserve finite limits and countable colimits (TemperoidMorphismClass). "Preserves countable colimits" is read as preserving colimits of every shape J : Type that is a CountableCategory (countably many objects and morphisms), in the same way as Mathlib's PreservesFiniteLimits quantifies over finite shapes in Type. The type is defined for arbitrary categories C₁, C₂ with the same object and morphism universes; the page uses it for connected temperoids.
Lemmas proved here (elementary properties, not results of the papers)
  • Temperedness is invariant under isomorphism of topological groups (isTemperedGroup_of_continuousMulEquiv).
  • A countable discrete group is tempered (isTemperedGroup_of_countable_discrete).
  • A topological group isomorphic to an inverse limit of a surjective inverse system of finite discrete groups is tempered (isTemperedGroup_of_finite_system). This is close to the definition itself, since a finite group is countable; it records that such profinite groups are covered.
  • A tempered group is totally disconnected (totallyDisconnectedSpace_of_isTemperedGroup), so the circle group is not tempered (circle_not_isTemperedGroup): the definition is not satisfied by every topological group.
  • A set with the trivial action is an object of B^temp(Π) when countable (trivialTemperedSet), and B^temp(Π) is a connected temperoid when Π is tempered (temperedSetCategory_isConnectedTemperoid).
  • A countable discrete group is a Galois-countable tempered group (isGaloisCountableTemperedGroup_of_countable_discrete), and a Galois-countable connected temperoid is a connected temperoid (IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid.isConnectedTemperoid).
Not covered
  • B(X), the category of finite étale coverings of a connected noetherian algebraic stack (page 34): algebraic stacks are not in Mathlib.
  • The subcategory C^0 of connected objects, the category (C^0)^⊤, the equivalence C ≃ (C^0)^⊤ (page 34) and morphisms C₁^0 → C₂^0 (page 35): the notions of connected object and of (·)^⊤ are those of [FrdI], §0, which is not defined on the pages read.
  • The reconstruction of Π up to inner automorphism from B^temp(Π) or B^temp(Π)^0 and the groups π₁(C), π₁(C^0) (page 34, citing Remark 2.5.3 (i), (T5)): open; not stated.
  • The natural bijections of page 35 between continuous outer homomorphisms Π₁ → Π₂, morphisms B^temp(Π₁) → B^temp(Π₂) and morphisms B^temp(Π₁)^0 → B^temp(Π₂)^0 for Galois-countable tempered Π₁, Π₂ (citing Remark 2.5.3 (ii), (E7) and [SemiAnbd], Proposition 3.2): open; not stated.
  • That ℤ_[p] (or any specific infinite profinite group) is tempered is not proved here.
Public declarations

IUT.PaperI.compatibleFamiliesdef

def compatibleFamilies {I : Type u} [Preorder I] (G : I → Type u) [∀ i, Group (G i)]
    (f : ∀ {i j : I}, i ≤ j → G j →* G i) : Subgroup (∀ i, G i) where
  carrier := {x | ∀ (i j : I) (h : i ≤ j), f h (x j) = x i}
  mul_mem' {a b} ha hb i j h := by simp [ha i j h, hb i j h]
  one_mem' i j h := by simp
  inv_mem' {a} ha i j h := by simp [ha i j h]

The subgroup of ∀ i, G i of families compatible with the transition maps f: those x with f h (x j) = x i whenever h : i ≤ j. With the subspace topology of the product topology it is the inverse limit of the system f.

IUT.PaperI.IsTemperedGroupdef

def IsTemperedGroup (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P] : Prop :=
  ∃ (I : Type u) (_ : Preorder I) (_ : IsDirected I (· ≤ ·)) (_ : Nonempty I)
    (G : I → Type u) (_ : ∀ i, Group (G i)) (_ : ∀ i, TopologicalSpace (G i))
    (f : ∀ {i j : I}, i ≤ j → G j →* G i),
    (∀ i, DiscreteTopology (G i)) ∧ (∀ i, Countable (G i)) ∧
    (∀ {i j : I} (h : i ≤ j), Function.Surjective (f h)) ∧
    (∀ (i : I) (x : G i), f (le_refl i) x = x) ∧
    (∀ {i j k : I} (hij : i ≤ j) (hjk : j ≤ k) (x : G k),
      f hij (f hjk x) = f (hij.trans hjk) x) ∧
    Nonempty (P ≃ₜ* compatibleFamilies G f)

IUT I, page 34: a topological group Π is tempered if it may be written as an inverse limit of an inverse system of surjections of countable discrete topological groups. The index set is a nonempty directed preorder I with no countability condition (the page states none); I and the groups G i are taken in the universe of Π.

IUT.PaperI.isTemperedGroup_of_continuousMulEquivtheorem

theorem isTemperedGroup_of_continuousMulEquiv {P P' : Type u} [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P]
  [Group P'] [TopologicalSpace P'] (e : P' ≃ₜ* P) (h : IsTemperedGroup P) :
  IsTemperedGroup P'

Temperedness is invariant under isomorphism of topological groups.

IUT.PaperI.isTemperedGroup_of_countable_discretetheorem

theorem isTemperedGroup_of_countable_discrete (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P]
  [DiscreteTopology P] [Countable P] : IsTemperedGroup P

A countable group with the discrete topology is tempered: it is the inverse limit of the inverse system with one index and the identity transition map.

IUT.PaperI.isTemperedGroup_of_finite_systemtheorem

theorem isTemperedGroup_of_finite_system (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P]
  {I : Type u} [Preorder I] [IsDirected I (· ≤ ·)] [Nonempty I]
  (G : I → Type u) [∀ i, Group (G i)] [∀ i, TopologicalSpace (G i)]
  [∀ i, DiscreteTopology (G i)] [∀ i, Finite (G i)]
  (f : ∀ {i j : I}, i ≤ j → G j →* G i)
  (hs : ∀ {i j : I} (h : i ≤ j), Function.Surjective (f h))
  (hid : ∀ (i : I) (x : G i), f (le_refl i) x = x)
  (hcomp : ∀ {i j k : I} (hij : i ≤ j) (hjk : j ≤ k) (x : G k),
    f hij (f hjk x) = f (hij.trans hjk) x)
  (e : P ≃ₜ* compatibleFamilies G f) : IsTemperedGroup P

A topological group isomorphic to the inverse limit of a surjective inverse system of finite discrete groups is tempered. This is close to the definition, since finite groups are countable; it records that such profinite groups satisfy the definition.

IUT.PaperI.totallyDisconnectedSpace_of_isTemperedGrouptheorem

theorem totallyDisconnectedSpace_of_isTemperedGroup (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P]
  (h : IsTemperedGroup P) : TotallyDisconnectedSpace P

A tempered group is totally disconnected: it is homeomorphic to a subspace of a product of discrete spaces.

IUT.PaperI.circle_not_isTemperedGrouptheorem

theorem circle_not_isTemperedGroup : ¬ IsTemperedGroup Circle

The circle group is not tempered: it is connected and nontrivial, while tempered groups are totally disconnected. So IsTemperedGroup is not satisfied by every topological group.

IUT.PaperI.IsCountableDiscreteContinuousdef

def IsCountableDiscreteContinuous {P : Type u} [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P] :
    ObjectProperty (Action (Type u) P) := fun X =>
  Countable X.V ∧
    letI : TopologicalSpace X.V := ⊥
    Continuous (fun p : P × X.V => X.ρ p.1 p.2)

A Π-set X (an object of Mathlib's Action (Type u) Π) is a countable discrete set with a continuous Π-action: its underlying set is countable, and the action map Π × X → X is continuous when X carries the discrete topology.

IUT.PaperI.TemperedSetCategoryabbrev

abbrev TemperedSetCategory (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P] : Type (u + 1) :=
  ObjectProperty.FullSubcategory (IsCountableDiscreteContinuous (P := P))

IUT I, page 34: the category B^temp(Π) whose objects are countable discrete sets with a continuous Π-action and whose morphisms are morphisms of Π-sets. It is the full subcategory of Action (Type u) Π (whose morphisms are the Π-equivariant maps) on the objects satisfying IsCountableDiscreteContinuous; its category structure is Mathlib's full subcategory structure.

IUT.PaperI.trivialTemperedSetdef

def trivialTemperedSet (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P] (S : Type u) [Countable S] :
    TemperedSetCategory P :=
  ⟨Action.trivial P S, ‹Countable S›, by
    let _ : TopologicalSpace S := ⊥
    exact continuous_snd⟩

A countable set with the trivial Π-action is an object of B^temp(Π); in particular the category is not empty.

IUT.PaperI.IsConnectedTemperoiddef

def IsConnectedTemperoid (C : Type v) [Category.{w} C] : Prop :=
  ∃ (P : Type u) (_ : Group P) (_ : TopologicalSpace P) (_ : IsTopologicalGroup P),
    IsTemperedGroup P ∧ Nonempty (C ≌ TemperedSetCategory P)

IUT I, page 34: a category C is a connected temperoid if it is equivalent to a category B^temp(Π) for some tempered topological group Π (in the universe u).

IUT.PaperI.temperedSetCategory_isConnectedTemperoidtheorem

theorem temperedSetCategory_isConnectedTemperoid (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P]
  [IsTopologicalGroup P] (h : IsTemperedGroup P) :
  IsConnectedTemperoid.{u} (TemperedSetCategory P)

B^temp(Π) is a connected temperoid when Π is a tempered topological group.

IUT.PaperI.IsGaloisCountableTemperedGroupdef

def IsGaloisCountableTemperedGroup (P : Type u) [Group P] [TopologicalSpace P] : Prop :=
  IsTemperedGroup P ∧ SecondCountableTopology P

IUT I, page 52, Remark 2.5.3 (i), (T1): a tempered group is Galois-countable if its topology admits a countable basis. Stated as the conjunction of IsTemperedGroup with Mathlib's SecondCountableTopology (existence of a countable basis of the topology).

IUT.PaperI.IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoiddef

def IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid (C : Type v) [Category.{w} C] : Prop :=
  ∃ (P : Type u) (_ : Group P) (_ : TopologicalSpace P) (_ : IsTopologicalGroup P),
    IsGaloisCountableTemperedGroup P ∧ Nonempty (C ≌ TemperedSetCategory P)

IUT I, page 52, Remark 2.5.3 (i), (T1): a connected temperoid is Galois-countable if it arises from a Galois-countable tempered group; "arises from" is read as "is equivalent to B^temp(Π) for".

IUT.PaperI.isGaloisCountableTemperedGroup_of_countable_discretetheorem

theorem isGaloisCountableTemperedGroup_of_countable_discrete (P : Type u) [Group P]
  [TopologicalSpace P] [DiscreteTopology P] [Countable P] :
  IsGaloisCountableTemperedGroup P

A countable discrete group is a Galois-countable tempered group.

IUT.PaperI.IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid.isConnectedTemperoidtheorem

theorem IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid.isConnectedTemperoid {C : Type v} [Category.{w} C]
  (h : IsGaloisCountableConnectedTemperoid.{u} C) : IsConnectedTemperoid.{u} C

A Galois-countable connected temperoid is a connected temperoid.

IUT.PaperI.ExactCountableFunctordef

def ExactCountableFunctor (C₁ : Type v) [Category.{w} C₁] (C₂ : Type v) [Category.{w} C₂] :
    Type (max v w) :=
  {F : C₂ ⥤ C₁ // Limits.PreservesFiniteLimits F ∧
    ∀ (J : Type) [SmallCategory J] [CountableCategory J], Limits.PreservesColimitsOfShape J F}

The functors C₂ ⥤ C₁ that preserve finite limits and countable colimits; "countable colimits" is read as colimits of every shape J : Type that is a CountableCategory.

IUT.PaperI.TemperoidMorphismClassdef

def TemperoidMorphismClass (C₁ : Type v) [Category.{w} C₁] (C₂ : Type v) [Category.{w} C₂] :
    Type (max v w) :=
  Quot (fun F G : ExactCountableFunctor C₁ C₂ => Nonempty (F.1 ≅ G.1))

IUT I, page 35: a morphism C₁ → C₂ of connected temperoids is an isomorphism class of functors C₂ → C₁ that preserve finite limits and countable colimits. Defined here for any two categories C₁, C₂ in the same universes; the page applies it to connected temperoids.

IUT II

Hodge–Arakelov-theoretic evaluation.

IUT.PaperII.Labels

IUT/PaperII/Labels.lean

IUT II: the label sets |𝔽_l|, 𝔽_l^⋇ and the exponents j² of the theta values

Module documentation

Sources, all read for this file, with every formula below checked against a rendered page:

  • Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory I, page 3 (rendered): the definitions l^⋇ = (l − 1)/2 and 𝔽_l^⋇ = 𝔽_l^× / {±1}.
  • Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory II, page 3 (rendered): j ∈ {0, 1, …, l^⋇} is uniquely determined by its image in |𝔽_l| = 𝔽_l / {±1} = {0} ∪ 𝔽_l^⋇.
  • IUT II, Remark 2.5.1 (i), page 72 (rendered): the theta value attached to j ∈ |𝔽_l| is qq_v^{j²} with j ∈ {0, 1, …, l^⋇} the representative of j. Here qq_v (a q with a double underline in the paper) is not the Tate parameter q_v of E_v: by IUT I, Example 3.2 (iv), page 71 (rendered), qq_v = q_v^{1/2l}, a chosen 2l-th root determined up to a μ_{2l}-multiple. Relative to the Tate parameter the theta value is q_v^{j²/(2l)}. The exponents treated in this file are the j² attached to qq_v.
  • IUT II, Proposition 4.1 (iv), page 122, and Remark 4.5.4, page 136 (both rendered): the Gaussian monoid carries the exponent vector (…, j² · log(p_v), …) indexed by j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇, with j read as a natural number in {1, …, l^⋇}.
  • IUT II, Remark 4.7.3 (iii), page 145 (rendered): the volume at each j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ gets weight 1/l^⋇.
  • IUT II, Remark 2.6.3 (ii), pages 79 and 80 (rendered): the quantity ‖Γ′‖ = |Γ′|⁻¹ · ∑_{j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} min_{j ∈ j ∩ Γ′} j² and claim (d) about it.
What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of any theorem about theta functions or Gaussian monoids. It checks elementary facts about the labels and the exponents that these constructions use:

  1. |𝔽_l| (defined as the quotient of ZMod l by x ∼ ±x) is in bijection with {0, 1, …, l^⋇} for odd l (page 3 of IUT II), so it has l^⋇ + 1 elements and l^⋇ nonzero elements.
  2. 𝔽_l^⋇ = 𝔽_l^× / {±1} (IUT I, page 3) has l^⋇ elements for an odd prime l.
  3. 24 · ∑_{j=1}^{l^⋇} j² = l (l² − 1) and the 1/l^⋇-weighted average of the exponents j² equals l (l + 1) / 12. Neither formula appears in the pages read; they are elementary arithmetic about the exponent vector of pages 122, 136, 145.
  4. Remark 2.6.3: for a finite connected subgraph Γ′ ≠ {0} of the line graph containing 0, i.e. a vertex set [−a, b] ⊆ ℤ with max(a, b) ≥ 1, the file computes |Γ′| = min(max(a,b), l^⋇) and ‖Γ′‖ = (n + 1)(2n + 1)/6 with n = |Γ′|, so ‖Γ′‖ is a function of |Γ′|, strictly increasing in it, monotone in max(a,b), and maximal (value l (l + 1)/12) exactly when max(a,b) ≥ l^⋇. The paper calls this "easily verified"; the file verifies it.

Interpretation choices (recorded in data/curated/iut2.json, record IUT2.Rem2_6_3):

  • The paper assumes l ≥ 5 prime. Facts 1, 3, 4 are proved for every odd l ≥ 3; fact 2 for every odd prime.
  • An element of 𝔽_l^⋇ is represented by its unique representative c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} (justified by fact 1); its fiber in ℤ is {k | k ≡ c or k ≡ −c mod l}.
  • "Min over the empty set is zero" and "|Γ′| is the number of classes c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ hit by Γ′" are taken literally from page 79. Nonzero vertices divisible by l map to 0 ∉ 𝔽_l^⋇ and are not counted.
  • Page 80 (d) says "monotone increasing [but not strictly increasing!] function of |Γ′| … constant for |Γ′| ≥ l^⋇". Since |Γ′| ≤ l^⋇ by definition, the literal reading gives a strictly increasing function (gammaNormValue_strictMono); the parenthetical and the constancy match the reading in terms of the radius max(a,b) (gammaNorm_eq_max_iff, gammaNorm_mono_radius, gammaNorm_le_max). Both are proved; neither is imposed.
  • Infinite Γ′ (Remark 2.6.3 (iii)) is not treated.
Public declarations

IUT.PaperII.Labels.lstardef

def lstar (l : ℕ) : ℕ := (l - 1) / 2

l^⋇ = (l − 1)/2 (IUT I, page 3).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.pmSetoiddef

def pmSetoid (l : ℕ) : Setoid (ZMod l) where
  r x y := x = y ∨ x = -y
  iseqv := by
    refine ⟨fun x => Or.inl rfl, ?_, ?_⟩
    · rintro x y (h | h)
      · exact Or.inl h.symm
      · exact Or.inr (by rw [h, neg_neg])
    · rintro x y z (h | h) (h' | h')
      · exact Or.inl (h.trans h')
      · exact Or.inr (h.trans h')
      · exact Or.inr (by rw [h, h'])
      · exact Or.inl (by rw [h, h', neg_neg])

The relation x ∼ y ⟺ x = y ∨ x = −y on 𝔽_l = ZMod l, whose quotient is |𝔽_l|.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.AbsLabeldef

def AbsLabel (l : ℕ) : Type := Quotient (pmSetoid l)

|𝔽_l| = 𝔽_l / {±1} (IUT II, page 3).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.labelRepdef

def labelRep (l : ℕ) (j : Fin (lstar l + 1)) : AbsLabel l :=
  Quotient.mk (pmSetoid l) ((j : ℕ) : ZMod l)

The class in |𝔽_l| of j ∈ {0, 1, …, l^⋇}.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.labelRep_bijectivetheorem

theorem labelRep_bijective {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) : Function.Bijective (labelRep l)

IUT II, page 3: for odd l, every class in |𝔽_l| has exactly one representative in {0, 1, …, l^⋇}.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.card_absLabeltheorem

|𝔽_l| has l^⋇ + 1 elements for odd l.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.zeroLabeldef

def zeroLabel (l : ℕ) : AbsLabel l := Quotient.mk (pmSetoid l) 0

The zero label 0 ∈ |𝔽_l|.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.card_nonzero_absLabeltheorem

theorem card_nonzero_absLabel {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) :
  Nat.card {x : AbsLabel l // x ≠ zeroLabel l} = lstar l

The decomposition |𝔽_l| = {0} ∪ 𝔽_l^⋇ at the level of cardinalities: for odd l there are exactly l^⋇ nonzero labels.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.FlStarabbrev

abbrev FlStar (l : ℕ) : Type := (ZMod l)ˣ ⧸ Subgroup.zpowers (-1 : (ZMod l)ˣ)

𝔽_l^⋇ = 𝔽_l^× / {±1} (IUT I, page 3), with {±1} the subgroup generated by −1.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.card_flStartheorem

theorem card_flStar {l : ℕ} [hp : Fact l.Prime] (hl : l ≠ 2) : Nat.card (FlStar l) = lstar l

For an odd prime l, 𝔽_l^⋇ has l^⋇ = (l − 1)/2 elements.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.six_mul_sum_sqtheorem

theorem six_mul_sum_sq (n : ℕ) : 6 * ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 n, j ^ 2 = n * (n + 1) * (2 * n + 1)

6 · ∑_{j=1}^{n} j² = n (n + 1) (2n + 1).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.sum_exponentstheorem

theorem sum_exponents {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) :
  24 * ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), j ^ 2 = l * (l ^ 2 - 1)

The exponents j², j = 1, …, l^⋇, of the Gaussian monoid (IUT II, pages 122, 136) satisfy 24 · ∑ j² = l (l² − 1) for odd l. Elementary; not stated in the paper.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.average_exponentstheorem

theorem average_exponents {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) :
  (∑ j ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), (j : ℚ) ^ 2) / lstar l = l * (l + 1) / 12

With weight 1/l^⋇ at each j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ (IUT II, Remark 4.7.3 (iii), page 145), the weighted total of the exponents j² is l (l + 1)/12, for odd l ≥ 3. Elementary; not stated in the paper.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.fiberIndef

def fiberIn (l a b c : ℕ) : Finset ℤ :=
  (Icc (-(a : ℤ)) b).filter fun k => (k : ZMod l) = (c : ZMod l) ∨ (k : ZMod l) = -(c : ZMod l)

The intersection of Γ′ = [−a, b] with the fiber over the class of c of the projection ℤ → |𝔽_l|, i.e. the integers k ∈ [−a, b] with k ≡ ±c (mod l).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.minSqdef

def minSq (S : Finset ℤ) : ℕ :=
  if h : S.Nonempty then (S.image fun k => k.natAbs ^ 2).min' (h.image _) else 0

min_{k ∈ S} k², with the convention of page 79 that the min over the empty set is 0.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaCarddef

def gammaCard (l a b : ℕ) : ℕ := ((Icc 1 (lstar l)).filter fun c => (fiberIn l a b c).Nonempty).card

|Γ′|: the number of classes c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇ (represented by c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇}) that contain a vertex of Γ′ = [−a, b], i.e. the size of the image in 𝔽_l^⋇ of the nonzero vertices.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormdef

def gammaNorm (l a b : ℕ) : ℚ :=
  (∑ c ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), (minSq (fiberIn l a b c) : ℚ)) / gammaCard l a b

‖Γ′‖ = |Γ′|⁻¹ · ∑_{c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} min_{k ∈ c ∩ Γ′} k² (IUT II, page 79). Domain: the paper's formula has a meaning only when |Γ′| ≥ 1, that is Γ′ ≠ {0} (one_le_gammaCard_iff). For a = b = 0 Lean's division by zero makes this function return 0; that value is a convention of the Lean definition, not a value given on page 79. Every lemma below that evaluates gammaNorm assumes 1 ≤ gammaCard l a b or 1 ≤ max a b.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.le_natAbs_of_mem_fiberlemma

lemma le_natAbs_of_mem_fiber {l c : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (hc : c ≤ lstar l) {k : ℤ}
  (hk : (k : ZMod l) = (c : ZMod l) ∨ (k : ZMod l) = -(c : ZMod l)) : c ≤ k.natAbs

A vertex in the fiber of c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} has absolute value at least c.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.fiberIn_nonempty_ifflemma

lemma fiberIn_nonempty_iff {l a b c : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (hc : c ≤ lstar l) :
  (fiberIn l a b c).Nonempty ↔ c ≤ max a b

The fiber of c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} meets [−a, b] if and only if c ≤ max a b.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.minSq_fiberInlemma

lemma minSq_fiberIn {l a b c : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (hc : c ≤ lstar l) (h : c ≤ max a b) :
  minSq (fiberIn l a b c) = c ^ 2

When the fiber of c ∈ {1, …, l^⋇} meets [−a, b], the minimum of k² over it is c².

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaCard_eqtheorem

theorem gammaCard_eq {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) :
  gammaCard l a b = min (max a b) (lstar l)

|Γ′| = min(max(a, b), l^⋇).

IUT.PaperII.Labels.sum_minSq_eqtheorem

theorem sum_minSq_eq {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) :
  ∑ c ∈ Icc 1 (lstar l), (minSq (fiberIn l a b c) : ℚ) =
    ∑ c ∈ Icc 1 (gammaCard l a b), (c : ℚ) ^ 2

∑_{c ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} min_{k ∈ c ∩ Γ′} k² = ∑_{c = 1}^{|Γ′|} c².

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormValuedef

def gammaNormValue (n : ℕ) : ℚ := ((n : ℚ) + 1) * (2 * n + 1) / 6

The closed form g(n) = (n + 1)(2n + 1)/6.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_eqtheorem

theorem gammaNorm_eq {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) (hpos : 1 ≤ gammaCard l a b) :
  gammaNorm l a b = gammaNormValue (gammaCard l a b)

Remark 2.6.3 (d), first part: ‖Γ′‖ is a function of |Γ′| alone, namely ‖Γ′‖ = (|Γ′| + 1)(2|Γ′| + 1)/6, whenever |Γ′| ≥ 1.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.one_le_gammaCard_ifftheorem

theorem one_le_gammaCard_iff {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) (a b : ℕ) :
  1 ≤ gammaCard l a b ↔ 1 ≤ max a b

|Γ′| ≥ 1 exactly when Γ′ ≠ {0}, for l ≥ 3.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormValue_strictMonotheorem

theorem gammaNormValue_strictMono : StrictMono gammaNormValue

The literal reading of (d): as a function of |Γ′|, the value ‖Γ′‖ is strictly increasing. Since |Γ′| ≤ l^⋇, its maximum is attained at |Γ′| = l^⋇.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaCard_letheorem

theorem gammaCard_le {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (a b : ℕ) : gammaCard l a b ≤ lstar l

|Γ′| ≤ l^⋇.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNormValue_lstartheorem

theorem gammaNormValue_lstar {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) :
  gammaNormValue (lstar l) = l * (l + 1) / 12

The maximal value: g(l^⋇) = l (l + 1)/12 for odd l.

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_le_maxtheorem

theorem gammaNorm_le_max {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) (a b : ℕ) (hab : 1 ≤ max a b) :
  gammaNorm l a b ≤ l * (l + 1) / 12

IUT.PaperII.Labels.gammaNorm_eq_max_ifftheorem

theorem gammaNorm_eq_max_iff {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) (a b : ℕ) (hab : 1 ≤ max a b) :

The radius reading of (d): the maximum l (l + 1)/12 is attained exactly when max(a, b) ≥ l^⋇, so ‖Γ′‖ is constant in the radius from l^⋇ on.

theorem gammaNorm_mono_radius {l : ℕ} (hl : Odd l) (h3 : 3 ≤ l) {a b a' b' : ℕ}

The radius reading of (d): ‖Γ′‖ is monotone in the radius max(a, b).

IUT III

Canonical splittings of the log-theta-lattice.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Comparison

IUT/PaperIII/Corollary312/Comparison.lean · imports IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Numerical, IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.HullOperator

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: an interface for the final comparison of regions

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Corollary 3.12 and Steps (x) to (xii) of its proof, pages 173 to 186; Proposition 3.9, pages 115 to 118; Remark 3.9.5 (i) to (iv), pages 126 to 129; Remark 3.1.1 (iii), (iv), pages 95 to 97; Remark 3.9.5 (vii), pages 132 and 133; IUT IV, Proposition 1.4 (i), page 13. Pages read for this file from the extracted text: 115 to 118, 125 to 129, 173 to 175, 180 to 186. Pages checked against rendered images: IUT III 95 to 97, 115 to 117, 127, 132, 133, 173 to 175, 181 to 185; IUT IV 13. Pages 118, 125, 126, 128, 129, 180 and 186 were read in text only; no formula of this file is taken from them.

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12. It is an interface for the last step of the proof, the passage from regions to the two numbers − |log(Θ)| and − |log(q)|. It does not construct any region from initial Θ-data, Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice, the log-Kummer correspondence or the multiradial representation of Theorem 3.11. All such regions are inputs.

The comparison of the q-pilot region with the hull of the union of the possible images (Containment, LogVolumeComparison below) is, in the paper, the content of Theorem 3.11 together with Steps (i) to (xi) of the proof. Here it is an unproved hypothesis. Every theorem below that takes it as a hypothesis is conditional on it, and nothing in this file gives evidence for or against it.

What is typed
  • RegionComparison X ι: data only. A family 𝓟 of regions on which a hull is defined, a family 𝓗 of hulls, a hull map with the properties (P1), (P2), (P3) of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) (IsHullMap from HullOperator.lean), an abstract region log-volume logVol : Set X → EReal with one property of the function (monotone into 𝓟), the q-pilot region, and a family of possible images indexed by an arbitrary type ι (the indeterminacy data). One regularity field: the union of the possible images lies in 𝓟. The bridge is proved at this level (RegionComparison.conclusion_of_containment).
  • logVolume μ S = ENNReal.log (μ S): the log-volume of a region for a measure, with log 0 = −∞ and log ∞ = +∞ (Mathlib's ENNReal.log). It is monotone, and finite exactly when 0 < μ S < ∞.
  • ComparisonSetting X ι: the measure instance, with logVol = logVolume μ (ComparisonSetting.toRegionComparison). Its theorems are the RegionComparison ones specialized; the interval examples below use it.
  • Containment (region form) and LogVolumeComparison (numerical form) of the comparison, as separate Props on a setting; logVolumeComparison_of_containment.
  • The bridge to Numerical.lean: toPilotLogVolumes, conclusion_iff (the conclusion is the conjunction of finiteness and LogVolumeComparison; nothing derived) and conclusion_of_containment (the inequality derived from containment, a sufficient condition; finiteness assumed).
  • Averaging over a finite set of labels j with equal weights (equalWeightAverage, conclusion_of_procContainment).
  • Nonvacuity on ℝ with Lebesgue measure and the interval hull Icc (sInf P) (sSup P): a setting where every hypothesis of the bridge holds (example_bridge_hypotheses), one where all of them except the comparison hold and the conclusion fails (example_comparison_fails), and one where no single possible image, and not the union either, contains the q-pilot region but the hull of the union does (example_union_matters).
Interpretation choices
  1. Regions and log-volume. Proposition 3.9 (i), pages 115 and 116, defines log-volumes on nonempty compact open subsets (nonarchimedean) or compact closures of nonempty open subsets (archimedean), determined by the normalized weights of Remark 3.1.1, and normalized so that the integral structure has log-volume 0 and multiplication by p_v adds −log(p_v) (multiplication by e adds 1). IUT IV, Proposition 1.4 (i), page 13, obtains such a normalization by dividing by the degree of the finite extension. Remark 3.1.1 (iii), page 95, warns that weighted sums of log-volumes do not in general arise as a positive multiple of the logarithm of a measure; (iv), pages 96 and 97, computes them on direct product regions as (1/N_E) times the logarithm of an E-weighted measure on another space. So the bridge is stated for an abstract logVol, and log μ is one instance. Here a region is an arbitrary subset.
  2. Normalization is not modeled. No field or hypothesis fixes the log-volume of an integral structure or the scaling law. The hypothesis μ qPilot < 1 of the measure instance (and qLogVolume < 0 in general) is only the sign condition |log(q)| > 0 of page 174: replacing a q-pilot region of mass 1/4 by one of mass 1/2 keeps it and changes the number. A normalized weighted packet log-volume would need: a log-volume on each direct summand normalized at the integral structure (Proposition 3.9 (i), pages 115 and 116), the weights of Remark 3.1.1 (ii), (iv), and, for comparison with the q-pilot object, the determinant raised to a positive tensor power det_{⊗M} with the normalization by O_(−) of Remark 3.9.5 (vii), (Ob3-1) to (Ob3), pages 132 and 133, where the details are left to the reader. Constructing it is open.
  3. The domain of the hull. Remark 3.9.5 (i), page 127, defines the hull of any subset that contains a relatively compact subset of finite log-volume; (ii) states (P1) to (P3) on the set ℙ of direct product regions. The paper does not say that the union of the possible images is a direct product region. The field union_mem takes 𝓟 to be a family on which (P1) to (P3) hold and which contains the union; this is a choice.
  4. Possible images. Page 174 defines n,◦U_{j,v_Q} as the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object subject to (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3), without a formula for the set of possible images. Here that set is an arbitrary indexed family image : ι → Set X; how (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3) generate it is not modeled.
  5. Signs. The symbol − |log(Θ)| is a name for the log-volume of the hull of the union, not the negative of an absolute value: negLogTheta is that log-volume itself. The symbol − |log(q)| is the log-volume of the q-pilot region, so absLogQ is minus that log-volume.
  6. Which form the text asserts. In the reading adopted here, the text never asserts a containment of regions. Steps (xi-c) and (xi-d), pages 182 and 183, say that the possible output data in 1,◦Ū are "linked/related" to the representation of the q-pilot object via isomorphisms of F^{⊩▶}-prime-strips. Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts the numerical inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}. So LogVolumeComparison is what the text asserts, and Containment is a region-level sufficient condition, not a quotation.
  7. Averaging. The statement (page 173) and Step (x) (page 181, rendered) average over j ∈ 𝔽_l^⊛; page 174 indexes the regions by j ∈ |𝔽_l|, and Step (xi-d), page 183, normalizes "with respect to j ∈ |𝔽_l|". Proposition 3.9 (i), page 116, defines the procession-normalization as "the average, over the various capsules". The average here is uniform over an arbitrary finite index type J (on an empty J the formula gives 0, a value that no theorem uses, see equalWeightAverage); the statement itself averages over 𝔽_l^⊛ with factor 1 / l^⊛ (pages 173 and 181); the file keeps J general.
What the interface omits, and what could be added next
  • *The sum over places v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ* (Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117): typed in Places.lean, for the measure instance.
  • The procession structure. The labels j index capsules of a procession, and the packet at j is a tensor product over j + 1 labels. Only the equal-weight average over j is here; LabelAverage.lean instantiates it at 𝔽_l^⋇ with factor 1/l^⋇.
  • *The identification of the two sides through the Θ^{×μ}_{LGP}-link* (Step (xi-a), page
    1. and through the permutation symmetry 0,◦U ≅ 1,◦U (Step (xi-b), page 181). Here both regions live in one space X with one log-volume logVol; that they can be placed there with a common log-volume is assumed by the typing, not proved. This is where IUT.Countermodels.Comparison shows some compatibility is indispensable.
  • Tensor packets, determinants and the tensor power of Step (xi-d), page 183, and the normalization of the log-volume (interpretation choice 2); not modeled.
  • The indeterminacies themselves. Here the family image is arbitrary. Indeterminacy.lean generates it as an orbit under the subgroup generated by two subgroups standing for (Ind1) and (Ind2), with invariance of log-volume as a hypothesis; (Ind3) is not typed there.
