IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolumedef

noncomputable def logVolume (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) : EReal := ENNReal.log (μ S)

The log-volume of a region S relative to a measure μ: log (μ S), with value −∞ when μ S = 0 and +∞ when μ S = ∞.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_monotheorem

theorem logVolume_mono (μ : Measure X) {S T : Set X} (h : S ⊆ T) :
  logVolume μ S ≤ logVolume μ T

Log-volume is monotone with respect to inclusion of regions.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_eq_bot_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_eq_bot_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  logVolume μ S = ⊥ ↔ μ S = 0

The log-volume is −∞ exactly when the region has measure zero.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_eq_top_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_eq_top_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  logVolume μ S = ⊤ ↔ μ S = ⊤

The log-volume is +∞ exactly when the region has infinite measure.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_finite_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_finite_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  (logVolume μ S ≠ ⊥ ∧ logVolume μ S ≠ ⊤) ↔ (μ S ≠ 0 ∧ μ S ≠ ⊤)

The log-volume is a real number exactly when the measure lies in (0, ∞).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.logVolume_neg_ifftheorem

theorem logVolume_neg_iff (μ : Measure X) (S : Set X) :
  logVolume μ S < 0 ↔ μ S < 1

The log-volume is negative exactly when the measure is less than 1.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparisonstructure

structure RegionComparison (X : Type*) (ι : Type*) where

The data for the last step of the proof of Corollary 3.12, for one component, with an abstract log-volume of regions. This is not the paper's situation: none of the fields is constructed from Hodge theaters or the multiradial representation. The region log-volume logVol is an arbitrary function with values in EReal; it need not be the logarithm of a measure (Remark 3.1.1 (iii), page 95: weighted sums of log-volumes are in general not a multiple of the logarithm of a measure). The fields are data, the hull axioms, one monotonicity property of logVol, and one well-posedness condition. No field relates the q-pilot region to the possible images.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.unionImagesdef

def unionImages : Set X := ⋃ i, S.image i

The union of the possible images (n,◦U, page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.hullOfUniondef

def hullOfUnion : Set X := S.hull S.unionImages

The hull of the union of the possible images (n,◦Ū, pages 174 and 175).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.hullOfUnion_memtheorem

theorem hullOfUnion_mem : S.hullOfUnion ∈ S.hullDomain

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.thetaLogVolumedef

def thetaLogVolume : EReal := S.logVol S.hullOfUnion

The log-volume of the hull of the union: the quantity named − |log(Θ)| (page 173).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.qLogVolumedef

def qLogVolume : EReal := S.logVol S.qPilot

The log-volume of the q-pilot region: the quantity named − |log(q)| (page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.image_subset_hullOfUniontheorem

theorem image_subset_hullOfUnion (i : ι) : S.image i ⊆ S.hullOfUnion

Each possible image lies in the hull of the union, by (P2).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.logVol_image_letheorem

theorem logVol_image_le (i : ι) : S.logVol (S.image i) ≤ S.thetaLogVolume

Each possible image has log-volume at most that of the hull of the union.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.Containmentdef

def Containment : Prop := S.qPilot ⊆ S.hullOfUnion

Comparison, region form. The q-pilot region is contained in the hull of the union of the possible images. The text does not assert this (see LogVolumeComparison); it is a sufficient condition, not formalized here.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.LogVolumeComparisondef

def LogVolumeComparison : Prop := S.qLogVolume ≤ S.thetaLogVolume

Comparison, numerical form. − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)|. This is what Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts; it is not formalized here. Once the two numbers are defined from a setting it is the second conjunct of Conclusion (see conclusion_iff).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.logVolumeComparison_of_containmenttheorem

theorem logVolumeComparison_of_containment (h : S.Containment) : S.LogVolumeComparison

The region form implies the numerical form, by monotonicity of logVol.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.containment_iff_forall_hulltheorem

theorem containment_iff_forall_hull :
  S.Containment ↔ ∀ H ∈ S.hulls, (∀ i, S.image i ⊆ H) → S.qPilot ⊆ H

The region form through the hull axioms: the q-pilot region lies in the hull of the union exactly when it lies in every hull that contains all possible images.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes (a b : EReal) (ha : a ≠ ⊥) (hneg : a < 0)
    (hb : b ≠ ⊥) : PilotLogVolumes where
  negLogTheta := b
  negLogTheta_ne_bot := hb
  absLogQ := -a.toReal
  absLogQ_pos := by
    have hcoe := EReal.coe_toReal (hneg.trans EReal.zero_lt_top).ne ha
    rw [← hcoe] at hneg
    linarith [EReal.coe_lt_coe_iff.mp (hneg.trans_eq (EReal.coe_zero).symm)]

Two log-volumes a (the q side) and b (the Θ side) as a PilotLogVolumes: negLogTheta = b and absLogQ = −a. The hypotheses are the facts PilotLogVolumes requires: b ≠ −∞ (value set ℝ ∪ {+∞}, page 173) and a real and negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PilotLogVolumes.conclusion_ofLogVolumes_ifftheorem

theorem PilotLogVolumes.conclusion_ofLogVolumes_iff (a b : EReal) (ha : a ≠ ⊥) (hneg : a < 0)
  (hb : b ≠ ⊥) : Conclusion (ofLogVolumes a b ha hneg hb) ↔ b ≠ ⊤ ∧ a ≤ b

For numbers built by ofLogVolumes, the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 is exactly finiteness of b together with a ≤ b.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.toPilotLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def toPilotLogVolumes (hq : S.qLogVolume ≠ ⊥) (hneg : S.qLogVolume < 0)
    (hΘ : S.thetaLogVolume ≠ ⊥) : PilotLogVolumes :=
  PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes S.qLogVolume S.thetaLogVolume hq hneg hΘ

The two numbers of Corollary 3.12 defined from a setting: − |log(Θ)| is the log-volume of the hull of the union, |log(q)| is minus the log-volume of the q-pilot region. The hypotheses hq, hneg say that − |log(q)| is real and negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174); hΘ says − |log(Θ)| ≠ −∞ (value set ℝ ∪ {+∞}, page 173).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.conclusion_ifftheorem

theorem conclusion_iff (hq : S.qLogVolume ≠ ⊥) (hneg : S.qLogVolume < 0)
  (hΘ : S.thetaLogVolume ≠ ⊥) :
  Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq hneg hΘ) ↔
    S.thetaLogVolume ≠ ⊤ ∧ S.LogVolumeComparison

The conclusion is the conjunction of two assumptions here. For the numbers defined from a setting, the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 (page 174) is exactly the conjunction of its two conjuncts: finiteness of − |log(Θ)| and LogVolumeComparison. Nothing is derived: the statement only unfolds definitions.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.conclusion_of_logVolumeComparisontheorem

theorem conclusion_of_logVolumeComparison (hq : S.qLogVolume ≠ ⊥) (hneg : S.qLogVolume < 0)
  (hΘ : S.thetaLogVolume ≠ ⊥) (hfin : S.thetaLogVolume ≠ ⊤)
  (hcomp : S.LogVolumeComparison) : Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq hneg hΘ)

The conclusion from its own two conjuncts. The hypotheses hfin (finiteness of − |log(Θ)|) and hcomp (the numerical inequality, asserted in Step (xi-f), page 184) are the two conjuncts of the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 (page 174); this theorem derives nothing and records only that no further hypothesis is involved. The one route below that derives a conjunct is conclusion_of_containment.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.RegionComparison.conclusion_of_containmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_containment (hq : S.qLogVolume ≠ ⊥) (hneg : S.qLogVolume < 0)
  (hfin : S.thetaLogVolume ≠ ⊤) (hcont : S.Containment) :
  Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq hneg
    (ne_bot_of_le_ne_bot hq (S.logVolumeComparison_of_containment hcont)))

Bridge, region form (conditional). Containment of the q-pilot region in the hull of the union, and finiteness of the log-volume of that hull, give the conclusion of Corollary 3.12. The inequality conjunct is derived from hcont by monotonicity of logVol; finiteness is assumed. Containment is a sufficient condition, not what the text asserts: Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts the numerical inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}. Both hcont and hfin are unproved hypotheses. − |log(Θ)| ≠ −∞ is derived from hcont and hq.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSettingstructure

structure ComparisonSetting (X : Type*) [MeasurableSpace X] (ι : Type*) where

The data of RegionComparison with the log-volume log (μ S) of one measure μ. This is not the paper's situation; see RegionComparison. The paper's packet log-volumes are weighted sums (Remark 3.1.1 (ii) to (iv), pages 94 to 97) and need not have this form.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.toRegionComparisondef

noncomputable def toRegionComparison : RegionComparison X ι where
  hullDomain := S.hullDomain
  hulls := S.hulls
  hull := S.hull
  isHullMap := S.isHullMap
  logVol := logVolume S.μ
  logVol_mono _ _ h _ := logVolume_mono S.μ h
  qPilot := S.qPilot
  image := S.image
  union_mem := S.union_mem

The measure setting as a RegionComparison with logVol S = log (μ S).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.Containmentdef

def Containment : Prop := S.qPilot ⊆ S.hullOfUnion

Comparison, region form. The q-pilot region is contained in the hull of the union of the possible images. In the reading adopted here the text does not assert this; see LogVolumeComparison. It is a sufficient condition for the numerical form (logVolumeComparison_of_containment); not formalized here.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.LogVolumeComparisondef

def LogVolumeComparison : Prop := S.qLogVolume ≤ S.thetaLogVolume

Comparison, numerical form. − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)|, i.e. the log-volume of the q-pilot region is at most that of the hull of the union. This is what Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts ("the inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}"). Its derivation in the paper rests on Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi-e); it is not formalized here. Once the two numbers are defined from a setting, this is the second conjunct of Conclusion (see conclusion_iff), so assuming it is assuming that conjunct.

theorem logVolumeComparison_of_containment (h : S.Containment) : S.LogVolumeComparison

The region form implies the numerical form, by monotonicity of log-volume.

The region form, through the hull axioms: the q-pilot region lies in the hull of the union exactly when it lies in every hull that contains all possible images.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.toPilotLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def toPilotLogVolumes (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
    (hΘ : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ 0) : PilotLogVolumes :=
  S.toRegionComparison.toPilotLogVolumes ((logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr hq0)
    ((logVolume_neg_iff _ _).mpr hq1) ((logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr hΘ)

The two numbers of Corollary 3.12 defined from a measure setting: − |log(Θ)| is the log-volume of the hull of the union, |log(q)| is minus the log-volume of the q-pilot region. Hypotheses: hq0, hq1: 0 < μ qPilot < 1, i.e. − |log(q)| is real and negative. This is the sign condition |log(q)| > 0 of page 174 and nothing more: it fixes no normalization (see the module docstring). hΘ: the hull of the union has positive measure, i.e. − |log(Θ)| ≠ −∞ (value set ℝ ∪ {+∞}, page 173).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.conclusion_ifftheorem

theorem conclusion_iff (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
  (hΘ : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ 0) :
  Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1 hΘ) ↔
    S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤ ∧ S.LogVolumeComparison

The conclusion is the conjunction of two assumptions here. For the numbers defined from a measure setting, the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 (page 174) is exactly finiteness of the measure of the hull of the union together with LogVolumeComparison: these are its two conjuncts, and nothing is derived.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.conclusion_of_logVolumeComparisontheorem

theorem conclusion_of_logVolumeComparison (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
  (hΘ : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ 0) (hfin : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤)
  (hcomp : S.LogVolumeComparison) : Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1 hΘ)

The conclusion from its own two conjuncts. The hypotheses hfin (the hull of the union has finite measure, i.e. − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ) and hcomp (the numerical inequality) are the two conjuncts of the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 (page 174); this theorem derives nothing. * hfin: the paper argues finiteness on page 175 from "the [easily verified] compactness" of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q}; the setting has no topology, so here finiteness is a separate hypothesis on the measure. Not formalized. * hcomp: asserted by Step (xi-f), page 184, from Theorem 3.11; not formalized. The only route here that derives the inequality conjunct is conclusion_of_containment.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ComparisonSetting.conclusion_of_containmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_containment (hq0 : S.μ S.qPilot ≠ 0) (hq1 : S.μ S.qPilot < 1)
  (hfin : S.μ S.hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤) (hcont : S.Containment) :
  Conclusion (S.toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1
    (fun h => hq0 (measure_mono_null hcont h)))

Bridge, region form (conditional). If the q-pilot region is contained in the hull of the union of the possible images, and that hull has finite measure, then the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 holds for the numbers defined from the setting. The inequality conjunct is derived from hcont; finiteness is assumed. Conditional on two unproved hypotheses: * hcont: the region form of the comparison, a sufficient condition and not what the text asserts (Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts a numerical inclusion); in the paper the comparison rests on Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi); * hfin: finite measure of the hull of the union; the paper argues finiteness on page 175 from compactness of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q}, which has no counterpart in this setting. The hypothesis hΘ (− |log(Θ)| ≠ −∞) is not needed: it follows from hcont and 0 < μ qPilot.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.equalWeightAveragedef

noncomputable def equalWeightAverage (v : J → EReal) : EReal :=
  (((Fintype.card J : ℝ)⁻¹ : ℝ) : EReal) * ∑ j, v j

The equal-weight average (1/|J|) · ∑_j v_j of a finite family of extended reals: the procession-normalization of Proposition 3.9 (i), page 116 ("the average, over the various capsules"). The paper's index set is nonempty. On an empty J the formula gives 0 (average_of_isEmpty); no theorem below uses that value: the bridge assumes the averaged q-side log-volume is negative, which excludes an empty J (nonempty_of_average_neg).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.average_of_isEmptytheorem

theorem average_of_isEmpty [IsEmpty J] (v : J → EReal) : equalWeightAverage v = 0

On an empty index type the average is 0, a value of the formula, not of the paper.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.nonempty_of_average_negtheorem

theorem nonempty_of_average_neg {v : J → EReal} (h : equalWeightAverage v < 0) :
  Nonempty J

A negative average forces a nonempty index type.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.average_monotheorem

theorem average_mono {v w : J → EReal} (h : ∀ j, v j ≤ w j) :
  equalWeightAverage v ≤ equalWeightAverage w

theorem average_ne_bot {v : J → EReal} (h : ∀ j, v j ≠ ⊥) : equalWeightAverage v ≠ ⊥

theorem average_ne_top {v : J → EReal} (h : ∀ j, v j ≠ ⊤) : equalWeightAverage v ≠ ⊤

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procThetaLogVolumedef

noncomputable def procThetaLogVolume : EReal := equalWeightAverage fun j => (S j).thetaLogVolume

The averaged log-volume of the hulls of the unions: − |log(Θ)| with the procession-normalization.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procQLogVolumedef

noncomputable def procQLogVolume : EReal := equalWeightAverage fun j => (S j).qLogVolume

The averaged log-volume of the q-pilot regions: − |log(q)| with the procession-normalization.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ProcContainmentdef

Region form of the comparison for a family of components: componentwise containment. Page 174 writes regions as collections indexed by (j, v_Q) and their containment componentwise. Not proved here; see ComparisonSetting.Containment.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ProcLogVolumeComparisondef

def ProcLogVolumeComparison : Prop := procQLogVolume S ≤ procThetaLogVolume S

Numerical form of the comparison with the procession-normalization: − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)| for the averaged log-volumes (Step (xi-f), page 184).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.procPilotLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def procPilotLogVolumes (hq : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧
      (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ ⊤) (hneg : procQLogVolume S < 0)
    (hΘ : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ 0) : PilotLogVolumes :=
  PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes (procQLogVolume S) (procThetaLogVolume S)
    (average_ne_bot fun j => (logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr (hq j).1) hneg
    (average_ne_bot fun j => (logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr (hΘ j))

The two numbers of Corollary 3.12 with the procession-normalization. Hypotheses: each q-pilot component has measure in (0, ∞) and the averaged q-side log-volume is negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174); each hull component has positive measure.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_of_procContainmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_procContainment (hq : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧
    (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ ⊤) (hneg : procQLogVolume S < 0)
  (hΘ : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ 0) (hfin : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤)
  (hcont : ProcContainment S) : Conclusion (procPilotLogVolumes S hq hneg hΘ)

Bridge with averaging (conditional). Componentwise containment of the q-pilot regions in the hulls of the unions, and finiteness of each hull, give the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 for the procession-normalized numbers. Conditional on hcont (Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi), not formalized) and hfin (page 175, compactness, not formalized).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.domaindef

def domain : Set (Set ℝ) := {P | P.Nonempty ∧ BddBelow P ∧ BddAbove P}

Bounded nonempty subsets of ℝ: the domain of the interval hull.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.hullsdef

def hulls : Set (Set ℝ) := {H | ∃ a b, a ≤ b ∧ H = Icc a b}

Nonempty closed bounded intervals: the hulls.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.hulldef

noncomputable def hull (P : Set ℝ) : Set ℝ := Icc (sInf P) (sSup P)

The interval hull P ↦ [inf P, sup P].

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.isHullMaptheorem

theorem isHullMap : IsHullMap domain hulls hull

The interval hull satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3) of Remark 3.9.5 (ii).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.intervalImagedef

noncomputable def intervalImage : Bool → Set ℝ
  | false => Icc 0 (1 / 8)
  | true => Icc (3 / 8) (1 / 2)

The two possible images [0, 1/8] and [3/8, 1/2], indexed by Bool.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.union_eqtheorem

theorem union_eq : (⋃ i, intervalImage i) = Icc 0 (1 / 8) ∪ Icc (3 / 8) (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.hull_uniontheorem

theorem hull_union : hull (⋃ i, intervalImage i) = Icc 0 (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.settingdef

noncomputable def setting (q : Set ℝ) : ComparisonSetting ℝ Bool where
  μ := volume
  hullDomain := domain
  hulls := hulls
  hull := hull
  isHullMap := isHullMap
  qPilot := q
  image := intervalImage
  union_mem := by
    rw [union_eq]
    exact ⟨⟨0, Or.inl ⟨le_rfl, by norm_num⟩⟩, bddBelow_Icc.union bddBelow_Icc,
      bddAbove_Icc.union bddAbove_Icc⟩

The setting on ℝ with Lebesgue measure, the interval hull, the two possible images above, and a q-pilot region q.

theorem setting_hullOfUnion (q : Set ℝ) : (setting q).hullOfUnion = Icc 0 (1 / 2)

theorem volume_hullOfUnion (q : Set ℝ) :
  (setting q).μ (setting q).hullOfUnion = ENNReal.ofReal (1 / 2)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_bridge_hypothesestheorem

theorem example_bridge_hypotheses :
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).qPilot < 1 ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).hullOfUnion ≠ 0 ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤ ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).Containment

Nonvacuity (a). With q-pilot region [0, 1/2], every hypothesis of the region-form bridge holds: 0 < μ q < 1, the hull of the union has finite positive measure, and the q-pilot region is contained in it. Hence conclusion_of_containment applies.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_conclusiontheorem

theorem example_conclusion :
  ∃ v : PilotLogVolumes, Conclusion v

Some value of PilotLogVolumes satisfies Conclusion. The statement is only this existential: it does not name the witness and says nothing about example (a), and any v with a finite nonnegative negLogTheta would also witness it. The proof uses the numbers of example (a), through conclusion_of_containment, but that choice is not part of what is stated.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_comparison_failstheorem

theorem example_comparison_fails :
  ∃ (hq0 : (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).qPilot ≠ 0)
    (hq1 : (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).qPilot < 1)
    (hΘ : (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).hullOfUnion ≠ 0),
    (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).μ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤ ∧
    ¬ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).LogVolumeComparison ∧
    ¬ (setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).Containment ∧
    ¬ Conclusion ((setting (Icc 0 (3 / 4))).toPilotLogVolumes hq0 hq1 hΘ)

Nonvacuity (b): the comparison is an extra condition. With q-pilot region [0, 3/4], every field of the setting and every side hypothesis of the bridge holds (0 < μ q < 1, the hull of the union has finite positive measure), but LogVolumeComparison fails, hence also Containment, and the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 is false for the numbers so defined.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IntervalExample.example_union_matterstheorem

theorem example_union_matters :
  (∀ i, ¬ Icc (0 : ℝ) (1 / 2) ⊆ hull (intervalImage i)) ∧
  (∀ i, ¬ logVolume volume (Icc (0 : ℝ) (1 / 2)) ≤ logVolume volume (hull (intervalImage i))) ∧
  ¬ Icc (0 : ℝ) (1 / 2) ⊆ ⋃ i, intervalImage i ∧
  (setting (Icc 0 (1 / 2))).Containment

Nonvacuity (c): the union and the hull matter. With q-pilot region [0, 1/2]: the hull of no single possible image contains the q-pilot region, nor does any single image satisfy the numerical comparison with its own hull; the union itself does not contain the q-pilot region; but the hull of the union does.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Indeterminacy

IUT/PaperIII/Corollary312/Indeterminacy.lean · imports IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Comparison

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: possible images as an orbit under the indeterminacies

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Theorem 3.11 (i), (ii), pages 153 to 157 (the indeterminacies (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3)); the statement of Corollary 3.12 and the opening of its proof, pages 173 to 175; Steps (ii) to (iv), pages 175 to 177; Step (x), page 181. Pages read for this file: 153 to 157, 173 to 178, 181. Pages checked against rendered images: 153, 154, 156, 157, 173 to 177, 181. Pages 155 and 178 were read in text only; no formula of this file is taken from them.

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12 or of Theorem 3.11. It replaces the arbitrary indexed family of possible images of Comparison.lean by an orbit. As in Comparison.lean, no region is constructed from initial Θ-data, Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice or the multiradial representation; the acting group, the base region and the q-pilot region are inputs. The comparison of the q-pilot region with the hull of the union of the possible images remains an unproved hypothesis, and nothing here gives evidence for or against it.

What the pages say
  • (Ind1), page 154 (rendered): the indeterminacies induced by the automorphisms of the procession of D^⊢-prime-strips.
  • (Ind2), page 154 (rendered): for each v_Q, the action of independent copies of Ism (nonarchimedean) or of the automorphisms of order 2 (archimedean) on each of the direct summands of the j + 1 factors of the tensor packet.
  • (Ind3), page 156 (rendered): as m varies, the isomorphisms of (a) are "upper semi-compatible" relative to the log-links, "in a sense that involves certain natural inclusions ⊆ at v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ^non and certain natural surjections ↠ at v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ^arc", with details deferred to Proposition 3.5 (ii) (a), (b).
  • The statement, pages 173 and 174 (rendered): − |log(Θ)| is the log-volume of the hull of the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object, subject to (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3).
  • Step (x), page 181 (rendered): the procession-normalized log-volumes are invariant under (Ind1), (Ind2), and convert (Ind3) into an inequality "from above" (asserted, "one verifies immediately").
  • Step (iv), pages 176 and 177 (rendered): working with vertically coric structures is possible "only at the cost of admitting the indeterminacy constituted by the upper semi-compatibility of (Ind3)". No further description of (Ind3) is given there.
What is typed
  • IndeterminacyOrbit X A: data only. Two subgroups ind1, ind2 of one group A acting on the ambient space X, and a base region. They are not assumed to commute: the joint indeterminacy is the subgroup ind1 ⊔ ind2 generated by both (jointGroup), inside the common group A. The possible images are the translates a • base for a in the joint group (orbitImage), and orbitUnion is their union.
  • smul_orbitUnion: the orbit union is invariant under the joint group.
  • HullEquivariant: equivariance of a hull map under a subgroup, as a hypothesis; with it, the hull of the orbit union is invariant (smul_hull_orbitUnion). example_not_equivariant shows that the interval hull of Comparison.lean is not equivariant under the transposition of 0 and 5 (a measurable bijection of ℝ).
  • toSetting: a ComparisonSetting whose possible images are the orbit, and logVolume_orbitImage_le: the log-volume of the hull of the orbit union bounds the log-volume of every member.
  • measureInvariantSubgroup μ: the elements a with μ (a • S) = μ S for every S. logVolume_orbitImage_eq: if every element of ind1 and every element of ind2 preserves μ, every member of the orbit has the log-volume of the base region. This is the abstract form of the invariance asserted in Step (x), page 181, taken as a hypothesis; the hypothesis is needed only for ind1 and ind2 separately, not for the joint group.
  • Examples on ℝ with Lebesgue measure: with (Ind1) generated by x ↦ −x and base region [0, 1/8], every member has volume 1/8 and the orbit union has volume 1/4 (example_orbit_enlarges); the comparison holds for q-pilot region [0, 1/8] (example_orbit_conclusion) and fails for [0, 1/2], with every structural hypothesis satisfied (example_orbit_comparison_fails). With (Ind1) generated by x ↦ 2x, two members have different volumes (example_members_differ), so without measure preservation "the log-volume of the possible image" is not one number, while the hull of the union still bounds all of them.
(Ind3) is not typed

Page 156 describes (Ind3) by "certain natural inclusions" at nonarchimedean places and "certain natural surjections" at archimedean places, and defers the details to Proposition 3.5 (ii) (a), (b), which is outside the pages read for this file. A surjection is not a containment of regions, and the pages read give no explicit family of enlargements. So (Ind3) is not typed here. A typed version would replace orbitImage by the upward closure {Y | ∃ a, a • base ⊑ Y} for an explicit relation ⊑; that relation is not available from the pages read.

Interpretation choices
  1. Measurability. The task of the orbit is to produce regions; logVolume is log of the outer measure of an arbitrary subset, so no measurability of the action is needed by any statement here, and none is assumed. Every map used in the examples is measurable.
  2. One ambient space. The action is on one space X carrying one measure. The paper's (Ind2) acts independently on direct summands of tensor packets at each place; that product structure is not modeled.
  3. Possible images as translates. A possible image is a • base. The paper does not give a formula for the set of possible images (page 174); the orbit is one reading of "subject to (Ind1), (Ind2)".
What is still omitted

(Ind3); tensor packets and their direct summands; the sum over places (Places.lean) and the average over labels (LabelAverage.lean); the identification of the two sides through the Θ^{×μ}_{LGP}-link (Steps (xi-a), (xi-b), pages 181 and 182); the determinant and tensor power normalization of Step (xi-d), page 183; compactness of the orbit union (page 175). IUT.Countermodels.Indet.separate_finite_joint_infinite shows that the union over the joint group can be unbounded although the unions for each indeterminacy separately are bounded.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbitstructure

structure IndeterminacyOrbit (X A : Type*) [Group A] [MulAction A X] where

The indeterminacies acting on regions: two subgroups of one group A acting on X (the abstract (Ind1) and (Ind2) of Theorem 3.11 (i), page 154), not assumed to commute, and a base region (the image of a Θ-pilot object before the indeterminacies act). Data only.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.jointGroupdef

def jointGroup : Subgroup A := O.ind1 ⊔ O.ind2

The joint indeterminacy: the subgroup generated by ind1 and ind2.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.orbitImagedef

def orbitImage (g : O.jointGroup) : Set X := (g : A) • O.baseRegion

The possible image indexed by an element of the joint group.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.orbitUniondef

def orbitUnion : Set X := ⋃ g, O.orbitImage g

The union of the possible images.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.baseRegion_subset_orbitUniontheorem

theorem baseRegion_subset_orbitUnion : O.baseRegion ⊆ O.orbitUnion

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.smul_orbitUniontheorem

theorem smul_orbitUnion {a : A} (ha : a ∈ O.jointGroup) : a • O.orbitUnion = O.orbitUnion

The orbit union is invariant under every element of the joint group.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.HullEquivariantdef

def HullEquivariant (hullDomain : Set (Set X)) (hull : Set X → Set X) (H : Subgroup A) : Prop :=
  ∀ a ∈ H, ∀ P ∈ hullDomain, hull (a • P) = a • hull P

Equivariance of a hull map under a subgroup, on the domain of the hull: a hypothesis on the hull map, not a property of every hull map (example_not_equivariant).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.smul_hull_orbitUniontheorem

theorem smul_hull_orbitUnion {hullDomain : Set (Set X)} {hull : Set X → Set X}
  (heq : HullEquivariant hullDomain hull O.jointGroup) (hU : O.orbitUnion ∈ hullDomain)
  {a : A} (ha : a ∈ O.jointGroup) : a • hull O.orbitUnion = hull O.orbitUnion

If the hull map is equivariant under the joint group, the hull of the orbit union is invariant under the joint group.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.toSettingdef

def toSetting (μ : Measure X) (hullDomain hulls : Set (Set X)) (hull : Set X → Set X)
    (isHullMap : IsHullMap hullDomain hulls hull) (qPilot : Set X)
    (union_mem : O.orbitUnion ∈ hullDomain) : ComparisonSetting X O.jointGroup where
  μ := μ
  hullDomain := hullDomain
  hulls := hulls
  hull := hull
  isHullMap := isHullMap
  qPilot := qPilot
  image := O.orbitImage
  union_mem := union_mem

The ComparisonSetting whose possible images are the orbit of the base region under the joint group.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.logVolume_orbitImage_letheorem

theorem logVolume_orbitImage_le (μ : Measure X) (hullDomain hulls : Set (Set X))
  (hull : Set X → Set X) (isHullMap : IsHullMap hullDomain hulls hull) (qPilot : Set X)
  (union_mem : O.orbitUnion ∈ hullDomain) (g : O.jointGroup) :
  logVolume μ (O.orbitImage g) ≤
    (O.toSetting μ hullDomain hulls hull isHullMap qPilot union_mem).thetaLogVolume

The log-volume of the hull of the orbit union is an upper bound for the log-volume of every possible image.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.measureInvariantSubgroupdef

def measureInvariantSubgroup {X : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X] (A : Type*) [Group A]
    [MulAction A X] (μ : Measure X) : Subgroup A where
  carrier := {a | ∀ S : Set X, μ (a • S) = μ S}
  one_mem' S := by simp
  mul_mem' {a b} ha hb S := by
    simp only [mem_ofPred_eq] at ha hb ⊢
    rw [← smul_smul, ha, hb]
  inv_mem' {a} ha S := by
    simp only [mem_ofPred_eq] at ha ⊢
    rw [← ha (a⁻¹ • S), smul_inv_smul]

The elements of A that preserve the measure of every subset under the action.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.IndeterminacyOrbit.logVolume_orbitImage_eqtheorem

theorem logVolume_orbitImage_eq (μ : Measure X)
  (h1 : ∀ a ∈ O.ind1, ∀ S : Set X, μ (a • S) = μ S)
  (h2 : ∀ a ∈ O.ind2, ∀ S : Set X, μ (a • S) = μ S) (g : O.jointGroup) :
  logVolume μ (O.orbitImage g) = logVolume μ O.baseRegion

Invariance under the joint indeterminacy. If every element of ind1 and every element of ind2 preserves μ, then every possible image has the log-volume of the base region. The hypotheses are the abstract form of the invariance under (Ind1) and (Ind2) asserted in Step (x), page 181; they are not proved here.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.OrbitExample.example_not_equivarianttheorem

theorem example_not_equivariant :
  ¬ HullEquivariant (A := Equiv.Perm ℝ) domain hull
    (Subgroup.zpowers (Equiv.swap (0 : ℝ) 5))

Equivariance can fail. The interval hull is not equivariant under the subgroup generated by the transposition of 0 and 5: for P = [0, 1] the hull of the image contains 2, the image of the hull does not.

def reflection : Equiv.Perm ℝ := Equiv.neg ℝ

The reflection x ↦ −x as a permutation of ℝ.

Indeterminacy enlarges the region. The reflection preserves Lebesgue measure, every possible image has volume 1/8, and the orbit union (equal to its hull) has volume 1/4 > 1/8.

Without measure preservation the possible images have different log-volumes. For the scaling x ↦ 2x, the possible images [0, 1] and [0, 2] have log-volumes 0 and log 2, so "the log-volume of the possible image" is not one number; only the hull of the union bounds all of them (logVolume_orbitImage_le).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.LabelAverage

IUT/PaperIII/Corollary312/LabelAverage.lean · imports IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Comparison, IUT.PaperII.Labels

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: the average over the labels j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Proposition 3.9 (i), page 116; Remark 3.9.3, pages 119 and 120; Theorem 3.11 (i), page 153; the statement of Corollary 3.12, pages 173 and 174; Steps (x) and (xi-d) of its proof, pages 181 and 183. Pages read for this file: 116, 119, 120, 153, 154, 173, 174, 181 to 183. Pages checked against rendered images: 116, 153, 154, 173, 174, 181, 183. Pages 119, 120 and 182 were read in text only; no formula of this file is taken from them. The label sets come from IUT.PaperII.Labels, whose sources are listed there (IUT I, page 3, and IUT II, page 3, both rendered).

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12. It instantiates the equal-weight average of Comparison.lean at the index set of the paper. As in Comparison.lean, no region is constructed from initial Θ-data, Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice or the multiradial representation of Theorem 3.11; the regions at the various labels are inputs, and the comparison of the q-pilot region with the hull of the union of the possible images remains an unproved hypothesis.

What the pages say
  • The statement, page 173 (rendered): − |log(Θ)| is the procession-normalized log-volume, "where the average is taken over j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇". Step (x), page 181 (rendered), says the same. 𝔽_l^⋇ has l^⋇ = (l − 1)/2 elements, so the average carries the factor 1/l^⋇.
  • Theorem 3.11 (i) (a), page 153 (rendered), indexes the packets by j ∈ |𝔽_l|, and page 174 (rendered) indexes the regions n,◦U_{j,v_Q} and the hulls n,◦Ū_{j,v_Q} by j ∈ |𝔽_l|.
  • |𝔽_l| = {0} ∪ 𝔽_l^⋇ (IUT II, page 3; IUT.PaperII.Labels.card_nonzero_absLabel).
What is typed
  • NonzeroLabel l: the nonzero elements of |𝔽_l| (the type AbsLabel l of IUT.PaperII.Labels), which is 𝔽_l^⋇ through the decomposition |𝔽_l| = {0} ∪ 𝔽_l^⋇. It has l^⋇ elements for odd l (card_nonzeroLabel).
  • labelAverage l v = (1/l^⋇) · ∑_{j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇} v_j, the normalization of pages 173 and 181, and labelAverage_eq_average: it is the equalWeightAverage of Comparison.lean at this index set.
  • conclusion_of_labelContainment: the averaged bridge of Comparison.lean at this index set, with the two numbers identified as label averages.
  • Nonvacuity for every odd l ≥ 3: every hypothesis of the bridge holds (example_label_conclusion), and every structural hypothesis holds while the conclusion fails (example_label_conclusion_fails).
Two gaps that this file does not resolve
  1. Page 183. Step (xi-d) applies the log-volume "suitably normalized, with respect to j ∈ |𝔽_l|". No factor is displayed there. An average over |𝔽_l| would carry the factor 1/(l^⋇ + 1) (IUT.PaperII.Labels.card_absLabel), not 1/l^⋇; nothing on pages 173, 174, 181 or 183 displays that factor. This file uses the factor 1/l^⋇ of pages 173 and 181.
  2. *The label 0.* The regions are indexed by |𝔽_l|, which contains the label 0, while the average runs over 𝔽_l^⋇. Page 174 recalls that the symbol △ results from identifying the labels 0 and ⟨𝔽_l^⋇⟩, and page 181 cites that identification as a source of the averages; neither page states how the component at 0 enters the average. It may be dropped, or identified with the diagonal label. This file takes families indexed by NonzeroLabel l only: a family indexed by |𝔽_l| must first be turned into one indexed by 𝔽_l^⋇, and no rule for doing so is assumed or provided here.
What is still omitted

The sum over places (see Places.lean), tensor packets and the identification of the capsule at j with a tensor product over j + 1 labels, the identification of the two sides through the Θ^{×μ}_{LGP}-link (Steps (xi-a), (xi-b), pages 181 and 182), the determinant and tensor power normalization of Step (xi-d), page 183, and the indeterminacies (see Indeterminacy.lean).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_of_labelContainmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_labelContainment (hl : Odd l) [Fintype (NonzeroLabel l)]
  (hq : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ 0 ∧ (S j).μ (S j).qPilot ≠ ⊤)
  (hneg : procQLogVolume S < 0) (hΘ : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ 0)
  (hfin : ∀ j, (S j).μ (S j).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤) (hcont : ProcContainment S) :
  Conclusion (procPilotLogVolumes S hq hneg hΘ) ∧
    (procPilotLogVolumes S hq hneg hΘ).negLogTheta =
      labelAverage l (fun j => (S j).thetaLogVolume) ∧
    (((-(procPilotLogVolumes S hq hneg hΘ).absLogQ : ℝ)) : EReal) =
      labelAverage l (fun j => (S j).qLogVolume)

Bridge with the paper's normalization (conditional). For a family of settings indexed by j ∈ 𝔽_l^⋇, componentwise containment and finiteness of each hull give the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 for the procession-normalized numbers of Comparison.lean, and these numbers are the label averages with factor 1/l^⋇: − |log(Θ)| is the average of the Θ-side log-volumes and − |log(q)| the average of the q-side ones. Conditional on hcont (Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi), not formalized) and hfin (page 175, compactness, not formalized).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Numerical

IUT/PaperIII/Corollary312/Numerical.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.Arithmetic

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: the numerical layer of the statement

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Corollary 3.12 and its proof, pages 173 to 186 of the May 2020 manuscript, read in full. The statement was also read from the rendered pages 173 and 174.

What this file is and is not

Corollary 3.12 defines two numbers from a collection of initial Θ-data:

  • − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ ∪ {+∞}, the procession-normalized mono-analytic log-volume of the holomorphic hull of the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object, subject to the indeterminacies (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3);
  • − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ, the corresponding log-volume of the image of a q-pilot object, not subject to the indeterminacies, with |log(q)| > 0.

It asserts that − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ and − |log(Θ)| ≥ − |log(q)|.

This project does not yet construct either number from initial Θ-data: Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice, tensor packets and the multiradial representation are not formalized. So this file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12. It formalizes only what can be said about the two numbers once they are given: the shape of the conclusion, the "i.e." reformulation in the statement, the reduction at the start of the proof, and the purely numerical content of Steps (xi-d) to (xi-f). The structure below has no field that assumes any conclusion of the paper.

Public declarations
variable (v : PilotLogVolumes)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PilotLogVolumesstructure

structure PilotLogVolumes where

The two numbers that the statement of Corollary 3.12 defines, with the two properties that the statement attributes to them by definition: the value set ℝ ∪ {+∞} of − |log(Θ)|, and |log(q)| > 0. Nothing here is constructed from initial Θ-data.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Conclusiondef

def Conclusion : Prop :=
  v.negLogTheta ≠ ⊤ ∧ ((-v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta

The conclusion of Corollary 3.12: − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ and − |log(Θ)| ≥ − |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.ConstantFormdef

def ConstantForm : Prop :=
  ∀ C : ℝ, v.negLogTheta ≤ ((C * v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) → -1 ≤ C

The reformulation after "i.e." in the statement: C_Θ ≥ −1 for any real number C_Θ such that − |log(Θ)| ≤ C_Θ · |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.constantForm_of_conclusiontheorem

theorem constantForm_of_conclusion (h : Conclusion v) : ConstantForm v

The conclusion implies the "i.e." form.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_of_constantFormtheorem

theorem conclusion_of_constantForm (hfin : v.negLogTheta ≠ ⊤) (h : ConstantForm v) :
  Conclusion v

When − |log(Θ)| is finite, the "i.e." form implies the inequality.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.constantForm_of_toptheorem

theorem constantForm_of_top (h : v.negLogTheta = ⊤) : ConstantForm v

The "i.e." form alone does not give finiteness: it holds when − |log(Θ)| = +∞, because then no real C_Θ satisfies its hypothesis. So the "i.e." of the statement reformulates the inequality, and the assertion − |log(Θ)| ∈ ℝ is a separate claim.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_iff_constantFormtheorem

theorem conclusion_iff_constantForm (hfin : v.negLogTheta ≠ ⊤) :
  Conclusion v ↔ ConstantForm v

With finiteness assumed, the conclusion and the "i.e." form are equivalent.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.inequality_of_nonnegtheorem

theorem inequality_of_nonneg (h : 0 ≤ v.negLogTheta) :
  ((-v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta

The reduction at the start of the proof: "we may assume without loss of generality that − |log(Θ)| < 0", because − |log(Θ)| ≥ 0 gives − |log(Θ)| ≥ 0 > − |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.possibleLogVolumesdef

def possibleLogVolumes : Set ℝ := {lam | (lam : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta}

The subset ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|} = {λ ∈ ℝ | λ ≤ − |log(Θ)|} of Step (xi-d).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.stepXIf_inclusion_iff_inequalitytheorem

theorem stepXIf_inclusion_iff_inequality :
  -v.absLogQ ∈ possibleLogVolumes v ↔ ((-v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal) ≤ v.negLogTheta

Step (xi-f) at the level of numbers. The proof ends: "The inclusion − |log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|}, hence also the inequality − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)|, then follows formally." The second step is indeed formal: the inclusion and the inequality are the same proposition, by unfolding the definition of the subset. Consequently every bit of mathematical content of Step (xi) lies in establishing the inclusion, that is, in Steps (xi-a) to (xi-e) and the first sentence of (xi-f). No formalization that keeps only the two real numbers can express those steps: stated about numbers alone, the premise of the last step is the conclusion. A non-circular formalization must model the multiradial representation itself, with the properties (IPL) and (SHE) of Remark 3.11.1.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.thm1_10_final_of_cor312theorem

theorem thm1_10_final_of_cor312 {l d D E Q : ℝ} (hl : 7 ≤ l) (hd : 1 ≤ d) (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hE : 0 ≤ E)
  (hbound : v.negLogTheta ≤ ((constant l d D E Q v.absLogQ * v.absLogQ : ℝ) : EReal))
  (hcor : Conclusion v) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * D + 20 * E

Conditional derivation of the final inequality of IUT IV, Theorem 1.10. Hypotheses, each a statement about given real numbers: * hbound: − |log(Θ)| ≤ C_Θ · |log(q)| for the constant C_Θ displayed in Theorem 1.10. This is the first assertion of Theorem 1.10 ("one may take the constant C_Θ to be ..."). * hcor: the conclusion of Corollary 3.12. * the side conditions 7 ≤ l, 1 ≤ d, 0 ≤ D, 0 ≤ E that the paper cites. Conclusion: (1/6)·log(𝔮) ≤ (1 + 20·d_mod/l)·(log 𝔡 + log 𝔣) + 20·(e*_mod·l + η_prm). The theorem is conditional. It does not show that the objects of the paper satisfy hbound or hcor; it shows that nothing else is used in passing from them to the final inequality.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Places

IUT/PaperIII/Corollary312/Places.lean · imports IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.Comparison

IUT III, Corollary 3.12: the sum over places in the interface of the final comparison

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 3.1.1 (ii) to (iv), pages 94 to 97; Proposition 3.9 (i) to (iii), pages 115 to 117; the statement of Corollary 3.12 and the opening of its proof, pages 173 to 175; Step (xi-d), page 183. Pages read for this file: 94 to 97, 115 to 120, 173 to 175, 181 to 183. Pages checked against rendered images: 94 to 97, 116, 117, 173 to 175, 181, 183. Pages 115, 118, 119 and 120 were read in text only; no formula of this file is taken from them.

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 3.12, nor of Proposition 3.9 (iii). It adds one feature to the interface of Comparison.lean: the passage from local log-volumes, one for each place, to a global log-volume. As in Comparison.lean, no region is constructed from initial Θ-data, Hodge theaters, the log-theta-lattice or the multiradial representation of Theorem 3.11; every region and every measure is an input. The comparison of the q-pilot region with the hull of the union of the possible images remains an unproved hypothesis, now placewise (PlacewiseContainment) or global (GlobalLogVolumeComparison); nothing here gives evidence for or against it.

What the pages say
  • Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117 (rendered): on the families indexed by v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ whose components have zero log-volume for all but finitely many v_Q, adding the local log-volumes of (i) ("all but finitely many of which are zero!") gives a global log-volume with values in ℝ.
  • Proposition 3.9 (i), pages 115 and 116 (page 116 rendered): the local log-volumes are defined on nonempty compact open subsets (nonarchimedean) or compact closures of nonempty open subsets (archimedean), with values in ℝ, and are determined by the normalized weights of Remark 3.1.1 (ii), (iii), (iv).
  • Remark 3.1.1 (ii), page 94 (rendered): the portion corresponding to K_v gets the weight [K_v : (F_mod)_v]⁻¹; a direct sum over the v above v_Q gets the normalized weight 1 / ([K_v : (F_mod)_v] · ∑_{w | v_Q} [(F_mod)_w : ℚ_{v_Q}]), and tensor products over A get the analogous products. Remark 3.1.1 (iv), page 97 (rendered): for a direct product region ∏_v S_v, (1/N_E) · μ^log_E(S) = ∑_{v ∈ V} (1/N_v) · μ^log_v(S_v).
What is typed
  • PlaceWeights P: a positive real weight at each place of an index type P. Two readings are covered. If P is 𝕍_ℚ, page 117 adds the local log-volumes with weight 1 (unitPlaceWeights), the weights of Remark 3.1.1 being already inside each local log-volume. If P is a set of places v lying over 𝕍_ℚ, the weight at v is a normalized weight of Remark 3.1.1 (ii) or the 1/N_v of (iv). Positivity is the one regularity field; each displayed weight on page 94 is the reciprocal of a positive number.
  • LogVolumeAlmostAllZero μ R: the condition of page 117, "zero log-volume for all but finitely many" places, for a family of regions R v with measures μ v.
  • globalLogVolume w μ R = ∑ᶠ v, w v · log μ_v (R v), with value in EReal.
  • Monotonicity under placewise containment (globalLogVolume_mono), finiteness when every local term is finite (globalLogVolume_ne_bot, globalLogVolume_ne_top), and the bridge to Conclusion (conclusion_of_placewiseContainment).
  • Examples on two places, built from IntervalExample.setting of Comparison.lean: one where every hypothesis of the bridge holds, one where every structural hypothesis holds and the global comparison and the conclusion fail, and one where the global comparison holds while the placewise comparison fails at one place.
The values −∞ and +∞

The paper's local log-volumes are real (page 116: values in ℝ on 𝔐(−)), so the paper's hypotheses exclude ±∞ at every place, and the sum of page 117 is a finite sum of real numbers. Here a local log-volume is log of an arbitrary measure (logVolume of Comparison.lean), so it can be −∞ (measure zero) or +∞ (infinite measure). In EReal, Mathlib's convention is ⊥ + ⊤ = ⊥ (EReal.bot_add): a sum containing both −∞ and +∞ is −∞. Nothing below depends on that case being meaningful:

  • globalLogVolume_mono holds in EReal with no finiteness hypothesis, because addition on EReal is monotone in each argument whatever the convention for ⊥ + ⊤;
  • finiteness of the global value is proved only from finiteness of each local term;
  • the bridge assumes every q-pilot component has positive measure and every hull component has positive finite measure, so the case ⊥ + ⊤ does not arise on either side.

If the support condition LogVolumeAlmostAllZero fails, ∑ᶠ returns 0 by Mathlib's convention; every theorem that uses the sum assumes the support condition, so that value is never used.

Interpretation choices
  1. Which places. The index type P and the weights are arbitrary; the reading of page 117 (P = 𝕍_ℚ, weight 1) is the instance unitPlaceWeights. Remark 3.1.1 (iii), page 95, warns that weighted sums of log-volumes are not in general the logarithm of one measure; here only weighted sums of logarithms of measures, one measure per place, are formed, and no product measure is used. The local log-volume is the measure instance logVolume of Comparison.lean; the abstract RegionComparison there is not used here, and the normalization of the local log-volumes (Proposition 3.9 (i), pages 115 and 116) is not modeled.
  2. Placewise containment. Page 174 writes the regions n,◦U and n,◦Ū as collections indexed by (j, v_Q) with j ∈ |𝔽_l| and v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ, and their inclusions componentwise. PlacewiseContainment is that componentwise inclusion over the places. Step (xi-f), page 184, asserts only the global numerical inequality; example_global_not_placewise shows that the global inequality does not imply the placewise one.
  3. Labels and places together. The procession-normalization (average over labels, see LabelAverage.lean) and the sum over places are typed separately. For finitely many labels and places of finite support the two operations commute; that is not stated here.
What is still omitted
  • No construction from initial Θ-data; the comparison is a hypothesis.
  • Finiteness of the global value needs the support condition for the hull family; page 175 argues finiteness from "the [easily verified] compactness" of the 1,◦U_{j,v_Q}, which gives finiteness at each place but not the support condition; not formalized.
  • Tensor packets, the identification of the two sides through the Θ^{×μ}_{LGP}-link and the permutation symmetry (Steps (xi-a), (xi-b), pages 181 and 182), and the determinant and tensor power normalization of Step (xi-d), page 183.
  • The invariance of the global log-volume under multiplication by elements of (M_MOD)_α and its equality with an arithmetic degree (page 117).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PlaceWeightsstructure

structure PlaceWeights (P : Type*) where

A positive real weight at each place (Remark 3.1.1 (ii), (iv), pages 94 and 97; weight 1 at each v_Q in the sum of Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.unitPlaceWeightsdef

def unitPlaceWeights (P : Type*) : PlaceWeights P := ⟨fun _ => 1, fun _ => one_pos⟩

The weights of Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117: the local log-volumes at the various v_Q ∈ 𝕍_ℚ are added, each with weight 1.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.LogVolumeAlmostAllZerodef

def LogVolumeAlmostAllZero (μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v)) (R : ∀ v, Set (X v)) : Prop :=
  {v | logVolume (μ v) (R v) ≠ 0}.Finite

The condition of Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117: the family has zero log-volume at all but finitely many places.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.weightedLocalLogVolumedef

noncomputable def weightedLocalLogVolume (w : PlaceWeights P) (μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v))
    (R : ∀ v, Set (X v)) (v : P) : EReal :=
  ((w.placeWeight v : ℝ) : EReal) * logVolume (μ v) (R v)

The weighted local log-volume w_v · log μ_v (R_v) at a place.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalLogVolumedef

noncomputable def globalLogVolume (w : PlaceWeights P) (μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v))
    (R : ∀ v, Set (X v)) : EReal :=
  ∑ᶠ v, weightedLocalLogVolume w μ R v

The global log-volume ∑_v w_v · log μ_v (R_v) (Proposition 3.9 (iii), page 117), as a finite sum. It is meaningful under LogVolumeAlmostAllZero; otherwise ∑ᶠ returns 0.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.hasFiniteSupport_weightedLocalLogVolumetheorem

theorem hasFiniteSupport_weightedLocalLogVolume (w : PlaceWeights P) {μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v)}
  {R : ∀ v, Set (X v)} (h : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero μ R) :
  Function.HasFiniteSupport (weightedLocalLogVolume w μ R)

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalLogVolume_monotheorem

theorem globalLogVolume_mono (w : PlaceWeights P) (μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v))
  {R R' : ∀ v, Set (X v)} (hR : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero μ R)
  (hR' : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero μ R') (h : ∀ v, R v ⊆ R' v) :
  globalLogVolume w μ R ≤ globalLogVolume w μ R'

Monotonicity. Placewise containment of two families, both of zero log-volume at all but finitely many places, gives the inequality of the global log-volumes. No finiteness is needed.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalLogVolume_ne_bottheorem

theorem globalLogVolume_ne_bot (w : PlaceWeights P) {μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v)}
  {R : ∀ v, Set (X v)} (h : ∀ v, logVolume (μ v) (R v) ≠ ⊥) :
  globalLogVolume w μ R ≠ ⊥

If no local log-volume is −∞, the global log-volume is not −∞.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalLogVolume_ne_toptheorem

theorem globalLogVolume_ne_top (w : PlaceWeights P) {μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v)}
  {R : ∀ v, Set (X v)} (h : ∀ v, logVolume (μ v) (R v) ≠ ⊤) :
  globalLogVolume w μ R ≠ ⊤

If no local log-volume is +∞, the global log-volume is not +∞.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalLogVolume_finitetheorem

theorem globalLogVolume_finite (w : PlaceWeights P) {μ : ∀ v, Measure (X v)}
  {R : ∀ v, Set (X v)} (h0 : ∀ v, μ v (R v) ≠ 0) (htop : ∀ v, μ v (R v) ≠ ⊤) :
  globalLogVolume w μ R ≠ ⊥ ∧ globalLogVolume w μ R ≠ ⊤

Finiteness: if every local log-volume is finite (0 < μ_v (R_v) < ∞ at each place), the global log-volume is a real number.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalQLogVolumedef

noncomputable def globalQLogVolume : EReal :=
  globalLogVolume w (fun v => (S v).μ) fun v => (S v).qPilot

The global log-volume of the q-pilot family: − |log(q)| summed over places.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalThetaLogVolumedef

noncomputable def globalThetaLogVolume : EReal :=
  globalLogVolume w (fun v => (S v).μ) fun v => (S v).hullOfUnion

The global log-volume of the family of hulls of unions: − |log(Θ)| summed over places.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.PlacewiseContainmentdef

def PlacewiseContainment : Prop := ∀ v, (S v).Containment

Region form of the comparison over places: at every place, the q-pilot region lies in the hull of the union of the possible images (componentwise inclusion, page 174). Not proved here; see ComparisonSetting.Containment.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.GlobalLogVolumeComparisondef

def GlobalLogVolumeComparison : Prop := globalQLogVolume S w ≤ globalThetaLogVolume S w

Numerical form of the comparison for the global log-volumes: − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)| (Step (xi-f), page 184). Not proved here.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalLogVolumeComparison_of_placewiseContainmenttheorem

theorem globalLogVolumeComparison_of_placewiseContainment
  (hqs : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero (fun v => (S v).μ) fun v => (S v).qPilot)
  (hΘs : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero (fun v => (S v).μ) fun v => (S v).hullOfUnion)
  (h : PlacewiseContainment S) : GlobalLogVolumeComparison S w

Placewise containment gives the global comparison, provided both families satisfy the support condition of page 117.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.globalPilotLogVolumesdef

noncomputable def globalPilotLogVolumes (hq0 : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).qPilot ≠ 0)
    (hneg : globalQLogVolume S w < 0) (hΘ0 : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).hullOfUnion ≠ 0) :
    PilotLogVolumes :=
  PilotLogVolumes.ofLogVolumes (globalQLogVolume S w) (globalThetaLogVolume S w)
    (globalLogVolume_ne_bot w fun v => (logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr (hq0 v)) hneg
    (globalLogVolume_ne_bot w fun v => (logVolume_eq_bot_iff _ _).not.mpr (hΘ0 v))

The two numbers of Corollary 3.12 from the global log-volumes. Hypotheses: every q-pilot component and every hull component has positive measure (no local term is −∞), and the global q-side log-volume is negative (|log(q)| > 0, page 174).

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_globalPilotLogVolumes_ifftheorem

theorem conclusion_globalPilotLogVolumes_iff (hq0 : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).qPilot ≠ 0)
  (hneg : globalQLogVolume S w < 0) (hΘ0 : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).hullOfUnion ≠ 0) :
  Conclusion (globalPilotLogVolumes S w hq0 hneg hΘ0) ↔
    globalThetaLogVolume S w ≠ ⊤ ∧ GlobalLogVolumeComparison S w

For the global numbers, the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 is finiteness of the global Θ-side log-volume together with GlobalLogVolumeComparison.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.conclusion_of_placewiseContainmenttheorem

theorem conclusion_of_placewiseContainment (hq0 : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).qPilot ≠ 0)
  (hneg : globalQLogVolume S w < 0) (hΘ0 : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).hullOfUnion ≠ 0)
  (hfin : ∀ v, (S v).μ (S v).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤)
  (hqs : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero (fun v => (S v).μ) fun v => (S v).qPilot)
  (hΘs : LogVolumeAlmostAllZero (fun v => (S v).μ) fun v => (S v).hullOfUnion)
  (hcont : PlacewiseContainment S) : Conclusion (globalPilotLogVolumes S w hq0 hneg hΘ0)

Bridge over places (conditional). Placewise containment of the q-pilot regions in the hulls of the unions, finiteness of each hull, and the support condition of page 117 for both families give the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 for the global numbers. Conditional on hcont (Theorem 3.11 and Steps (i) to (xi); the text asserts only the global numerical form, Step (xi-f), page 184), on hfin (page 175, compactness; not formalized) and on hΘs (the support condition for the hulls; not argued on the pages read).

theorem globalLogVolume_bool (μ : Bool → Measure ℝ) (R : Bool → Set ℝ) :
  globalLogVolume (unitPlaceWeights Bool) μ R = ENNReal.log (μ false (R false) * μ true (R true))

With unit weights, the global log-volume on two places is the log of the product of the two measures.

The Θ-side global log-volume is log (1/2 · 1/2) whatever the q-pilot regions.

      (unitPlaceWeights Bool) = ENNReal.log (ENNReal.ofReal a * ENNReal.ofReal b)

    (unitPlaceWeights Bool) < 0

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.TwoPlaceExample.example_global_conclusiontheorem

theorem example_global_conclusion :
  ∃ (hq0 : ∀ v, (settings (qPair (1 / 2) (1 / 2)) v).μ
      (settings (qPair (1 / 2) (1 / 2)) v).qPilot ≠ 0)
    (hneg : globalQLogVolume (settings (qPair (1 / 2) (1 / 2))) (unitPlaceWeights Bool) < 0)
    (hΘ0 : ∀ v, (settings (qPair (1 / 2) (1 / 2)) v).μ
      (settings (qPair (1 / 2) (1 / 2)) v).hullOfUnion ≠ 0),
    Conclusion (globalPilotLogVolumes (settings (qPair (1 / 2) (1 / 2)))
      (unitPlaceWeights Bool) hq0 hneg hΘ0)

Nonvacuity (a). With q-pilot region [0, 1/2] at both places, every hypothesis of conclusion_of_placewiseContainment holds, so the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 holds for the global numbers.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.TwoPlaceExample.example_global_comparison_failstheorem

theorem example_global_comparison_fails :
  ∃ (hq0 : ∀ v, (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)) v).μ
      (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)) v).qPilot ≠ 0)
    (hneg : globalQLogVolume (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4))) (unitPlaceWeights Bool) < 0)
    (hΘ0 : ∀ v, (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)) v).μ
      (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)) v).hullOfUnion ≠ 0),
    (∀ v, (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)) v).μ
      (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)) v).hullOfUnion ≠ ⊤) ∧
    ¬ GlobalLogVolumeComparison (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4))) (unitPlaceWeights Bool) ∧
    ¬ Conclusion (globalPilotLogVolumes (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (3 / 4)))
      (unitPlaceWeights Bool) hq0 hneg hΘ0)

Nonvacuity (b). With q-pilot region [0, 3/4] at both places, every structural hypothesis holds (positive measures, finite hulls, the support condition, negative global q-side log-volume), but the global comparison fails, and so does the conclusion of Corollary 3.12 for the global numbers.

IUT.PaperIII.Corollary312.TwoPlaceExample.example_global_not_placewisetheorem

theorem example_global_not_placewise :
  GlobalLogVolumeComparison (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (1 / 8))) (unitPlaceWeights Bool) ∧
    ¬ (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (1 / 8)) false).LogVolumeComparison ∧
    ¬ PlacewiseContainment (settings (qPair (3 / 4) (1 / 8)))

The global inequality does not imply the placewise one. With q-pilot regions [0, 3/4] at place false and [0, 1/8] at place true, the global comparison holds (3/4 · 1/8 ≤ 1/4) while the placewise comparison fails at place false: the deficit at one place is compensated at the other.

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.HullOperator

IUT/PaperIII/Remark3_9_5/HullOperator.lean

IUT III, Remark 3.9.5 (ii): the order-theoretic characterization of the hull map

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Remark 3.9.5, (i), (ii), pages 126 to 128. Pages 126 to 145 (all of Remark 3.9.5) were read from the extracted text; page 127, which carries the displays of (i) and (ii), was checked against a rendered image.

What the paper says

Remark 3.9.5 (i) defines the holomorphic hull of a suitable subset U of a tensor packet ℐ^ℚ(−) as the smallest subset of the form λ · 𝒪 containing U. Remark 3.9.5 (ii) writes ℙ for the set of direct product regions and ℍ ⊆ ℙ for the set of hulls, and asserts that forming the hull is a map φ : ℙ → ℍ that "may be characterized uniquely" by

  • (P1) φ(H) = H for every H ∈ ℍ;
  • (P2) P ⊆ φ(P) for every P ∈ ℙ;
  • (P3) φ(P₁) ⊆ φ(P₂) whenever P₁ ⊆ P₂ in ℙ.

It then says that, since any intersection of elements of ℍ which is itself of finite log-volume necessarily determines an element of ℍ (the qualifier "of finite log-volume" applies to the intersection, not to the members intersected), it "follows formally" from (P1), (P2), (P3) that φ(P) is the intersection of all H ∈ ℍ containing P.

What this file is and is not

This file is not a formalization of holomorphic hulls or of Remark 3.9.5. Tensor packets, log-shells and direct product regions are not formalized. It checks the order-theoretic part of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) for arbitrary families 𝓗 ⊆ 𝓟 of subsets of an arbitrary type:

  • IsHullMap.eq_sInter: (P1), (P2), (P3), together with ℍ ⊆ ℙ and φ(ℙ) ⊆ ℍ (the two other clauses of IsHullMap, both stated on page 127), force φ(P) = ⋂ {H ∈ 𝓗 | P ⊆ H}. The intersection hypothesis quoted by the paper is not needed for this formula.
  • IsHullMap.unique: hence at most one map satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3); this is the paper's "characterized uniquely".
  • isHullMap_sInter: if for each P ∈ 𝓟 the intersection of the members of 𝓗 containing P lies in 𝓗, then that intersection defines a map satisfying (P1), (P2), (P3). This is the role of the intersection hypothesis: it gives existence, not the formula.

The paper's closure claim (an intersection of hulls that has finite log-volume is a hull) is replaced by the plain hypothesis hcl of isHullMap_sInter: for each P ∈ 𝓟 the intersection of the members of 𝓗 containing P lies in 𝓗. That hypothesis is a statement about the given families only; no log-volume occurs in this file, and the finite-volume condition of the paper is not modeled.

Public declarations
variable {X : Type*}
variable {𝓟 𝓗 : Set (Set X)} {φ : Set X → Set X}

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.IsHullMapstructure

structure IsHullMap (𝓟 𝓗 : Set (Set X)) (φ : Set X → Set X) : Prop where

A map φ on subsets of X satisfies the conditions of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) relative to the families 𝓟 (playing the role of direct product regions) and 𝓗 (playing the role of hulls): it sends 𝓟 into 𝓗, and satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3).

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.IsHullMap.eq_sIntertheorem

theorem eq_sInter (h : IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 φ) {P : Set X} (hP : P ∈ 𝓟) :
  φ P = ⋂₀ {H | H ∈ 𝓗 ∧ P ⊆ H}

The clauses of IsHullMap (ℍ ⊆ ℙ, φ(ℙ) ⊆ ℍ, (P1), (P2), (P3)) force φ(P) = ⋂ {H ∈ ℍ | P ⊆ H}, with no hypothesis on intersections.

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.IsHullMap.uniquetheorem

theorem unique {ψ : Set X → Set X} (hφ : IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 φ) (hψ : IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 ψ)
  {P : Set X} (hP : P ∈ 𝓟) : φ P = ψ P

At most one map satisfies the conditions of Remark 3.9.5 (ii) on ℙ.

IUT.PaperIII.Remark3_9_5.isHullMap_sIntertheorem

theorem isHullMap_sInter {𝓟 𝓗 : Set (Set X)} (hsub : 𝓗 ⊆ 𝓟)
  (hcl : ∀ P ∈ 𝓟, ⋂₀ {H | H ∈ 𝓗 ∧ P ⊆ H} ∈ 𝓗) :
  IsHullMap 𝓟 𝓗 (fun P => ⋂₀ {H | H ∈ 𝓗 ∧ P ⊆ H})

Existence: if ℍ ⊆ ℙ and, for each P ∈ ℙ, the intersection of the members of ℍ containing P lies in ℍ, then P ↦ ⋂ {H ∈ ℍ | P ⊆ H} satisfies (P1), (P2), (P3).

IUT IV

Log-volume computations and set-theoretic foundations.

IUT.PaperIV.ArithmeticDivisor

IUT/PaperIV/ArithmeticDivisor.lean

IUT IV, Definition 1.9 (i): arithmetic divisors on a number field

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Definition 1.9, pages 21 and 22 of the April 2020 manuscript, which recalls notation from [GenEll], §1.

For a number field F, the set 𝕍(F) of valuations is the disjoint union of the nonarchimedean ones and the archimedean ones. An ℝ-arithmetic divisor is a finite formal sum ∑ c_v · v with real coefficients. The degree map sends a nonarchimedean v to log(q_v), with q_v the cardinality of the residue field, and an archimedean v to 1.

Interpretation choices, recorded
  • Nonarchimedean valuations are represented by the nonzero prime ideals of the ring of integers (IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F)), archimedean ones by NumberField.InfinitePlace F. A pair of complex conjugate embeddings is one valuation.
  • The archimedean weight is 1 for real and complex places alike, exactly as in the display of Definition 1.9 (i). Other conventions weight a complex place by 2.
  • The pull-back 𝔞|_K to a finite extension, and the identity deg(𝔞|_K) = deg(𝔞) for the normalized degree, are stated in the paper "in the evident fashion" and are not formalized here. Part (ii) of the definition is not formalized here either.
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Valuationsabbrev

abbrev Valuations : Type _ :=
  IsDedekindDomain.HeightOneSpectrum (𝓞 F) ⊕ InfinitePlace F

The set 𝕍(F) of valuations of F: nonarchimedean ones on the left, archimedean ones on the right.

IUT.PaperIV.ADivabbrev

abbrev ADiv : Type _ := Valuations F →₀ ℝ

The group ADiv_ℝ(F) of ℝ-arithmetic divisors: finite formal real combinations of valuations.

IUT.PaperIV.degWeightdef

noncomputable def degWeight : Valuations F → ℝ
  | .inl v => Real.log (Ideal.absNorm v.asIdeal)
  | .inr _ => 1

The weight of a valuation in the degree map: log(q_v) at a nonarchimedean valuation, where q_v is the cardinality of the residue field, and 1 at an archimedean one.

The support Supp(𝔞): the valuations with nonzero coefficient.

IUT.PaperIV.ADiv.Effectivedef

def ADiv.Effective (a : ADiv F) : Prop := ∀ v, 0 ≤ a v

An arithmetic divisor is effective when all its coefficients are ≥ 0.

IUT.PaperIV.degFdef

noncomputable def degF : ADiv F →+ ℝ :=
  Finsupp.liftAddHom fun v => AddMonoidHom.mulLeft (degWeight v)

The degree map deg_F : ADiv_ℝ(F) → ℝ, a homomorphism of groups.

IUT.PaperIV.degF_singletheorem

  degF (Finsupp.single v c) = degWeight v * c

theorem degF_apply (a : ADiv F) : degF a = a.sum fun v c => degWeight v * c

An effective arithmetic divisor has nonnegative degree.

The normalized degree deg(𝔞) = deg_F(𝔞) / [F : ℚ].

  normalizedDeg (a + b) = normalizedDeg a + normalizedDeg b
