IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.Arithmetic

IUT/PaperIV/Corollary2_2/Arithmetic.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.ElementaryFacts, IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1

IUT IV, Corollary 2.2: the real arithmetic of (C1), (C2) and of the proof

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Corollary 2.2 and its proof, pages 41 to 48 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 42 to 48; every display formalized here was checked against the renders.

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 2.2. The corollary constructs initial Θ-data from a point of a compactly bounded subset of the λ-line and applies Theorem 1.10; that uses the theory of elliptic curves, [GenEll] (Lemma 3.5, Proposition 3.4, Lemma 3.1, Proposition 1.4), [CanLift] and [IUTchI], none of which is formalized. Every arithmetic fact about the curve is a hypothesis here; what is checked is the real arithmetic of the proof:

  • the bound on θ_A (thetaA_chain) and the choice of l in (P1) (P1_upper, P1_lower, P1_l_ge_seven, P1_bound), which is condition (C1);
  • the boundedness arguments behind (P4) and (P5) (P4_bound, P5_bound) and the chain h^{1/2}·log(l) ≤ 16·δ^{1/4}·h^{3/4} (sqrt_mul_log_l);
  • the application of Theorem 1.10 (apply_theorem_1_10), the comparison of log(𝔮^{∤2}) with log(𝔮) (q_not2_step), the constant (15δ)² (fifteen_delta_step) and the assembly of the bound (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε_E/5)·D + (2/5)·ε_E·(1/6)·h + C_K/2 (assembly);
  • the lower bound ε_E ≥ 5·δ·h^{-1/2} (epsE_ge) and the real-power estimate showing that ε < ε_E bounds h (h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsE), with the explicit constant H_unif (h_bound_Hunif);
  • the final step (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε_E)·D + C_K for 0 < ε_E ≤ 1 (final_step), which is the inequality (C2) after D ≤ log-diff_X(x_E) + log-cond_D(x_E) (C2_of_final).

Notation: s = h^{1/2} with h = s² = log(𝔮^∀); L = log(2δh); D = log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}); Q = log(𝔮), Q2 = log(𝔮^{∤2}).

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.thetaA_chaintheorem

theorem thetaA_chain {θ s δ L ξ : ℝ} (hδ : 2 ≤ δ) (hL : 1 ≤ L) (hξ : 0 ≤ ξ) (hs : ξ ≤ s)
  (h : θ ≤ 2 * s + δ * s + 2 * δ * s * L) :
  θ ≤ 4 * δ * s * L ∧ θ ≤ -ξ + 5 * δ * s * L

**The bound on θ_A** (page 45). If θ_A ≤ 2s + δs + 2δsL (the contributions of (S1), (S3), (S2)), δ ≥ 2, L ≥ 1 and 0 ≤ ξ ≤ s, then θ_A ≤ 4δ·s·L ≤ -ξ + 5δ·s·L.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.S1_boundtheorem

theorem S1_bound {ξ s : ℝ} (hs : ξ ≤ s) (hξ : 0 ≤ ξ)
  (hθ : ∀ x : ℝ, ξ ≤ x → Chebyshev.theta x ≤ 4 / 3 * x) : Chebyshev.theta s ≤ 2 * s

The contribution of (S1): with ξ ≤ s and θ(x) ≤ (4/3)·x for x ≥ ξ, θ(s) ≤ 2s.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_uppertheorem

theorem P1_upper {l θ ξ δ s L : ℝ} (hl : l ≤ 2 * (θ + ξ)) (hθ : θ ≤ -ξ + 5 * δ * s * L) :
  l ≤ 10 * δ * s * L

(P1), upper bound. If l ≤ 2(θ_A + ξ) (Proposition 2.1 (ii)) and θ_A ≤ -ξ + 5δ·s·L, then l ≤ 10δ·s·L.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_lowertheorem

theorem P1_lower {A : Finset ℕ} {l : ℕ} {s : ℝ} (hS1 : ∀ p : ℕ, p.Prime → (p : ℝ) ≤ s → p ∈ A)
  (hl : l.Prime) (hlA : l ∉ A) : s < l

(P1), lower bound. If every prime ≤ s lies in A (condition (S1)) and the prime l does not, then s < l.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_l_ge_seventheorem

theorem P1_l_ge_seven {l : ℕ} {s : ℝ} (hl : l.Prime) (hs : 5 ≤ s) (hsl : s < l) : 7 ≤ l

**l ≥ 7.** Theorem 1.10 in its final form requires l ≥ 7. The paper notes on page 22 that l ≠ 5 follows from [IUTchI] Definition 3.1 (c), since the 5-torsion points are rational over F. Independently, since 5 ≤ s < l (with s = h^{1/2}) and l is prime, l ≥ 7.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P1_boundtheorem

theorem P1_bound {δ s : ℝ} (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hs : 1 ≤ s) :
  10 * δ * s * Real.log (2 * δ * s ^ 2) ≤ 20 * δ ^ 2 * (s ^ 2) ^ 2

(P1), parenthetical bound. 10δ·s·log(2δh) ≤ 20·δ²·h² for h = s², s ≥ 1, δ ≥ 1; uses Proposition 2.1 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P4_boundtheorem

theorem P4_bound {l Q T : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (hT : 0 ≤ T)
  (h : (l - 2) / 24 * Q ≤ 2 * Real.log l + T) : Q ≤ 48 * (1 + T)

(P4), boundedness. (l-2)/24·Q ≤ 2·log(l) + T with l ≥ 5 and T ≥ 0 implies Q ≤ 48·(1 + T); the paper uses 1 ≤ 2·log(l) ≤ 48·(l-2)/24.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.sqrt_mul_log_ltheorem

theorem sqrt_mul_log_l {a b l : ℝ} (ha : 1 ≤ a) (hb : 1 ≤ b) (hl0 : 0 < l)
  (hl : l ≤ 20 * (a ^ 4) ^ 2 * (b ^ 4) ^ 2) :
  b ^ 2 * Real.log l ≤ 16 * a * b ^ 3

**The chain h^{1/2}·log(l) ≤ 16·δ^{1/4}·h^{3/4}** (pages 45 and 46), written with δ = a⁴, h = b⁴ (so h^{1/2} = b²). Uses (P1) l ≤ 20δ²h², 20 ≤ 5², 5 ≤ 2⁴ and Proposition 2.1 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.sqrt_mul_log_l_stepstheorem

theorem sqrt_mul_log_l_steps {a b : ℝ} (ha : 1 ≤ a) (hb : 1 ≤ b) :
  Real.log (20 * (a ^ 4) ^ 2 * (b ^ 4) ^ 2) ≤ 2 * Real.log (5 * a ^ 4 * b ^ 4) ∧
    Real.log (5 * a ^ 4 * b ^ 4) ≤ 4 * Real.log (2 * a * b)

The paper's intermediate steps of the same chain: log(20δ²h²) ≤ 2·log(5δh) and log(5δh) ≤ 4·log(2·δ^{1/4}·h^{1/4}), with δ = a⁴, h = b⁴.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.P5_boundtheorem

theorem P5_bound {b B c : ℝ} (hb : 1 ≤ b) (hB : 0 ≤ B) (h : b ^ 4 ≤ B + c * b ^ 3) :
  b ≤ B + c

(P5), boundedness. If h ≤ B + c·h^{3/4} with h ≥ 1 and B, c ≥ 0, then h is bounded; written with h = b⁴: b⁴ ≤ B + c·b³ and b ≥ 1 imply b ≤ B + c.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.apply_theorem_1_10theorem

theorem apply_theorem_1_10 {Q D d dstar l E η δ s L : ℝ} (hs : 0 < s) (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hd : 0 ≤ d) (hdd : 20 * d ≤ dstar) (hdδ : dstar ≤ δ) (hsl : s ≤ l)
  (hl : l ≤ 10 * δ * s * L) (hE : E ≤ dstar * l + η)
  (h : 1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * D + 20 * E) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + δ / s) * D + 200 * δ ^ 2 * s * L + 20 * η

Applying Theorem 1.10 (page 46). From the final inequality of Theorem 1.10 with E ≤ d*_mod·l + η_prm (Remark 1.10.7 (i): e*_mod ≤ d*_mod), 20·d_mod ≤ d*_mod ≤ δ, s ≤ l ≤ 10δ·s·L and D ≥ 0: (1/6)·Q ≤ (1 + δ/s)·D + 200·δ²·s·L + 20·η_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.q_not2_steptheorem

theorem q_not2_step {Q Q2 s l δ h : ℝ} (hs : 0 ≤ s) (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hh : 1 ≤ h)
  (h1 : 1 / 6 * Q2 - 1 / 6 * Q ≤ 1 / 6 * (s * Real.log l))
  (h2 : s * Real.log l ≤ 2 * s * Real.log (5 * δ * h)) :
  1 / 6 * Q2 - 1 / 6 * Q ≤ s * Real.log (2 * δ * h)

**The comparison of log(𝔮^{∤2}) with log(𝔮)** (page 47). Given (1/6)·Q2 - (1/6)·Q ≤ (1/6)·s·log(l) (from (P3)) and s·log(l) ≤ 2·s·log(5δh), with δ, h ≥ 1 and 5 ≤ 2³: (1/6)·Q2 - (1/6)·Q ≤ s·log(2δh).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.fifteen_delta_steptheorem

theorem fifteen_delta_step {δ : ℝ} (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) :
  200 * δ ^ 2 + 1 ≤ (15 * δ) ^ 2 ∧ (15 * δ) ^ 2 ≤ 1 / 6 * (2 / 5) * (60 * δ) ^ 2

**The constant (15δ)²** (page 47): 200·δ² + 1 ≤ (15δ)² ≤ (1/6)·(2/5)·(60δ)² for δ ≥ 1; the second inequality is the paper's "6·5 ≤ 2·4²".

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.assemblytheorem

theorem assembly {Q Q2 D s δ L η B εE : ℝ} (hs : 0 < s) (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hL : 0 ≤ L)
  (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hB : 1 / 6 * s ^ 2 - 1 / 6 * Q2 ≤ B)
  (hq2 : 1 / 6 * Q2 - 1 / 6 * Q ≤ s * L)
  (hthm : 1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + δ / s) * D + 200 * δ ^ 2 * s * L + 20 * η)
  (hεE : εE = (60 * δ) ^ 2 * s⁻¹ * L) (hδε : δ / s ≤ εE / 5) :
  1 / 6 * s ^ 2 ≤ (1 + εE / 5) * D + 2 / 5 * εE * (1 / 6 * s ^ 2) + (40 * η + 2 * B) / 2

**Assembly of the bound on (1/6)·h** (page 47). With (1/6)·h - (1/6)·Q2 ≤ B (the bounded discrepancy B_K of assertion (i)), the two preceding steps, C = 40·η + 2·B, h = s², ε_E = (60δ)²·s⁻¹·L, and δ/s ≤ ε_E/5: (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε_E/5)·D + (2/5)·ε_E·(1/6)·h + C/2.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.epsE_getheorem

theorem epsE_ge {δ s L : ℝ} (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hL : 1 ≤ L) (hs : 0 < s) :
  5 * δ * s⁻¹ ≤ (60 * δ) ^ 2 * s⁻¹ * L

**ε_E ≥ 5·δ·h^{-1/2}** (page 47): (60δ)²·s⁻¹·L ≥ 5δ·s⁻¹ for δ ≥ 1, L ≥ 1, s > 0.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.eps_star_estimatestheorem

theorem eps_star_estimates {εd : ℝ} (h0 : 0 < εd) (h1 : εd ≤ 1) :
  1 / (1 / 2 - εd / 16) ≤ 3 ∧ (2 + εd / 16) / (1 / 2 - εd / 16) ≤ 4 + εd ∧ 4 + εd ≤ 5

The two estimates of page 47 for ε*_d = ε_d/16: 1/(1/2 - ε*_d) = 16/(8 - ε_d) ≤ 3 and (2 + ε*_d)/(1/2 - ε*_d) = (32 + ε_d)/(8 - ε_d) ≤ 4 + ε_d ≤ 5, for 0 < ε_d ≤ 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsEtheorem

theorem h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsE {h δ εd ε : ℝ} (hh : 1 ≤ h) (hδ : 1 ≤ δ) (hεd0 : 0 < εd)
  (hεd1 : εd ≤ 1) (hε0 : 0 < ε) (hε1 : ε ≤ 1)
  (hlt : ε < (60 * δ) ^ 2 * h ^ (-(1 / 2 : ℝ)) * Real.log (2 * δ * h)) :
  h < (ε * (εd / 16)) ^ (-(3 : ℝ)) * (60 * δ) ^ (4 + εd)

The real-power estimate of page 47. Let h ≥ 1, δ ≥ 1, 0 < ε_d ≤ 1, ε*_d = ε_d/16 and 0 < ε ≤ 1. If ε < ε_E = (60δ)²·h^{-1/2}·log(2δh), then h < (ε·ε*_d)^{-3}·(60δ)^{4+ε_d}. For ε = 1 this is the paper's argument for (ii); for general ε it is the argument for (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.h_bound_Huniftheorem

theorem h_bound_Hunif {d εd ε : ℝ} (hd : 1 ≤ d) (hεd0 : 0 < εd) (hεd1 : εd ≤ 1)
  (hε0 : 0 < ε) :
  (ε * (εd / 16)) ^ (-(3 : ℝ)) * (60 * (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * d)) ^ (4 + εd)
    ≤ 16 ^ 3 * (60 * (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5)) ^ 5 * ε ^ (-(3 : ℝ)) * εd ^ (-(3 : ℝ))
      * d ^ (4 + εd)

**The explicit constant H_unif.** With δ = 2¹²·3³·5·d and d ≥ 1, the bound of h_lt_of_eps_lt_epsE is at most H_unif·ε^{-3}·ε_d^{-3}·d^{4+ε_d} with H_unif = 16³·(60·2¹²·3³·5)⁵, a constant independent of K_V, d, ε_d and ε.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.final_steptheorem

theorem final_step {H D C ε : ℝ} (hε0 : 0 < ε) (hε1 : ε ≤ 1) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hC : 0 ≤ C)
  (h : 1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε / 5) * D + 2 / 5 * ε * (1 / 6 * H) + C / 2) :
  1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε) * D + C

The final step (page 48). For 0 < ε ≤ 1, D, C ≥ 0 and (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε/5)·D + (2/5)·ε·(1/6)·h + C/2, one has (1/6)·h ≤ (1 + ε)·D + C, by (1 + ε/5)/(1 - 2ε/5) ≤ 1 + ε and 1 - 2ε/5 ≥ 1/2.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_2.C2_of_finaltheorem

theorem C2_of_final {H dF fF logdiff logcond C ε : ℝ} (hε : 0 ≤ 1 + ε)
  (hdiff : logdiff = dF) (hcond : fF ≤ logcond)
  (h : 1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε) * (dF + fF) + C) :
  1 / 6 * H ≤ (1 + ε) * (logdiff + logcond) + C

(C2) from the final step. With log-diff_X(x_E) = log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) and log(𝔣^{F_tpd}) ≤ log-cond_D(x_E) (stated in Corollary 2.2 (ii)) and 1 + ε ≥ 0, the bound on log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}) becomes the bound of (C2).

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3

IUT/PaperIV/Corollary2_3.lean

IUT IV, Corollary 2.3: the bounded discrepancy logic of the proof

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Corollary 2.3, its proof, and Remark 2.3.1 (ii), pages 54 and 55 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 54 and 55. The bounded discrepancy relations are those of [GenEll], Definition 1.2 (ii), with the correction of Remark 2.3.1 (ii): α ≲ β on F means that there is a constant C with α(x) - β(x) ≤ C for all x ∈ F.

This file is not a formalization of Corollary 2.3. The proof of the corollary has two parts:

  1. By [GenEll], Theorem 2.1 ((ii) implies (i)), it suffices to prove the inequality ht_{ω_X(D)} ≲ (1 + ε)·(log-diff_X + log-cond_D) on K_V ∩ U_X(ℚ̄)^{≤d} for the λ-line X = ℙ¹, D = {0, 1, ∞} and a compactly bounded K_V as in Corollary 2.2. That reduction is an external theorem and is not formalized here.
  2. On K_V ∩ U_X(ℚ̄)^{≤d}, the inequality follows from Corollary 2.2 (i), (ii) (condition (C2)) and (iii). This file checks only the logical form of that step, as an abstract lemma about real functions on an arbitrary set S (cor2_3_on_KV): an equality of bounded discrepancy classes, a bound outside a finite exceptional set, a bound on a further function outside that set and a nonnegativity condition give an inequality of bounded discrepancy classes on all of S.

Nothing here refers to curves, divisors, points of bounded degree, heights, log-differents or log-conductors: these are arbitrary real functions on an arbitrary type, and the lemma holds for every S, including S = ∅. It says nothing about K_V unless the hypotheses are established for the actual functions, which requires Corollary 2.2 and is not done here. Corollary 2.3 itself is not stated in Lean.

def BDle (S : Set α) (f g : α → ℝ) : Prop := ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ x ∈ S, f x - g x ≤ C

f ≲ g on S ([GenEll], Definition 1.2 (ii), as corrected by IUT IV, Remark 2.3.1 (ii)): there is a constant C with f(x) - g(x) ≤ C for every x ∈ S.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDeqdef

def BDeq (S : Set α) (f g : α → ℝ) : Prop := ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ x ∈ S, |f x - g x| ≤ C

f ≈ g on S: there is a constant C with |f(x) - g(x)| ≤ C for every x ∈ S.

  BDle S f k

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDle_of_finite_exceptionstheorem

theorem BDle_of_finite_exceptions {S : Set α} (Exc : Finset α) {f g : α → ℝ} (C : ℝ)
  (h : ∀ x ∈ S, x ∉ Exc → f x - g x ≤ C) : BDle S f g

A bound that holds outside a finite exceptional set gives an inequality of bounded discrepancy classes on the whole set.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.cor2_3_on_KVtheorem

theorem cor2_3_on_KV {S : Set α} (Exc : Finset α) {ht H Λ εE : α → ℝ} {ε CK : ℝ}
  (hi : BDeq S ht (fun x => 1 / 6 * H x))
  (hC2 : ∀ x ∈ S, x ∉ Exc → 1 / 6 * H x ≤ (1 + εE x) * Λ x + CK)
  (hiii : ∀ x ∈ S, x ∉ Exc → εE x ≤ ε)
  (hΛ : ∀ x ∈ S, 0 ≤ Λ x) :
  BDle S ht (fun x => (1 + ε) * Λ x)

**Abstract form of the step of the proof of Corollary 2.3 on K_V ∩ U_X(ℚ̄)^{≤d}** (page 55). This is a lemma about arbitrary real functions on an arbitrary set S; it holds for every S, so it carries information about K_V only once its hypotheses are proved for the actual functions. In the intended application S is the set of points, ht the height ht_{ω_X(D)}, H the function log(𝔮^∀), Λ the function log-diff_X + log-cond_D, and εE the invariant ε_E of Corollary 2.2 (ii). Assume: * (Corollary 2.2 (i)) ht ≈ (1/6)·H on S; * (Corollary 2.2 (ii), (C2)) (1/6)·H(x) ≤ (1 + ε_E(x))·Λ(x) + C_K for x ∈ S outside a finite set Exc; * (Corollary 2.2 (iii)) ε_E(x) ≤ ε for x ∈ S outside Exc; * Λ ≥ 0 on S (log-differents and log-conductors are degrees of effective divisors). Then ht ≲ (1 + ε)·Λ on S.

IUT.PaperIV.Corollary2_3.BDle_mono_epstheorem

theorem BDle_mono_eps {S : Set α} {ht Λ : α → ℝ} {ε ε' : ℝ} (hεε' : ε ≤ ε')
  (hΛ : ∀ x ∈ S, 0 ≤ Λ x) (h : BDle S ht (fun x => (1 + ε) * Λ x)) :
  BDle S ht (fun x => (1 + ε') * Λ x)

Corollary 2.2 (iii) is stated for ε ≤ 1. For ε > 1 the inequality follows from the case ε = 1, since Λ ≥ 0; this monotonicity is the following statement.

IUT.PaperIV.Definition1_9

IUT/PaperIV/Definition1_9.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.ArithmeticDivisor

IUT IV, Definition 1.9 (ii): the degree deg_E of an arithmetic divisor relative to a set

Module documentation

of valuations

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Definition 1.9, pages 21 and 22 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 21 and 22 (with a magnified crop of the display of (ii)).

Definition 1.9 (ii) of the paper: for a nonempty set E ⊆ 𝕍(F) of valuations lying over a single valuation v_ℚ of ℚ, and an arithmetic divisor 𝔞 on F, write 𝔞_E for the portion of 𝔞 supported in E and set

deg_E(𝔞) = deg(𝔞_E) / ∑_{v ∈ E} [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}].

The paper then states that deg_{E|K}(𝔞|_K) = deg_E(𝔞) for a finite extension K of F, where E|K is the set of valuations of K over E; part (i) states the analogous invariance deg(𝔞|_K) = deg(𝔞) of the normalized degree.

Interpretation of the numerator. In the render of page 22 the line under deg(𝔞_E) is the fraction bar, so the numerator is the degree deg_F of Definition 1.9 (i), not the normalized degree; this file uses degF in the numerator.

Domain. degE is defined only on PlaceSet F, the finite sets E that are nonempty and whose elements all lie over one place of ℚ (LiesOverOnePlace). Interpretation choice: the place of ℚ below a valuation is encoded by placeBelow as some p, with p the residue characteristic ringChar (𝓞 F ⧸ 𝔭), for a finite place 𝔭, and as none for an archimedean place.

What is formalized: the domain (placeBelow, LiesOverOnePlace, PlaceSet), the definition (degE), the local degree [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}] (localDeg, as e_v·f_v at finite places and as InfinitePlace.mult at infinite places), basic properties (degE_add, degE_nonneg), the identification deg_E(𝔞) = deg(𝔞_E) (normalized degree) under the hypothesis that the local degrees over E sum to [F : ℚ] (degE_eq_normalizedDeg), and its unconditional instance for the set of all archimedean valuations (degE_arc_eq_normalizedDeg).

What is not formalized: the pull-back 𝔞|_K of arithmetic divisors along a finite extension K/F, the set E|K, and both invariance statements (deg(𝔞|_K) = deg(𝔞) of (i) and deg_{E|K}(𝔞|_K) = deg_E(𝔞) of (ii)); the fact that the local degrees over all valuations above one finite v_ℚ sum to [F : ℚ] is also not proved here.

IUT.PaperIV.localDegdef

noncomputable def localDeg : Valuations F → ℕ
  | .inl v => v.asIdeal.ramificationIdx ℤ * v.asIdeal.inertiaDeg ℤ
  | .inr w => w.mult

The local degree [F_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}]: e_v·f_v at a finite place (ramification index times inertia degree of the prime of 𝓞 F over ℤ), and 1 or 2 at an infinite place.

IUT.PaperIV.placeBelowdef

noncomputable def placeBelow : Valuations F → Option ℕ
  | .inl v => some (ringChar (𝓞 F ⧸ v.asIdeal))
  | .inr _ => none

The place of ℚ below a valuation of F: some p for a finite place 𝔭, where p is the residue characteristic of 𝔭, and none for an archimedean place.

IUT.PaperIV.LiesOverOnePlacedef

def LiesOverOnePlace (E : Finset (Valuations F)) : Prop :=
  E.Nonempty ∧ ∃ vQ : Option ℕ, ∀ v ∈ E, placeBelow v = vQ

The domain condition of Definition 1.9 (ii): E is nonempty and all its elements lie over one place v_ℚ of ℚ.

If the local degrees over E sum to [F : ℚ], then deg_E(𝔞) is the normalized degree of 𝔞_E. The hypothesis holds when E is the set of all valuations over one v_ℚ; this file proves that only for the archimedean place (sum_localDeg_arc).

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.Different

IUT/PaperIV/LocalFields/Different.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.3 (i): the lower bound for the order of the different

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Propositions 1.1 to 1.3, pages 9 to 12 of the April 2020 manuscript. Pages read for this file: 9 to 15. Pages checked against rendered images: 9, 10, 11 and 12 (the definition of 𝔡_i and the normalization of ord on page 9; the statement of Proposition 1.3 (i) on pages 11 and 12).

The statement of the paper

Page 9: ord is the p-adic valuation on \bar ℚ_p with ord(p) = 1; R_i is the ring of integers of a finite extension k_i of ℚ_p and 𝔡_i ∈ ℚ_{≥ 0} is the order ord of any generator of the different ideal of R_i over ℤ_p. Page 10: e_i is the ramification index of k_i over ℚ_p. Page 11: for a subfield ℚ_p ⊆ k_0 ⊆ k_i with ring of integers R_0, different order 𝔡_0 and ramification index e_0, and e_{i/0} = e_i/e_0, Proposition 1.3 (i) asserts 𝔡_i ≥ 𝔡_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/e_i, with equality when k_i is tamely ramified over k_0 (page 12).

What is formalized, and the normalizations chosen

The setting is that of Mathlib's different ideal: a Dedekind domain A with fraction field K, a finite separable extension L/K, and the integral closure B of A in L; p is a nonzero maximal ideal of A and P a prime of B over p. With A = ℤ_p, K = ℚ_p, L = k_i this is the paper's setting (see IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.Padic).

  • differentExponent A B P is the multiplicity of P in the different ideal 𝔇_{B/A} (Mathlib's differentIdeal), a natural number.
  • ramificationIdx is Mathlib's Ideal.ramificationIdx P A, the ramification index e of P over p.
  • differentOrder A B P = differentExponent A B P / e, a real number. This is the paper's normalization: if p is generated by an element ϖ with ord(ϖ) = 1 (for A = ℤ_p, the element p), then p·B_P = P^e·B_P, so a uniformizer of B_P has ord = 1/e, and a generator of 𝔇_{B/A}·B_P has ord = differentExponent/e. The paper writes ord of a generator of the different of R_i, which presupposes that this ideal is principal; it is when R_i is local, which holds for the ring of integers of a finite extension of ℚ_p. That locality is not proved here, so every statement below is made for every prime P over p; when P is the only one, differentOrder is the paper's 𝔡_i.

Proved, from Mathlib's pow_sub_one_dvd_differentIdeal_aux (P^{e-1} divides the different) and the transitivity of the different (differentIdeal_eq_differentIdeal_mul_differentIdeal):

  • ramificationIdx_sub_one_le_differentExponent: e - 1 ≤ differentExponent;
  • differentOrder_ge: (e - 1)/e ≤ differentOrder, i.e. Proposition 1.3 (i) for k_0 = ℚ_p (where 𝔡_0 = 0, e_0 = 1);
  • differentOrder_ge_tower: for a tower A ⊆ B ⊆ C of such rings (the paper's ℤ_p ⊆ R_0 ⊆ R_i) and primes P ⊆ C over P₀ ⊆ B over p, differentOrder A C P ≥ differentOrder A B P₀ + (e_{P/P₀} - 1)/e_{P/p}, which is the inequality of Proposition 1.3 (i) with e_{i/0} = e_{P/P₀} and e_i = e_{P/p} (e_{P/p} = e_{P₀/p}·e_{P/P₀} by Ideal.ramificationIdx_tower).

Not formalized: the equality in the tamely ramified case (Mathlib, at the pinned version, has no statement computing the exact exponent of the different in the tame case; only the bound and the characterization P ∣ 𝔇 ↔ P ramified, dvd_differentIdeal_iff, are available), Proposition 1.3 (ii), and the uniqueness of P over p for ℤ_p.

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentExponentdef

noncomputable def differentExponent (A B : Type*) [CommRing A] [CommRing B] [Algebra A B]
    [IsDomain A] [IsIntegrallyClosed A] [IsDedekindDomain B] [Module.IsTorsionFree A B]
    (P : Ideal B) : ℕ :=
  multiplicity P (differentIdeal A B)

The exponent of the prime P in the different ideal of B over A: the multiplicity of P in differentIdeal A B (with Mathlib's convention that multiplicity is 0 when it is infinite, which does not occur for a nonzero prime and the nonzero different).

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentOrderdef

noncomputable def differentOrder (A B : Type*) [CommRing A] [CommRing B] [Algebra A B]
    [IsDomain A] [IsIntegrallyClosed A] [IsDedekindDomain B] [Module.IsTorsionFree A B]
    (P : Ideal B) : ℝ :=
  (differentExponent A B P : ℝ) / (P.ramificationIdx A : ℝ)

The paper's order 𝔡 of the different at P, normalized so that ord(ϖ) = 1 for a generator ϖ of the prime of A below P: the exponent of P in the different divided by the ramification index of P over A.

theorem ramificationIdx_pos_of_liesOver {A B : Type*} [CommRing A] [CommRing B] [Algebra A B]

  (hp : p ≠ ⊥) (P : Ideal B) [P.IsPrime] [P.LiesOver p] : 0 < P.ramificationIdx A

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.ramificationIdx_sub_one_le_differentExponenttheorem

theorem ramificationIdx_sub_one_le_differentExponent {p : Ideal A} [p.IsMaximal] (hp : p ≠ ⊥)

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentOrder_getheorem

theorem differentOrder_ge {p : Ideal A} [p.IsMaximal] (hp : p ≠ ⊥) (P : Ideal B) [P.IsPrime]

**Proposition 1.3 (i) for k_0 = ℚ_p** in the setting of Dedekind domains: (e - 1)/e ≤ 𝔡, where e is the ramification index of P over p and 𝔡 = differentOrder.

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentIdeal_tower_eqtheorem

theorem differentIdeal_tower_eq :
  differentIdeal A C = differentIdeal B C * (differentIdeal A B).map (algebraMap B C)

Transitivity of the different in the tower: 𝔇_{C/A} = 𝔇_{C/B}·𝔇_{B/A}C. This is Mathlib's differentIdeal_eq_differentIdeal_mul_differentIdeal, whose separability hypothesis on the fraction rings is derived here from the separability of M/K.

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentExponent_towertheorem

theorem differentExponent_tower {p : Ideal A} [p.IsMaximal] (hp : p ≠ ⊥) (P₀ : Ideal B)
  [P₀.IsPrime] [P₀.LiesOver p] (P : Ideal C) [P.IsPrime] [P.LiesOver P₀] :
  differentExponent A C P =
    differentExponent B C P + P.ramificationIdx B * differentExponent A B P₀

The exponents of the different in the tower: for primes P ⊆ C over P₀ ⊆ B over p ⊆ A, differentExponent A C P = differentExponent B C P + e_{P/P₀}·differentExponent A B P₀.

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.differentOrder_ge_towertheorem

theorem differentOrder_ge_tower {p : Ideal A} [p.IsMaximal] (hp : p ≠ ⊥) (P₀ : Ideal B)
  [P₀.IsPrime] [P₀.LiesOver p] (P : Ideal C) [P.IsPrime] [P.LiesOver P₀] :
  differentOrder A B P₀ + ((P.ramificationIdx B : ℝ) - 1) / P.ramificationIdx A ≤
    differentOrder A C P

Proposition 1.3 (i), the inequality (pages 11 and 12), in the setting of Dedekind domains: for primes P ⊆ C over P₀ ⊆ B over the nonzero maximal ideal p ⊆ A, 𝔡_i ≥ 𝔡_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/e_i, with 𝔡_i = differentOrder A C P, 𝔡_0 = differentOrder A B P₀, e_{i/0} = e_{P/P₀} and e_i = e_{P/p}.

IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.Padic

IUT/PaperIV/LocalFields/Padic.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.Different, IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4

IUT IV, Propositions 1.2 to 1.4: the different of a finite extension of ℚ_p

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Propositions 1.1 to 1.4, pages 9 to 14 of the April 2020 manuscript. Pages read for this file: 9 to 15. Pages checked against rendered images: 9 to 14 (the normalization ord(p) = 1 and the definition of 𝔡_i on page 9, the definitions of e_i, a_i, b_i on page 10, Proposition 1.3 (i) on pages 11 and 12, the inequalities 𝔡_I + a_I ≥ |I|, 𝔡_I + a_I ≥ 2|I| of Proposition 1.4 (iii) on page 13 with their proof on page 14).

This file specializes IUT.PaperIV.LocalFields.Different to the paper's objects: A = ℤ_p, K = ℚ_p, L = k_i a finite extension of ℚ_p, and R_i = integralClosure ℤ_p k_i its ring of integers. Here p is a prime number, ℤ_[p], ℚ_[p] are Mathlib's p-adic integers and numbers, and k_i is any field with a finite-dimensional ℚ_[p]-algebra structure (and the compatible ℤ_[p]-algebra structure). The ramification index e_i is that of a prime P of R_i over the maximal ideal pℤ_p, and 𝔡_i = differentOrder ℤ_[p] R_i P (exponent of P in the different divided by e_i); since p generates the maximal ideal of ℤ_p, this is the paper's normalization ord(p) = 1. The ring R_i has only one such prime P, but that is not proved here, so the statements hold for every prime P over p.

What is proved:

  • isDedekindDomain_ringOfIntegers_padic, isTorsionFree_ringOfIntegers_padic: R_i is a Dedekind domain, torsion free over ℤ_p (so the general results apply; this also shows that their hypotheses are satisfiable);
  • padic_differentOrder_ge: Proposition 1.3 (i) with k_0 = ℚ_p, i.e. 𝔡_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i;
  • padic_differentOrder_add_a_ge: 𝔡_i + a_i ≥ 1 if p > 2 and 𝔡_i + a_i ≥ 2 if p = 2, with a_i = Proposition1_2.a p e_i the constant of page 10 evaluated at the actual p and e_i (the inequality Proposition1_2.d_add_a_ge with its hypothesis discharged);
  • padic_dI_add_aI_ge: for finitely many such extensions k_i (i ∈ I), the last sentence of Proposition 1.4 (iii), 𝔡_I + a_I ≥ |I| if p > 2 and 𝔡_I + a_I ≥ 2|I| if p = 2, with 𝔡_I = ∑ 𝔡_i and a_I = ∑ a_i (Proposition1_4.dI_add_aI_ge with its hypothesis discharged).

Not formalized here: the inclusions of Propositions 1.1 and 1.2 (tensor products of the R_i, their normalization, p-adic logarithms), the tame equality of Proposition 1.3 (i), Proposition 1.3 (ii), and the log-volumes of Proposition 1.4.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_2.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.ElementaryFacts

IUT IV, Proposition 1.2: the constants a_i, b_i (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.2 and its proof, and the uses of a_i, b_i in the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii), pages 10, 11, 13 and 14 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 10 to 14. The definitions of a_i, b_i were checked against the render of page 10, which shows a ceiling in a_i and a floor in b_i (both lost in the text extraction).

The paper defines, for a finite extension k_i/ℚ_p with ramification index e_i,

  • a_i = (1/e_i)·⌈e_i/(p-2)⌉ if p > 2, and a_i = 2 if p = 2;
  • b_i = ⌊log(p·e_i/(p-1))/log(p)⌋ - 1/e_i.

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.2. The inclusions of (i) to (iv) concern p-adic logarithms of unit groups and tensor products of rings of integers (they rest on [Kobl], p. 81, and Proposition 1.1), which need local field theory not formalized here. This file checks every elementary statement about a_i and b_i that the proposition and its proof, and the proof of Proposition 1.4 (iii), use:

  • p > 2, e_i ≤ p - 2 imply a_i = 1/e_i = -b_i (a_b_of_le), and e_i = 1, p > 2 imply a_i = 1 = -b_i (a_b_of_e_one, used in (iv));
  • a_i > 1/(p-1) and p^{b_i + 1/e_i}/e_i > 1/(p-1) (a_gt, rpow_b_gt), the inputs to (i);
  • the floor estimate used in (iii) (iii_floor);
  • a_i - 1/e_i ≤ 4/p ≤ 2/log(p) and (b_i + 1/e_i)·log(p) ≤ log(2e_i) ≤ 1 + log(e_i) (a_sub_le, b_add_le), used in Proposition 1.4 (iii);
  • a_i ≥ 1/e_i for p > 2, hence d_i + a_i ≥ 1 when d_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i, and d_i + a_i ≥ 2 for p = 2 when d_i ≥ 0 (d_add_a_ge), used in Proposition 1.4 (iii).

Hypotheses: p is a natural number ≥ 2 (the primality of p is not needed) and e ≥ 1.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.adef

noncomputable def a (p e : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if p = 2 then 2 else (1 / (e : ℝ)) * ⌈(e : ℝ) / ((p : ℝ) - 2)⌉

a_i of Proposition 1.2, as a function of p and e = e_i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.bdef

noncomputable def b (p e : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ⌊Real.log ((p : ℝ) * e / ((p : ℝ) - 1)) / Real.log p⌋ - 1 / (e : ℝ)

b_i of Proposition 1.2, as a function of p and e = e_i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.b_add_invtheorem

  b p e + 1 / (e : ℝ) = ⌊Real.log ((p : ℝ) * e / ((p : ℝ) - 1)) / Real.log p⌋

b_i + 1/e_i = ⌊log_p(p·e_i/(p-1))⌋.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.a_b_of_letheorem

  a p e = 1 / (e : ℝ) ∧ b p e = -(1 / (e : ℝ))

If p > 2 and 1 ≤ e ≤ p - 2, then a_i = 1/e_i = -b_i.

Proposition 1.2 (iv) uses: if p > 2 and e_i = 1, then a_i = 1 and b_i = -1.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.rpow_b_gttheorem

p^{b_i + 1/e_i}/e_i > 1/(p-1), the second input to Proposition 1.2 (i).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.iii_floortheorem

theorem iii_floor (y a b : ℝ) (hab : a = -b) : y - 1 ≤ (⌊y - a⌋ : ℝ) - b

The estimate in the proof of Proposition 1.2 (iii): if a = -b, then for every real y, ⌊y - a⌋ - b ≥ y - 1. With y = λ - d_I this is ⌊λ - d_I - a_I⌋ - b_I ≥ λ - d_I - 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2.d_add_a_getheorem

d_i + a_i ≥ 1 when p > 2 and d_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i (Proposition 1.3 (i) with k_0 = ℚ_p); d_i + a_i ≥ 2 when p = 2 and d_i ≥ 0. Used in Proposition 1.4 (iii).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_3.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.3: estimates of differents (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.3 and its proof, pages 11 to 13 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 11, 12 and 13; the displays formalized here were checked against the renders.

Statement of the paper (with d_i, d_0 the orders of generators of the differents of k_i, k_0 over ℚ_p, e_0 the ramification index of k_0, e_{i/0} = e_i/e_0, n_i the p-adic valuation of [k_i : k_0]):

  • (i) d_i ≥ d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/(e_{i/0}·e_0) = d_0 + (e_{i/0} - 1)/e_i, with equality when k_i/k_0 is tamely ramified;
  • (ii) if k_i is Galois over some k_1 ⊇ k_0 with k_1/k_0 tame, then d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0.

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.3. The proof computes differents through Eisenstein and Kummer polynomials over local rings, which needs local field theory not formalized here. This file checks the arithmetic of the proof:

  • the minimum in the display of (i) (i_min);
  • the base case n_i = 0 of the induction in (ii), i.e. that (i) with equality gives d_i ≤ d_0 + 1/e_0 (ii_base);
  • the chain of the inductive step of (ii) (ii_step), and the passage "it suffices to verify d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 + ε for every ε > 0" (ii_of_forall_eps).
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.i_mintheorem

theorem i_min {e' e0 : ℝ} (he' : 1 ≤ e') (he0 : 1 ≤ e0) :
  min (1 / e0) ((e' - 1) / (e' * e0)) = (e' - 1) / (e' * e0)

The minimum in the proof of (i): for integers e' = e_{i/0} ≥ 1, e_0 ≥ 1, (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0) ≤ 1/e_0, so min(1/e_0, (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0)) = (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0), and this equals (e' - 1)/e_i with e_i = e'·e_0.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.ii_basetheorem

theorem ii_base {di d0 e' e0 : ℝ} (he' : 1 ≤ e') (he0 : 1 ≤ e0)
  (h : di = d0 + (e' - 1) / (e' * e0)) : di ≤ d0 + 1 / e0

The base case of the induction in (ii): if d_i = d_0 + (e' - 1)/(e'·e_0) (tame case of (i)), then d_i ≤ d_0 + 1/e_0.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.ii_steptheorem

theorem ii_step {di dstar d0 n e0 p o estar ordpw ε : ℝ} (hp : 1 ≤ p) (hε : 0 < ε)
  (hestar : p / ε ≤ estar)
  (h1 : di ≤ ordpw + dstar) (hord : ordpw = 1 + (p - 1) * o)
  (hind : dstar ≤ d0 + n - 1 + 1 / e0)
  (ho : o ≤ (p - 1) / (p * estar)) :
  di ≤ d0 + n + 1 / e0 + ε

The inductive step of (ii) (page 12). Inputs: * d_i ≤ ord(p·ϖ_i^{p-1}) + d_* (different of the Kummer extension); * ord(p·ϖ_i^{p-1}) = 1 + (p-1)·ord(ϖ_i); * the induction hypothesis d_* ≤ d_0 + n_i - 1 + 1/e_0; * ord(ϖ_i) ≤ (p-1)/(p·e_*) (the paper also states 0 ≤ ord(ϖ_i), which is not needed), and e_* ≥ p/ε. Conclusion: d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 + ε, via (p-1)·ord(ϖ_i) ≤ (p-1)²/(p·e_*) ≤ p/e_* ≤ ε.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_3.ii_of_forall_epstheorem

theorem ii_of_forall_eps {di X : ℝ} (h : ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → di ≤ X + ε) : di ≤ X

"It suffices to verify d_i ≤ d_0 + n_i + 1/e_0 + ε for every positive ε."

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_4.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_2

IUT IV, Proposition 1.4: nonarchimedean normalized log-volume estimates (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.4 and its proof, pages 13 and 14 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages 13 and 14; the displays formalized here were checked against the renders.

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.4. The log-volume μ^log of [AbsTopIII], Proposition 5.7, on compact open subsets of p-adic fields and their tensor products, is not formalized. Its properties used in the proof enter here as real-number hypotheses:

  • μ^log(p^k·M) = μ^log(M) - k·log(p) (normalization of (i));
  • μ^log(log_p(R_I^×)) = ∑_{i ∈ I} μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) + μ^log(R_I) (the paper's display);
  • μ^log(R_I) ≤ 0 and μ^log((R_I)^∼) = 0 ((i) together with Proposition 1.1).

What is checked:

  • the computation of (ii): (1/(e·f))·(log(1 - p^{-f}) - log(p^m·(p^f - 1))) = -(1/e + m/(e·f))·log(p) (ii_identity), so μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) ≤ -(1/e_i)·log(p) (ii_le);
  • the two summation chains of (iii) (iii_first, iii_second), with the per-index estimates of Proposition 1.2 (Proposition1_2.a_sub_le, Proposition1_2.b_add_le) and the fact that a_i = 1/e_i = -b_i for i ∉ I*;
  • the lower bounds d_I + a_I ≥ |I| (p > 2) and d_I + a_I ≥ 2|I| (p = 2) (dI_add_aI_ge).

In the first chain the paper writes p^{λ - d_I - a_I}; the render shows a floor, which is the source of the +1. Here the floor step is a hypothesis hfloor of the form μ ≤ (-λ + d_I + a_I + 1)·log(p) + μ^log(log_p(R_I^×)).

Public declarations
variable {ι : Type*} [DecidableEq ι]

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.ii_identitytheorem

theorem ii_identity {p e : ℝ} {f m : ℕ} (hp : 1 < p) (he : 0 < e) (hf : 1 ≤ f) :
  1 / (e * f) * (Real.log (1 - (p ^ f)⁻¹) - Real.log (p ^ m * (p ^ f - 1)))
    = -(1 / e + m / (e * f)) * Real.log p

Proposition 1.4 (ii), the computation. For p > 1, e, f > 0 and m ≥ 0: (1/(e·f))·(log(1 - p^{-f}) - log(p^m·(p^f - 1))) = -(1/e + m/(e·f))·log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.ii_letheorem

theorem ii_le {p e mu : ℝ} {m f : ℕ} (hp : 1 < p) (he : 0 < e) (hf : 1 ≤ f)
  (hmu : mu = -(1 / e + m / (e * f)) * Real.log p) : mu ≤ -(1 / e) * Real.log p

Consequence used in (iii): μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) ≤ -(1/e_i)·log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.iii_firsttheorem

theorem iii_first {p : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (I Istar : Finset ι) (hsub : Istar ⊆ I) (e : ι → ℕ)
  (he : ∀ i, 1 ≤ e i) (hout : ∀ i ∈ I, i ∉ Istar → e i ≤ p - 2)
  {lam dI mu muM muR : ℝ} (mui : ι → ℝ)
  (hfloor : mu ≤ (-lam + dI + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i) + 1) * Real.log p + muM)
  (hsum : muM = ∑ i ∈ I, mui i + muR)
  (hmui : ∀ i ∈ I, mui i ≤ -(1 / (e i : ℝ)) * Real.log p) (hR : muR ≤ 0) :
  mu ≤ (-lam + dI + 1 + 4 * Istar.card / p) * Real.log p

Proposition 1.4 (iii), first chain. Let p ≥ 2, e : ι → ℕ with e_i ≥ 1, and I* ⊆ I such that e_i ≤ p - 2 for i ∈ I \ I*. Assume the floor step hfloor, the decomposition hsum, μ^log(log_p(R_i^×)) ≤ -(1/e_i)·log(p) and μ^log(R_I) ≤ 0. Then μ^log(p^{⌊λ - d_I - a_I⌋}·log_p(R_I^×)) ≤ (-λ + d_I + 1 + 4·|I*|/p)·log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.a_add_b_letheorem

theorem a_add_b_le {p e : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (he : 1 ≤ e) :
  (a p e + b p e) * Real.log p ≤ 3 + Real.log e

Per-index estimate of (iii): (a_i + b_i)·log(p) ≤ 3 + log(e_i).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.iii_secondtheorem

theorem iii_second {p : ℕ} (hp : 2 ≤ p) (I Istar : Finset ι) (hsub : Istar ⊆ I) (e : ι → ℕ)
  (he : ∀ i, 1 ≤ e i) (hout : ∀ i ∈ I, i ∉ Istar → e i ≤ p - 2) {lam dI mu : ℝ}
  (h : mu ≤ (-lam + dI + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i) + ∑ i ∈ I, b p (e i) + 1) * Real.log p) :
  mu ≤ (-lam + dI + 1) * Real.log p + ∑ i ∈ Istar, (3 + Real.log (e i))

Proposition 1.4 (iii), second chain. With the same I* and the hypothesis μ ≤ (-λ + d_I + a_I + b_I + 1)·log(p) (from μ^log((R_I)^∼) = 0 and the floor step): μ^log(p^{⌊λ - d_I - a_I⌋ - b_I}·(R_I)^∼) ≤ (-λ + d_I + 1)·log(p) + ∑_{i ∈ I*} {3 + log(e_i)}.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_4.dI_add_aI_getheorem

theorem dI_add_aI_ge {p : ℕ} (I : Finset ι) (e : ι → ℕ) (he : ∀ i, 1 ≤ e i) (d : ι → ℝ) :
  (2 < p → (∀ i ∈ I, ((e i : ℝ) - 1) / e i ≤ d i) →
      (I.card : ℝ) ≤ ∑ i ∈ I, d i + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i)) ∧
    (p = 2 → (∀ i ∈ I, 0 ≤ d i) →
      2 * (I.card : ℝ) ≤ ∑ i ∈ I, d i + ∑ i ∈ I, a p (e i))

**d_I + a_I ≥ |I| for p > 2, d_I + a_I ≥ 2|I| for p = 2.** Here d_I = ∑ d_i with d_i ≥ (e_i - 1)/e_i (Proposition 1.3 (i) with k_0 = ℚ_p) when p > 2, and d_i ≥ 0 when p = 2.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_5.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.5: archimedean metric estimates (elementary part)

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.5, pages 14 to 16 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered page 15; the statements of (i) and (iii) were checked against the render.

Statement (i) of the paper: the isomorphism of topological rings ℂ ⊗_ℝ ℂ ≅ ℂ ⊕ ℂ is compatible with the tensor product metric on the left and the direct sum metric on the right up to a factor of 2 (the metric on the right is 2 times the metric on the left; lengths differ by √2).

This file is not a formalization of Proposition 1.5. Tensor products of metrized real vector spaces, the primitive automorphisms of (ii), the integral structures B_I of (iii) and the inclusion of (iv) are not formalized. This file checks:

  • (i) in coordinates: writing an element of ℂ ⊗_ℝ ℂ as x₁₁·(1⊗1) + x_{i1}·(i⊗1) + x_{1i}·(1⊗i) + x_{ii}·(i⊗i) (an orthonormal basis for the tensor product metric), the isomorphism a⊗b ↦ (a·b, a·b̄) (crt, crt_pure) multiplies squared lengths by exactly 2 (crt_normSq);
  • the count in (iii): since dim_ℝ M_I = (2|V|)^{|I|} and each copy of ℂ has real dimension 2, the number of copies is 2^{|I|-1}·|V|^{|I|} (count_iii).
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.crtdef

noncomputable def crt (x11 xi1 x1i xii : ℝ) : ℂ × ℂ :=
  (⟨x11 - xii, xi1 + x1i⟩, ⟨x11 + xii, xi1 - x1i⟩)

The isomorphism ℂ ⊗_ℝ ℂ → ℂ ⊕ ℂ, a⊗b ↦ (a·b, a·b̄), in the coordinates (x₁₁, x_{i1}, x_{1i}, x_{ii}) with respect to the basis 1⊗1, i⊗1, 1⊗i, i⊗i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.crt_puretheorem

theorem crt_pure (a b : ℂ) :
  crt (a.re * b.re) (a.im * b.re) (a.re * b.im) (a.im * b.im) = (a * b, a * (starRingEnd ℂ) b)

On a pure tensor a ⊗ b, whose coordinates are (a₁b₁, a₂b₁, a₁b₂, a₂b₂) for a = a₁ + a₂·i, b = b₁ + b₂·i, the map crt is (a·b, a·b̄).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.crt_normSqtheorem

theorem crt_normSq (x11 xi1 x1i xii : ℝ) :
  Complex.normSq (crt x11 xi1 x1i xii).1 + Complex.normSq (crt x11 xi1 x1i xii).2
    = 2 * (x11 ^ 2 + xi1 ^ 2 + x1i ^ 2 + xii ^ 2)

Proposition 1.5 (i) in coordinates: the direct sum squared length of crt x is 2 times the tensor product squared length x₁₁² + x_{i1}² + x_{1i}² + x_{ii}².

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_5.count_iiitheorem

theorem count_iii (n v : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) : (2 * v) ^ n = 2 * (2 ^ (n - 1) * v ^ n)

The count of Proposition 1.5 (iii). For |I| ≥ 1, (2|V|)^{|I|} = 2·(2^{|I|-1}·|V|^{|I|}), so a real vector space of dimension (2|V|)^{|I|} is a direct sum of 2^{|I|-1}·|V|^{|I|} copies of ℂ.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_6.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.6: the consequence of the prime number theorem defining η_prm

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.6 and its proof, page 16 of the April 2020 manuscript. Page 16 was read from the extracted text and from the rendered page; both displays below were checked against the render.

The paper writes p_n for the n-th smallest prime (so p_1 = 2, p_2 = 3) and states:

  • there is an integer n₀ with n ≤ 4·p_n / (3·log(p_n)) for all n ≥ n₀;
  • "in particular" there is a positive real η_prm with ∑_{p ≤ η} 1 ≤ 4·η / (3·log(η)) for all real η ≥ η_prm, the sum over primes p ≤ η.

The paper derives the first display from the prime number theorem, cited as [DmMn], §3.10, and says the second "follows formally".

What is proved here, unconditionally: both displays, from Mathlib's prime number theorem Nat.isEquivalent_primeCounting (π(x) ~ x / log x). We also check the paper's step "the final portion follows formally", that is, the second display follows from the first one alone (counting_of_nth).

Conventions
  • ∑_{p ≤ η} 1 is Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊, the number of primes ≤ ⌊η⌋, which is the number of primes ≤ η.
  • The paper's p_n is Nat.nth Nat.Prime (n - 1), since Mathlib's Nat.nth is 0-indexed.
  • We produce η_prm ≥ 2, which is stronger than the paper's "positive". Nothing later needs more than positivity: Step (viii) of the proof of Theorem 1.10 divides by log(η_prm) only in the case 1 < e*_mod·l < η_prm, where log(η_prm) > 0 already holds (page 31; see IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.StepVIII).
  • proposition1_6 states both displays together, as the proposition does.
Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.pdef

noncomputable def p (n : ℕ) : ℕ := Nat.nth Nat.Prime (n - 1)

The paper's p_n, the n-th smallest prime for n ≥ 1: p 1 = 2, p 2 = 3.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.p_onetheorem

theorem p_one : p 1 = 2

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.primeCounting_ptheorem

theorem primeCounting_p {n : ℕ} (hn : 1 ≤ n) : Nat.primeCounting (p n) = n

π(p_n) = n for n ≥ 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.exists_eta_prmtheorem

theorem exists_eta_prm :
  ∃ ηprm : ℝ, 2 ≤ ηprm ∧ ∀ η : ℝ, ηprm ≤ η →
    (Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * η / (3 * Real.log η)

Proposition 1.6, second display (unconditional). There is a real η_prm ≥ 2 such that for every real η ≥ η_prm the number of primes p ≤ η is at most 4·η / (3·log(η)).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.exists_n0theorem

theorem exists_n0 :
  ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n → 1 ≤ n →
    (n : ℝ) ≤ 4 * (p n : ℝ) / (3 * Real.log (p n))

Proposition 1.6, first display (unconditional). There is an n₀ such that n ≤ 4·p_n / (3·log(p_n)) for every n ≥ n₀. We include 1 ≤ n among the conclusions' hypotheses because p_n is only defined by the paper for positive n.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.proposition1_6theorem

theorem proposition1_6 :
  (∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n → 1 ≤ n →
    (n : ℝ) ≤ 4 * (p n : ℝ) / (3 * Real.log (p n))) ∧
  ∃ ηprm : ℝ, 0 < ηprm ∧ 2 ≤ ηprm ∧ ∀ η : ℝ, ηprm ≤ η →
    (Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * η / (3 * Real.log η)

Proposition 1.6 (both displays, unconditional): there is an n₀ with n ≤ 4·p_n / (3·log(p_n)) for all n ≥ n₀ (n ≥ 1), and there is a real η_prm > 0 (in fact ≥ 2) with ∑_{p ≤ η} 1 ≤ 4·η / (3·log(η)) for all real η ≥ η_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.div_log_monotoneOntheorem

theorem div_log_monotoneOn {x y : ℝ} (hx : Real.exp 1 ≤ x) (hxy : x ≤ y) :
  x / Real.log x ≤ y / Real.log y

The function x / log x is monotone on [e, ∞).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6.counting_of_nththeorem

theorem counting_of_nth
  (h : ∃ n₀ : ℕ, ∀ n : ℕ, n₀ ≤ n → 1 ≤ n →
    (n : ℝ) ≤ 4 * (p n : ℝ) / (3 * Real.log (p n))) :
  ∃ ηprm : ℝ, 0 < ηprm ∧ ∀ η : ℝ, ηprm ≤ η →
    (Nat.primeCounting ⌊η⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * η / (3 * Real.log η)

"The final portion of Proposition 1.6 follows formally." The second display follows from the first one, with no further input from analytic number theory.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition1_7.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 1.7: weighted averages

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 1.7 and its proof, pages 16 and 17 of the April 2020 manuscript. Both pages were read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages; the displays were checked against the renders.

Statement of the paper. Let E be a nonempty finite set and n a positive integer; for e ∈ E let λ_e > 0 and β_e ∈ ℝ. For an n-tuple e⃗ = (e₁, …, eₙ) ∈ Eⁿ put β_e⃗ = ∑_j β_{e_j} and λ_Πe⃗ = ∏_j λ_{e_j}; put λ_E = ∑_e λ_e, β_E = ∑_e β_e·λ_e, β_avg = β_E / λ_E. Then for every i = 1, …, n

(∑_{e⃗} β_e⃗ · λ_Πe⃗) / (∑_{e⃗} λ_Πe⃗) = (∑_{e⃗} n·β_{e_i} · λ_Πe⃗) / (∑_{e⃗} λ_Πe⃗) = n·β_avg.

This file proves the statement exactly, together with the two identities of its proof (sum_lamProd, sum_beta_i_mul_lamProd). The proposition is elementary; it does not depend on any other result of the papers.

Indexing: i ranges over Fin n, which is the paper's 1, …, n shifted by one.

Public declarations
variable {E : Type*} [Fintype E] {n : ℕ}

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.lamProddef

def lamProd (lam : E → ℝ) (v : Fin n → E) : ℝ := ∏ j, lam (v j)

λ_Πe⃗ = ∏_j λ_{e_j}.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.betaVecdef

def betaVec (beta : E → ℝ) (v : Fin n → E) : ℝ := ∑ j, beta (v j)

β_e⃗ = ∑_j β_{e_j}.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.betaAvgdef

noncomputable def betaAvg (lam beta : E → ℝ) : ℝ := (∑ e, beta e * lam e) / ∑ e, lam e

β_avg = β_E / λ_E with β_E = ∑_e β_e·λ_e and λ_E = ∑_e λ_e.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.sum_lamProdtheorem

theorem sum_lamProd (lam : E → ℝ) :
  ∑ v : Fin n → E, lamProd lam v = (∑ e, lam e) ^ n

First identity of the proof: λ_Eⁿ = ∑_{e⃗} λ_Πe⃗.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.sum_beta_i_mul_lamProdtheorem

theorem sum_beta_i_mul_lamProd (lam beta : E → ℝ) (i : Fin n) :
  ∑ v : Fin n → E, beta (v i) * lamProd lam v
    = (∑ e, beta e * lam e) * (∑ e, lam e) ^ (n - 1)

Second identity of the proof: β_E · λ_E^{n-1} = ∑_{e⃗} β_{e_i} · λ_Πe⃗ for every i.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.sum_betaVec_mul_lamProdtheorem

theorem sum_betaVec_mul_lamProd (lam beta : E → ℝ) :
  ∑ v : Fin n → E, betaVec beta v * lamProd lam v
    = n * ((∑ e, beta e * lam e) * (∑ e, lam e) ^ (n - 1))

Summing the second identity over i: n · β_E · λ_E^{n-1} = ∑_{e⃗} β_e⃗ · λ_Πe⃗.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_7.weighted_averagestheorem

theorem weighted_averages [Nonempty E] (hn : 1 ≤ n) (lam beta : E → ℝ)
  (hlam : ∀ e, 0 < lam e) (i : Fin n) :
  (∑ v : Fin n → E, betaVec beta v * lamProd lam v) / (∑ v : Fin n → E, lamProd lam v)
      = (∑ v : Fin n → E, n * beta (v i) * lamProd lam v) / (∑ v : Fin n → E, lamProd lam v)
    ∧ (∑ v : Fin n → E, n * beta (v i) * lamProd lam v) / (∑ v : Fin n → E, lamProd lam v)
      = n * betaAvg lam beta

Proposition 1.7. For a nonempty finite E, n ≥ 1, positive weights λ and real β, and every index i, both weighted averages of the display equal n·β_avg.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1

IUT/PaperIV/Proposition2_1.lean

IUT IV, Proposition 2.1: well-known estimates

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Proposition 2.1 and its proof, page 40 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered page; the displays were checked against the render. The extracted text of the final sentence reads "p ∈ A"; the render shows "p ∉ A", which is what is proved here.

Statement of the paper:

  • (i) log(x) ≤ x for all real x ≥ 1;
  • (ii) there is a real ξ_prm ≥ 5 with (2/3)·x ≤ θ(x) ≤ (4/3)·x for all real x ≥ ξ_prm, where θ(x) = ∑_{p ≤ x} log(p); in particular, for a finite set A of primes with θ_A = ∑_{p ∈ A} log(p), there is a prime p ∉ A with p ≤ 2(θ_A + ξ_prm).

The paper derives (ii) from the prime number theorem ([Edw], [GenEll] Lemma 4.1). Here both parts are proved unconditionally; (ii) uses Mathlib's Chebyshev.tendsto_theta_div_atTop (θ(x)/x → 1).

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1.log_le_self_of_one_letheorem

theorem log_le_self_of_one_le {x : ℝ} (hx : 1 ≤ x) : Real.log x ≤ x

Proposition 2.1 (i). log(x) ≤ x for x ≥ 1 (it holds for every x > 0).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1.exists_xi_prmtheorem

theorem exists_xi_prm :
  ∃ ξ : ℝ, 5 ≤ ξ ∧ ∀ x : ℝ, ξ ≤ x → 2 / 3 * x ≤ Chebyshev.theta x ∧
    Chebyshev.theta x ≤ 4 / 3 * x

Proposition 2.1 (ii), first part. There is ξ_prm ≥ 5 with (2/3)·x ≤ θ(x) ≤ (4/3)·x for all x ≥ ξ_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1.thetaAdef

noncomputable def thetaA (A : Finset ℕ) : ℝ := ∑ p ∈ A, Real.log p

θ_A = ∑_{p ∈ A} log(p).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1.exists_prime_not_mem_oftheorem

theorem exists_prime_not_mem_of {ξ : ℝ} (hξ : 0 < ξ)
  (hθ : ∀ x : ℝ, ξ ≤ x → 2 / 3 * x ≤ Chebyshev.theta x)
  (A : Finset ℕ) :
  ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ p ∉ A ∧ (p : ℝ) ≤ 2 * (thetaA A + ξ)

Proposition 2.1 (ii), "in particular". Let ξ > 0 satisfy (2/3)·x ≤ θ(x) for x ≥ ξ. For any finite set A of natural numbers (in the paper, of primes) there is a prime p ∉ A with p ≤ 2(θ_A + ξ).

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1.exists_prime_not_memtheorem

theorem exists_prime_not_mem :
  ∃ ξ : ℝ, 5 ≤ ξ ∧ ∀ A : Finset ℕ,
    ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ p ∉ A ∧ (p : ℝ) ≤ 2 * (thetaA A + ξ)

Proposition 2.1 (ii), "in particular", with the ξ_prm of exists_xi_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Proposition2_1.proposition2_1theorem

theorem proposition2_1 :
  (∀ x : ℝ, 1 ≤ x → Real.log x ≤ x) ∧
  ∃ ξ : ℝ, 5 ≤ ξ ∧ (∀ x : ℝ, ξ ≤ x → 2 / 3 * x ≤ Chebyshev.theta x ∧
    Chebyshev.theta x ≤ 4 / 3 * x) ∧
    ∀ A : Finset ℕ, ∃ p : ℕ, p.Prime ∧ p ∉ A ∧ (p : ℝ) ≤ 2 * (thetaA A + ξ)

Proposition 2.1, both parts with a single ξ_prm, as on page 40: (i) log(x) ≤ x for x ≥ 1; (ii) there is ξ_prm ≥ 5 with (2/3)·x ≤ θ(x) ≤ (4/3)·x for all x ≥ ξ_prm, and, for the same ξ_prm, every finite set A admits a prime p ∉ A with p ≤ 2(θ_A + ξ_prm). The paper takes A to be a set of primes; here A is any finite set of natural numbers, which is more general.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.Arithmetic

IUT/PaperIV/Theorem1_10/Arithmetic.lean

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10: the elementary real arithmetic of the proof

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, Theorem 1.10 and its proof, pages 22 to 31 of the April 2020 manuscript. The displays formalized here were read from the rendered pages 23, 30 and 31.

This file checks the parts of that proof that are plain manipulations of real numbers. It does not construct any of the arithmetic objects. Every quantity of the paper is a real variable here:

  • l the prime l, as a real number;
  • d the degree d_mod;
  • D the sum log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd});
  • E the sum e*_mod · l + η_prm;
  • Q the quantity log(𝔮);
  • q the quantity |log(q)|, which the paper states equals Q / (2 l);
  • L the quantity l*_mod · log(𝔰^≤) of Step (viii).

What is proved: given the hypotheses on these numbers that the paper derives elsewhere, the conclusions the paper draws from them by "applying the estimate ..." do follow. What is not proved here: that the arithmetic objects of the paper satisfy those hypotheses.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.constantdef

noncomputable def constant (l d D E Q q : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (l + 1) / (4 * q) * ((1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 10 * E - 1 / 6 * (1 - 12 / l ^ 2) * Q) - 1

The value of C_Θ displayed in the statement of Theorem 1.10 (page 23): (l+1)/(4·|log(q)|) · {(1 + 12·d_mod/l)·(log 𝔡 + log 𝔣) + 10·(e*_mod·l + η_prm) − (1/6)·(1 − 12/l²)·log(𝔮)} − 1. The two q-quantities are distinct arguments: Q stands for log(𝔮), the normalized degree of the q-parameter divisor, and q stands for |log(q)|, the absolute value of the q-pilot log-volume of IUT III, Corollary 3.12, which page 23 states equals log(𝔮)/(2l). The relation q = Q/(2l) is not built into the definition; the theorems that need it take it as a hypothesis (constant_bound_of_raw, step_viii_constant).

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.E1theorem

theorem E1 (n : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) :
  (1 / (n : ℝ)) * ∑ m ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n, (m : ℝ) = (n + 1) / 2

(E1): (1/n) · ∑_{m=1}^{n} m = (n + 1)/2.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.E2theorem

theorem E2 (n : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) :
  (1 / (n : ℝ)) * ∑ m ∈ Finset.Icc 1 n, (m : ℝ) ^ 2 = (2 * n + 1) * (n + 1) / 6

(E2): (1/n) · ∑_{m=1}^{n} m² = (2n + 1)(n + 1)/6.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_vii_archimedeantheorem

theorem step_vii_archimedean {l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) :
  (l + 5) / 4 * Real.log Real.pi ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * 4

Step (vii), final display: (l+5)/4 · log π ≤ (l+1)/4 · 4, "cf. the fact that l ≥ 5".

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_estimatetheorem

theorem step_viii_estimate {l : ℝ} (hl : 0 < l) :
  1 / (2 * l) ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * (1 / 6) * (12 / l ^ 2)

Step (viii): "we apply the estimate (l+1)/4 · 1/6 · 12/l² ≥ 1/(2l) [cf. the fact that l ≥ 1]". The estimate in fact holds for every l > 0.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_absorbtheorem

theorem step_viii_absorb {l e η : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (he : 1 ≤ e) (hη : 0 ≤ η) :
  2 * Real.log l + 56 ≤ 1 / 3 * (4 / 3) * (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l + η)

Step (viii): (1/3)·(4/3)·(e*_mod·l + η_prm) ≥ (1/3)·(4/3)·e*_mod·l ≥ 2·2·2¹²·3·5·l ≥ 2·log(l) + 56, with e*_mod = 2¹²·3³·5·e_mod. The paper cites e_mod ≥ 1, 2¹²·3·5 ≥ 56, l ≥ 5 ≥ 1, l ≥ log(l). Here e is e_mod and η is η_prm.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_substitutetheorem

theorem step_viii_substitute {l L E : ℝ} (hL : L ≤ 4 / 3 * E)
  (habs : 2 * Real.log l + 56 ≤ 1 / 3 * (4 / 3) * E) (hE : 0 ≤ E) :
  2 * Real.log l + 56 + 20 / 3 * L ≤ 10 * E

Step (viii), substitution: with L ≤ (4/3)·E (the consequence of Proposition 1.6 drawn in the proof) and the bound of step_viii_absorb, the terms 2·log(l) + 56 + (20/3)·L are at most 10·E. The paper's "estimate (20+1)/3 · 4/3 = 7·4/3 ≤ 10".

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.constant_bound_of_rawtheorem

theorem constant_bound_of_raw {l d D E Q q L X : ℝ} (hl : 0 < l) (hq : 0 < q)
  (hqQ : q = Q / (2 * l)) (hE : 0 ≤ E) (hL : L ≤ 4 / 3 * E)
  (habs : 2 * Real.log l + 56 ≤ 1 / 3 * (4 / 3) * E)
  (hraw : X ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 2 * Real.log l + 56
    - 1 / 6 * (1 - 12 / l ^ 2) * Q + 20 / 3 * L) - 1 / (2 * l) * Q) :
  X / q ≤ constant l d D E Q q

Step (viii), conclusion. Let X be the quantity that the proof bounds, which the paper calls C_Θ · |log(q)|. If X is at most the raw sum of the first display of Step (viii), and L ≤ (4/3)·E, and the absorption bound holds, then X / |log(q)| is at most the constant displayed in the statement of the theorem. Here q = Q / (2l) is |log(q)|.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.final_estimate_onetheorem

theorem final_estimate_one {l : ℝ} (hl : 7 ≤ l) : (1 - 12 / l ^ 2)⁻¹ ≤ 2

First estimate cited at the end of the proof: (1 − 12/l²)⁻¹ ≤ 2, "cf. the fact that l ≥ 7".

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.final_estimate_twotheorem

theorem final_estimate_two {l d : ℝ} (hl : 7 ≤ l) (hd : 1 ≤ d) :
  (1 - 12 / l ^ 2)⁻¹ * (1 + 12 * d / l) ≤ 1 + 20 * d / l

Second estimate cited at the end of the proof: (1 − 12/l²)⁻¹ · (1 + 12·d_mod/l) ≤ 1 + 20·d_mod/l, "cf. the fact that l ≥ 7, d_mod ≥ 1".

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.final_inequalitytheorem

theorem final_inequality {l d D E Q q : ℝ} (hl : 7 ≤ l) (hd : 1 ≤ d) (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hE : 0 ≤ E) (hq : 0 < q) (hC : -1 ≤ constant l d D E Q q) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * D + 20 * E

The final portion of Theorem 1.10, as real arithmetic. If the constant displayed in the statement is at least −1 (this is where the paper invokes the inequality C_Θ ≥ −1 of IUT III, Corollary 3.12), then (1/6)·log(𝔮) ≤ (1 + 20·d_mod/l)·(log 𝔡 + log 𝔣) + 20·(e*_mod·l + η_prm). The hypotheses 7 ≤ l, 1 ≤ d are the facts the paper cites. 0 ≤ D and 0 ≤ E hold in the paper because both are sums of nonnegative quantities. No sign condition on Q is needed.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.final_inequality_Ftheorem

theorem final_inequality_F {l d Dtpd DF E Q : ℝ} (hl : 0 < l) (hd : 0 ≤ d)
  (hstep_ii : Dtpd ≤ DF) (h : 1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * Dtpd + 20 * E) :
  1 / 6 * Q ≤ (1 + 20 * d / l) * DF + 20 * E

The second line of the final display: replacing the F_tpd quantities by the F quantities, given the inequality D_tpd ≤ D_F that the paper takes from "the first display of Step (ii)".

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, Step (i): the elementary facts (E3), (E4), (E6) and numerical bounds

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, proof of Theorem 1.10, Step (i), pages 23 and 24 of the April 2020 manuscript, and the numerical estimates the later steps quote "as consequences of the fact that l ≥ 5" (pages 24, 26, 29). Pages 23, 24, 26 and 29 were read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages; every display formalized here was checked against the render.

This file is not a formalization of Theorem 1.10. It checks the elementary facts that the proof recalls in Step (i), and the purely numerical inequalities that the later steps take from them:

  • (E3) |GL₂(𝔽_p)| = p(p+1)(p-1)² for a prime p (card_GL2).
  • (E4) the three values 2·3, 3·2⁴, 5·2⁵·3 (E4), and the product |GL₂(𝔽₂)|·|GL₂(𝔽₃)|·|GL₂(𝔽₅)|·2 = 2¹¹·3³·5 that bounds [F : F_mod] (E4_product); the proof of Corollary 2.2 uses it to get [F : ℚ] ≤ δ.
  • (E6) 0 ≤ log 2 ≤ 1, 1 ≤ log 3 ≤ log π ≤ log 5 ≤ 2 (E6).
  • log(l)/l ≤ 1/2 and 1 + 4/l ≤ 2 for l ≥ 5, quoted in Step (ii); the first holds for all l > 0 (log_div_le_half).
  • log(2¹¹·3³·5²) ≤ 21 (Step (ii)) and log(2·3·5) ≤ 5 (Step (iii)).

(E5), the bound [F_mod(√-1) : F_mod] ≤ 2, is field theory and is not formalized here.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.card_GL2theorem

theorem card_GL2 (p : ℕ) [hp : Fact p.Prime] :
  Nat.card (GL (Fin 2) (ZMod p)) = p * (p + 1) * (p - 1) ^ 2

(E3): |GL₂(𝔽_p)| = p(p+1)(p-1)² for a prime p.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.E4theorem

theorem E4 :
  2 * (2 + 1) * (2 - 1) ^ 2 = 2 * 3 ∧ 3 * (3 + 1) * (3 - 1) ^ 2 = 3 * 2 ^ 4 ∧
    5 * (5 + 1) * (5 - 1) ^ 2 = 5 * 2 ^ 5 * 3

(E4): the values of p(p+1)(p-1)² at p = 2, 3, 5.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.E4_producttheorem

theorem E4_product : (2 * 3) * (3 * 2 ^ 4) * (5 * 2 ^ 5 * 3) * 2 = 2 ^ 11 * 3 ^ 3 * 5

The product |GL₂(𝔽₂)|·|GL₂(𝔽₃)|·|GL₂(𝔽₅)|·2 = 2¹¹·3³·5, which bounds [F : F_mod] via the outer inclusion Gal(F/F_mod) ↪ GL₂(𝔽₂) × GL₂(𝔽₃) × GL₂(𝔽₅) × ℤ/2ℤ used in Step (ii) and in the proof of Corollary 2.2 ("[F : ℚ] ≤ δ, cf. (E3), (E4), (E5)").

  0 ≤ Real.log 2 ∧ Real.log 2 ≤ 1 ∧ 1 ≤ Real.log 3 ∧ Real.log 3 ≤ Real.log Real.pi ∧
    Real.log Real.pi ≤ Real.log 5 ∧ Real.log 5 ≤ 2

(E6): 0 ≤ log(2) ≤ 1, 1 ≤ log(3) ≤ log(π) ≤ log(5) ≤ 2.

log(l)/l ≤ 1/2, quoted in Step (ii) as a consequence of l ≥ 5; it holds for every l > 0. Stated without division: 2·log(l) ≤ l.

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, Step (ii): the bounds on differents and conductors

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, proof of Theorem 1.10, Step (ii), page 24 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered page; every display formalized here was checked against the render.

This file is not a formalization of Step (ii). Step (ii) derives three inequalities between the quantities log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}), log(𝔡^F) + log(𝔣^F) and log(𝔡^K) from Proposition 1.3 and from facts about the Galois groups Gal(F/F_tpd), Gal(K/F). Those local field inputs are not formalized. Here every quantity is a real variable and the inputs are hypotheses; what is checked is that the constants 21 and 46 of the paper follow from them.

Variables: Dtpd = log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}), DF = log(𝔡^F) + log(𝔣^F), dK = log(𝔡^K), fK = log(𝔣^K).

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_ii_Ftheorem

theorem step_ii_F {Dtpd DF : ℝ} (h : DF ≤ Dtpd + Real.log (2 ^ 11 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 ^ 2)) :
  DF ≤ Dtpd + 21

Second display of Step (ii). Input (the paper's application of Proposition 1.3 at the primes dividing 2·3·5 and not dividing it): DF ≤ Dtpd + log(2¹¹·3³·5²). Conclusion: DF ≤ Dtpd + 21.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_ii_Ktheorem

theorem step_ii_K {Dtpd DF dK fK l : ℝ} (hf : 0 ≤ fK)
  (hK : dK + fK ≤ DF + 2 * Real.log l)
  (hF : DF ≤ Dtpd + Real.log (2 ^ 11 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 ^ 2)) :
  dK ≤ Dtpd + 2 * Real.log l + 21

Third display of Step (ii). Inputs: log(𝔣^K) ≥ 0, the bound log(𝔡^K) + log(𝔣^K) ≤ DF + 2·log(l) that the paper takes from Proposition 1.3 and Gal(K/F) ↪ GL₂(𝔽_l), and the second display. Conclusion: log(𝔡^K) ≤ Dtpd + 2·log(l) + 21.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_ii_finaltheorem

theorem step_ii_final {Dtpd dK l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (h : dK ≤ Dtpd + 2 * Real.log l + 21) :
  (1 + 4 / l) * dK ≤ (1 + 4 / l) * Dtpd + 2 * Real.log l + 46

Final display of Step (ii): (1 + 4/l)·log(𝔡^K) ≤ (1 + 4/l)·Dtpd + 2·log(l) + 46, using log(l)/l ≤ 1/2 and 1 + 4/l ≤ 2, "consequences of the fact that l ≥ 5".

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, Step (iii): ramification indices and the arithmetic divisor 𝔰

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, proof of Theorem 1.10, Step (iii), pages 24 to 26 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages; every display formalized here was checked against the renders of pages 25 and 26.

This file is not a formalization of Step (iii). Step (iii) introduces the distinguished valuations, the divisors 𝔰^{F_*}_{ADiv}, 𝔰^≤_{ADiv}, the conditions (D0) to (D7) and the bounds (R1) to (R4) on ramification indices. The facts about the arithmetic objects (that the conditions are equivalent, that (R1) to (R3) hold, that log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ 2·d_mod·D + log(2·3·5·l)) are taken as hypotheses. What is checked is the real arithmetic that the paper performs on them:

  • (R1) to (R3) imply (R4) (R4);
  • the bound (1/p)·log(p) ≤ 1/2 for p = l ≥ 5 (step_iii_p_eq_l);
  • the chain log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ 2·d_mod·D + 5 + log(l), then (4/l)·log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ (8·d_mod/l)·D + 6;
  • the final display (1 + 4/l)·log(𝔡^K) + (4/l)·log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ (1 + 12·d_mod/l)·D + 2·log(l) + 52, which needs D ≥ 0 and d_mod ≥ 1.

Variables: D = log(𝔡^{F_tpd}) + log(𝔣^{F_tpd}), d = d_mod, sQ = log(𝔰^ℚ), dK = log(𝔡^K), e = e_mod.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.R4theorem

theorem R4 {ev pv e l : ℝ} (he : 1 ≤ e) (hl : 5 ≤ l) (hev : 1 ≤ ev)
  (hR : (pv = l ∧ ev ≤ 2 ^ 11 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l ^ 4) ∨ ev ≤ 2 ^ 11 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l)
  (hram : pv - 1 ≤ ev) :
  pv ≤ 2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l ∧
    Real.log ev ≤ -3 + 4 * Real.log (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l)

(R1) to (R3) imply (R4). Let v be a valuation with ramification index e_v ≥ 1 and residue characteristic p_v. Suppose that either p_v = l and (R1) e_v ≤ 2¹¹·3³·5·e_mod·l⁴, or (R2)/(R3) e_v ≤ 2¹¹·3³·5·e_mod·l (R3 is the stronger bound e_v ≤ 2¹¹·3³·5·e_mod, which implies this one since l ≥ 1). If e_v ≥ p_v - 1, then p_v ≤ 2¹²·3³·5·e_mod·l = e*_mod·l and log(e_v) ≤ -3 + 4·log(e*_mod·l).

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_iii_p_eq_ltheorem

theorem step_iii_p_eq_l {l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) : 1 / l * Real.log l ≤ 1 / 2

Second display of Step (iii): for p_{v_ℚ} = l ≥ 5, (1/p)·log(p) ≤ 1/2.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_iii_sQtheorem

theorem step_iii_sQ {sQ d D l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l)
  (h : sQ ≤ 2 * d * D + Real.log (2 * 3 * 5 * l)) :
  sQ ≤ 2 * d * D + 5 + Real.log l ∧ 4 / l * sQ ≤ 8 * d / l * D + 6

Step (iii), the chain on log(𝔰^ℚ). Input: log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ 2·d_mod·D + log(2·3·5·l), which the paper takes from Proposition 1.3 (i). Conclusions: log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ 2·d_mod·D + 5 + log(l) and (4/l)·log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ (8·d_mod/l)·D + 6 ("cf. (E6); the fact that l ≥ 5").

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_iii_finaltheorem

theorem step_iii_final {dK sQ d D l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (hd : 1 ≤ d) (hD : 0 ≤ D)
  (hii : (1 + 4 / l) * dK ≤ (1 + 4 / l) * D + 2 * Real.log l + 46)
  (hs : 4 / l * sQ ≤ 8 * d / l * D + 6) :
  (1 + 4 / l) * dK + 4 / l * sQ ≤ (1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 2 * Real.log l + 52

Final display of Step (iii). From the final display of Step (ii) and the bound on (4/l)·log(𝔰^ℚ): (1 + 4/l)·log(𝔡^K) + (4/l)·log(𝔰^ℚ) ≤ (1 + 12·d_mod/l)·D + 2·log(l) + 52. The paper applies d_mod ≥ 1; the step 4/l·D ≤ 4·d_mod/l·D also needs D ≥ 0, which holds because D is a sum of degrees of effective arithmetic divisors.

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, Steps (v) and (vii): weighted averages and procession-normalized bounds

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, proof of Theorem 1.10, Steps (v) and (vii), pages 27 to 30 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages; every display formalized here was checked against the renders of pages 28, 29 and 30.

This file is not a formalization of Steps (v) and (vii). Those steps apply Propositions 1.4 and 1.5 to the tensor packets of log-shells of IUT III, Theorem 3.11, and that application is not formalized. Here the local log-volume bounds are real variables, and what is checked is the arithmetic the paper performs on them:

  • the bound ∑_{i ∈ I*} {3 + log(e_i)} ≤ 4(j+1)·l*_mod that turns the second inequality of Proposition 1.4 (iii) into the third display of page 28, given (R4) (sum_Istar_le);
  • the passage to weighted averages via Proposition 1.7, including the fact that the weighted average of the local quantities log(𝔡^K_v) with weights [(F_mod)_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}] is log(𝔡^K_{v_ℚ}) when these are normalized as in Definition 1.9 (ii) with the unnormalized degree in the numerator (weighted_average_normalized);
  • the "procession-normalized upper bound": averaging over j ∈ {1, …, l*} with (E1), (E2) (procession_average), its rewriting with l* = (l-1)/2 (procession_average_l), and the final inequality of Step (v) (step_v_final);
  • the averaging of Step (vii) (step_vii_average).

Step (vi) (page 29) has no declaration here. Its content is the bound 0 on the log-volume of the container (R_I)^∼ given by the inclusion and the final equality of Proposition 1.4 (iv); neither the container nor its log-volume is formalized, and averaging the constant 0 checks nothing about them.

Notation: n is l* = (l-1)/2, a natural number with l = 2n+1; A = log(𝔡^K_{v_ℚ}), B = log(𝔮_{v_ℚ}), C = log(𝔰^ℚ_{v_ℚ}), T = l*_mod·log(𝔰^≤_{v_ℚ}), L = l*_mod.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.sum_Istar_letheorem

theorem sum_Istar_le {ι : Type*} {I Istar : Finset ι} {loge : ι → ℝ} {L : ℝ} {j : ℕ}
  (hsub : Istar ⊆ I) (hcard : I.card = j + 1) (hL : 0 ≤ L)
  (hR4 : ∀ i ∈ Istar, loge i ≤ -3 + 4 * L) :
  ∑ i ∈ Istar, (3 + loge i) ≤ 4 * (j + 1) * L

Page 28: if every i ∈ I* satisfies log(e_i) ≤ -3 + 4·l*_mod (this is (R4)), I* ⊆ I and |I| = j+1, then ∑_{i ∈ I*} {3 + log(e_i)} ≤ 4(j+1)·l*_mod. Needs l*_mod ≥ 0, which holds since l*_mod = log(e*_mod·l) and e*_mod·l ≥ 1.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.weighted_average_normalizedtheorem

theorem weighted_average_normalized {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [Nonempty ι] (N lam : ι → ℝ)
  (κ : ℝ) (hκ : 0 < κ) (hlam : ∀ v, 0 < lam v) :
  Proposition1_7.betaAvg lam (fun v => N v / (κ * lam v)) = (∑ v, N v) / (κ * ∑ v, lam v)

The weighted average used in Step (v), with the normalization of Definition 1.9 (ii). For each v over v_ℚ let N v be the unnormalized degree of the portion over v of an arithmetic divisor on K, λ v = [(F_mod)_v : ℚ_{v_ℚ}] > 0, and κ = [K : F_mod] > 0, so that ∑_{w | v} [K_w : ℚ_{v_ℚ}] = κ·λ v. The local quantity is x v = N v / (κ·λ v). Then the λ-weighted average of x is (∑_v N v) / (κ·∑_v λ v), which is the quantity at v_ℚ (with ∑_v λ v = [F_mod : ℚ] and κ·[F_mod : ℚ] = [K : ℚ]).

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.betaAvg_affinetheorem

theorem betaAvg_affine {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [Nonempty ι] (lam f g : ι → ℝ) (a b c : ℝ)
  (hlam : ∀ v, 0 < lam v) :
  Proposition1_7.betaAvg lam (fun v => a * f v + b * g v + c)
    = a * Proposition1_7.betaAvg lam f + b * Proposition1_7.betaAvg lam g + c

Linearity of the weighted average, used to pass from the bound β_e of page 28 to the "weighted average upper bound": avg(a·f + b·g + c) = a·avg(f) + b·avg(g) + c.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_v_weightedtheorem

theorem step_v_weighted {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [Nonempty ι] (lam Aloc Bloc : ι → ℝ)
  (hlam : ∀ v, 0 < lam v) (j : ℕ) (l logp iota L : ℝ) (hl : l ≠ 0) (i : Fin (j + 1)) :
  (∑ v : Fin (j + 1) → ι,
      Proposition1_7.betaVec
        (fun w => Aloc w - (j : ℝ) ^ 2 / (2 * l * (j + 1)) * Bloc w
          + 1 / (j + 1) * logp + 4 * iota * L) v * Proposition1_7.lamProd lam v)
    / (∑ v : Fin (j + 1) → ι, Proposition1_7.lamProd lam v)
    = (j + 1) * Proposition1_7.betaAvg lam Aloc
      - (j : ℝ) ^ 2 / (2 * l) * Proposition1_7.betaAvg lam Bloc
      + logp + 4 * (j + 1) * iota * L

The "weighted average upper bound" of Step (v) (last display of page 28). With β_v = A_v - j²/(2l(j+1))·B_v + 1/(j+1)·log(p) + 4·ι·L (page 28), Proposition 1.7 with n = j+1 gives, for every index i, that the weighted average of the symmetrized bound β_e⃗ equals (j+1)·avg(A) - j²/(2l)·avg(B) + log(p) + 4(j+1)·ι·L.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.procession_sumtheorem

theorem procession_sum (A B C T l : ℝ) (k : ℕ) :
  ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 k, (((j : ℝ) + 1) * A - (j : ℝ) ^ 2 / (2 * l) * B + C + 4 * ((j : ℝ) + 1) * T)
    = k * (k + 3) / 2 * A - k * (k + 1) * (2 * k + 1) / (12 * l) * B + k * C
      + 2 * k * (k + 3) * T

Closed form of the sum over j used in the procession-normalized bound.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.procession_averagetheorem

theorem procession_average (A B C T l : ℝ) (n : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) :
  1 / (n : ℝ) * ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 n,
      (((j : ℝ) + 1) * A - (j : ℝ) ^ 2 / (2 * l) * B + C + 4 * ((j : ℝ) + 1) * T)
    = (n + 3) / 2 * A - (2 * n + 1) * (n + 1) / (12 * l) * B + C + 2 * (n + 3) * T

First line of the display of page 29. Averaging the weighted average upper bound over j ∈ {1, …, l*} with (E1), (E2): (1/l*)·∑_{j=1}^{l*} [(j+1)A - j²/(2l)·B + C + 4(j+1)T] = (l*+3)/2·A - (2l*+1)(l*+1)/(12l)·B + C + 2(l*+3)·T.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.procession_average_ltheorem

theorem procession_average_l (A B C T : ℝ) (n : ℕ) :
  ((n : ℝ) + 3) / 2 * A - (2 * n + 1) * (n + 1) / (12 * (2 * n + 1)) * B + C
      + 2 * (n + 3) * T
    = ((2 * n + 1) + 5) / 4 * A - ((2 * n + 1) + 1) / 24 * B + C + ((2 * n + 1) + 5) * T

Second line of the display of page 29. With l = 2·l* + 1: (l*+3)/2 = (l+5)/4, (2l*+1)(l*+1)/(12l) = (l+1)/24, 2(l*+3) = l+5.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_v_finaltheorem

theorem step_v_final {A B C T l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (hA : 0 ≤ A) (hC : 0 ≤ C) (hT : 0 ≤ T) :
  (l + 5) / 4 * A - (l + 1) / 24 * B + C + (l + 5) * T
    ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 4 / l) * A - 1 / 6 * B + 4 / l * C + 20 / 3 * T)

Third line of the display of page 29 (final inequality of Step (v)). Using 1/(l+1) ≤ 1/l and 4(l+5)/(l+1) ≤ 20/3, "consequences of the fact that l ≥ 5": (l+5)/4·A - (l+1)/24·B + C + (l+5)·T ≤ (l+1)/4·{(1 + 4/l)·A - 1/6·B + 4/l·C + 20/3·T}. The inequality needs A, C, T ≥ 0; in the paper these are log(𝔡^K_{v_ℚ}), log(𝔰^ℚ_{v_ℚ}) and l*_mod·log(𝔰^≤_{v_ℚ}), all nonnegative by definition.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_vii_averagetheorem

theorem step_vii_average (n : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) :
  1 / (n : ℝ) * ∑ j ∈ Icc 1 n, ((j : ℝ) + 1) * Real.log Real.pi
    = ((2 * n + 1) + 5) / 4 * Real.log Real.pi

Step (vii), averaging. With local bound (j+1)·log(π), the procession-normalized bound is (l*+3)/2·log(π) = (l+5)/4·log(π) for l = 2l*+1; the existing theorem step_vii_archimedean then gives ≤ (l+1)/4·4.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.StepVIII

IUT/PaperIV/Theorem1_10/StepVIII.lean · imports IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.StepIII, IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.Arithmetic, IUT.PaperIV.Proposition1_6

IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, Step (viii): summing the local bounds and the prime count

Module documentation

Source: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory IV, proof of Theorem 1.10, Step (viii), pages 30 and 31 of the April 2020 manuscript, read from the extracted text and from the rendered pages; every display formalized here was checked against the renders.

This file is not a formalization of Step (viii). The step sums the local procession-normalized bounds of Steps (v), (vi), (vii) over the places of ℚ, applies the final display of Step (iii), and bounds l*_mod·log(𝔰^≤) with Proposition 1.6. The local bounds and the identification of log(𝔰^≤) with a count of primes are taken as hypotheses (they rest on the unformalized Steps (iii) to (vii)). What is checked:

  • summing the local bounds (step_viii_sum) and passing to the form hraw of constant_bound_of_raw, using the estimate (l+1)/4·(1/6)·(12/l²) ≥ 1/(2l) and log(𝔮) ≥ 0 (step_viii_raw);
  • the two-case bound l*_mod·log(𝔰^≤) ≤ (4/3)(e*_mod·l + η_prm) (step_viii_prime_count), and its instance with the η_prm produced by Proposition1_6.exists_eta_prm (step_viii_prime_count_prm);
  • the assembly into the bound C_Θ ≤ constant of Theorem 1.10 (step_viii_constant), given that C_Θ·|log(𝔮)| is at most the summed bound.

The first case of the prime count (e*_mod·l ≥ η_prm) divides by log(e*_mod·l) and the second (e*_mod·l < η_prm) by log(η_prm). No condition on η_prm beyond the paper's positivity is needed: since e*_mod·l ≥ 1, the second case has 1 ≤ e*_mod·l < η_prm, which already gives log(η_prm) > 0 (page 31). Accordingly step_viii_prime_count and step_viii_constant assume only η > 0, the positivity stated in Proposition 1.6, together with e*_mod·l > 1, which holds since e*_mod·l ≥ 2¹²·3³·5·5.

Notation: x = e*_mod·l, sle = log(𝔰^≤), Q = log(𝔮), L = l*_mod·log(𝔰^≤), E = e*_mod·l + η_prm.

Public declarations

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_sumtheorem

theorem step_viii_sum {ι : Type*} (S : Finset ι) (X A B C T : ι → ℝ) (Xarc l : ℝ)
  (hloc : ∀ v ∈ S,
    X v ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 4 / l) * A v - 1 / 6 * B v + 4 / l * C v + 20 / 3 * T v))
  (harc : Xarc ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * 4) :
  ∑ v ∈ S, X v + Xarc
    ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 4 / l) * ∑ v ∈ S, A v - 1 / 6 * ∑ v ∈ S, B v
        + 4 / l * ∑ v ∈ S, C v + 20 / 3 * ∑ v ∈ S, T v + 4)

Summing the local bounds. Over a finite set S of nonarchimedean places, local procession-normalized bounds of the form of the last line of page 29 (step_v_final), together with an archimedean bound (l+1)/4·4 (Step (vii), step_vii_archimedean), sum to the bound with the global quantities ∑_v A_v, ∑_v B_v, ∑_v C_v, ∑_v T_v.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_rawtheorem

theorem step_viii_raw {X dK Q sQ L d D l : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (hQ : 0 ≤ Q)
  (hsum : X ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 4 / l) * dK - 1 / 6 * Q + 4 / l * sQ + 20 / 3 * L + 4))
  (hiii : (1 + 4 / l) * dK + 4 / l * sQ ≤ (1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 2 * Real.log l + 52) :
  X ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 2 * Real.log l + 56
    - 1 / 6 * (1 - 12 / l ^ 2) * Q + 20 / 3 * L) - 1 / (2 * l) * Q

First display of Step (viii). From the summed bound and the final display of Step (iii), with log(𝔮) ≥ 0 and the estimate (l+1)/4·(1/6)·(12/l²) ≥ 1/(2l), one obtains the bound in the form hraw of constant_bound_of_raw: X ≤ (l+1)/4·{(1 + 12·d_mod/l)·D + 2·log(l) + 56 - 1/6·(1 - 12/l²)·Q + 20/3·L} - 1/(2l)·Q.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_prime_counttheorem

theorem step_viii_prime_count {x η sle : ℝ} (hx : 1 < x) (hη : 0 < η)
  (hprm : ∀ y : ℝ, η ≤ y → (Nat.primeCounting ⌊y⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * y / (3 * Real.log y))
  (hs0 : 0 ≤ sle) (hs : sle ≤ Nat.primeCounting ⌊x⌋₊) :
  Real.log x * sle ≤ 4 / 3 * (x + η)

The prime count of Step (viii) (two cases, pages 30 and 31). Let x = e*_mod·l > 1 and let η > 0 satisfy the conclusion of Proposition 1.6. In the case x < η the inequality 1 < x < η gives log(η) > 0, so no further condition on η is used. If 0 ≤ sle ≤ π(x) (the paper's log(𝔰^≤) = ∑_{p ≤ x} 1 over the distinguished primes, so at most the number of primes ≤ x), then log(x)·sle ≤ (4/3)·(x + η).

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_prime_count_prmtheorem

theorem step_viii_prime_count_prm :
  ∃ η : ℝ, 2 ≤ η ∧ ∀ x sle : ℝ, 1 < x → 0 ≤ sle → sle ≤ Nat.primeCounting ⌊x⌋₊ →
    Real.log x * sle ≤ 4 / 3 * (x + η)

The prime count bound with the η_prm of Proposition 1.6, proved unconditionally from Mathlib's prime number theorem.

IUT.PaperIV.Theorem1_10.step_viii_constanttheorem

theorem step_viii_constant {X dK Q sQ sle d D l e η q : ℝ} (hl : 5 ≤ l) (he : 1 ≤ e)
  (hη : 0 < η)
  (hprm : ∀ y : ℝ, η ≤ y → (Nat.primeCounting ⌊y⌋₊ : ℝ) ≤ 4 * y / (3 * Real.log y))
  (hs0 : 0 ≤ sle) (hs : sle ≤ Nat.primeCounting ⌊2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l⌋₊)
  (hq : 0 < q) (hqQ : q = Q / (2 * l))
  (hsum : X ≤ (l + 1) / 4 * ((1 + 4 / l) * dK - 1 / 6 * Q + 4 / l * sQ
    + 20 / 3 * (Real.log (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l) * sle) + 4))
  (hiii : (1 + 4 / l) * dK + 4 / l * sQ ≤ (1 + 12 * d / l) * D + 2 * Real.log l + 52) :
  X / q ≤ constant l d D (2 ^ 12 * 3 ^ 3 * 5 * e * l + η) Q q

Assembly of Step (viii). Let e = e_mod ≥ 1, x = 2¹²·3³·5·e·l = e*_mod·l, E = x + η with η > 0 as in Proposition 1.6, L = log(x)·sle with 0 ≤ sle ≤ π(x), q = Q/(2l) > 0, and X bounded by the summed local bound. Then X/q ≤ constant l d D E Q q, the right-hand side of the bound for C_Θ in Theorem 1.10.

Objections and responses

Elementary mathematics behind the Scholze–Stix objections and Mochizuki's responses. Nothing here settles the dispute.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVEx

IUT/Audit/MochizukiResponses/LVEx.lean

Mochizuki's example (LVEx): indeterminacies make log-volumes relate non-linearly

Module documentation

Sources: S. Mochizuki, Report on Discussions, Held During the Period March 15 to 20, 2018, Concerning Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory (IUTch), version dated February 2019, items (MlLV) and (LVEx), pages 24 to 26; and Comments on the Manuscript (2018-08 Version) by Scholze–Stix, September 2018, item (C14), page 4. Read for this file: report pages 24 to 26 and comments page 4, in the extracted text. The displayed areas and inequalities of (LVEx) were checked against the rendered pages 24 and 25 for the companion file IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.

In V = ℝ² with the reflection σ(x, y) = (−x, y), the example takes the region

R_{a,b} = {|x| ≤ 1, |y| ≤ a} ∪ {0 ≤ x ≤ 1, a ≤ |y| ≤ a + b}

and its symmetrization S_{a,b} = R_{a,b} ∪ σ(R_{a,b}), for a, b > 0. The report states that their areas are 4a + 2b and 4a + 4b. This file takes those two areas as given (they are derived from Lebesgue measure in IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure) and checks the claim made about them: the log-volumes log(4a + 2b) and log(4a + 4b) are not related by a fixed linear map.

In the analogy of the report, R corresponds to the Θ-pilot in the Θ-holomorphic structure and S to the holomorphic hull of its multiradial representation. The example shows that the hypothesis "(Lin)" of the Scholze–Stix argument (see IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Diagram) can fail for a hull under a group of indeterminacies. Whether the situation of IUT III, Corollary 3.12, behaves like this example is not addressed by this file.

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.logVolRdef

noncomputable def logVolR (a b : ℝ) : ℝ := Real.log (4 * a + 2 * b)

Log-volume of the region R_{a,b}, from the area 4a + 2b stated in the report.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.logVolSdef

noncomputable def logVolS (a b : ℝ) : ℝ := Real.log (4 * a + 4 * b)

Log-volume of the symmetrized region S_{a,b}, from the stated area 4a + 4b.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.logVolR_lt_logVolStheorem

theorem logVolR_lt_logVolS {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : logVolR a b < logVolS a b

μ^log(S_{a,b}) > μ^log(R_{a,b}), as displayed in (LVEx).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.not_lineartheorem

theorem not_linear : ¬ ∃ c : ℝ, ∀ a b : ℝ, 0 < a → 0 < b → logVolS a b = c * logVolR a b

No positive scalar relates the two log-volumes for all a, b > 0: there is no c with μ^log(S_{a,b}) = c · μ^log(R_{a,b}) identically. At a = 1/8, b = 1/4 the region R has area 1, so log-volume 0, while S has area 3/2.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.not_affine_shifttheorem

theorem not_affine_shift :
  ¬ ∃ s : ℝ, ∀ a b : ℝ, 0 < a → 0 < b → logVolS a b = logVolR a b + s

Nor are they related by a fixed additive shift, that is, by rescaling the measure: the difference μ^log(S) − μ^log(R) = log((4a + 4b)/(4a + 2b)) depends on (a, b).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure
