IUT/Audit/MochizukiResponses/LVExMeasure.lean · imports IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVEx

Mochizuki's example (LVEx), with Lebesgue measure

Module documentation

Source: S. Mochizuki, Report on Discussions, Held During the Period March 15 to 20, 2018, Concerning Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory (IUTch), version dated February 2019, item (LVEx), pages 24 to 26. Read for this file: report pages 24 to 26; the definitions of R_{a,b}, S_{a,b}, the displayed log-volumes, and the inequalities on λ were checked against the rendered pages 24 and 25.

Whose argument. Mochizuki gives (LVEx), together with (MlLV), in reply to what he calls the assertion (Lin) underlying Section 2.2 of the Scholze–Stix manuscript (comments on the 2018-08 version, item (C14)). The companion file IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVEx takes the two areas 4a + 2b and 4a + 4b as given. This file derives them from the Lebesgue measure volume on ℝ × ℝ and then checks the remaining assertions of (LVEx) about λ and ρ.

The example. σ (x, y) = (−x, y), R_{a,b} = {|x| ≤ 1, 0 ≤ |y| ≤ a} ∪ {0 ≤ x ≤ 1, a ≤ |y| ≤ a + b}, S_{a,b} = R_{a,b} ∪ σ(R_{a,b}), a, b > 0. The report states μ^log(S_{a,b}) = log(4a + 4b) > μ^log(R_{a,b}) = log(4a + 2b), and then takes λ with μ^log(S) > 0 > λ > μ^log(R), ρ = μ^log(R) · λ⁻¹ > 1, and the "gluing" x ↦ x / ρ that sends μ^log(R) to λ.

What is proved. The two areas (volume_R, volume_S); σ(S) = S and σ(R) ≠ R; the existence of admissible (a, b, λ); ρ > 1; the gluing sends μ^log(R) to λ; the inequality μ^log(S) > λ.

Observation (assessment of this project). In the example the inequality μ^log(S) > λ, which the report describes as "entirely logically consistent" with the gluing, is one of the hypotheses imposed on λ (μ^log(S) > 0 > λ); logVolS_gt_lambda is immediate from them. The example shows that such λ exist and that the relation between μ^log(S) and μ^log(R) is not linear. It does not show, and is not offered as showing, that the situation of IUT III, Corollary 3.12, has this form. This file does not settle the dispute.

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.σdef

def σ (p : ℝ × ℝ) : ℝ × ℝ := (-p.1, p.2)

The reflection σ (x, y) = (−x, y).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.σ_σtheorem

theorem σ_σ (p : ℝ × ℝ) : σ (σ p) = p

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.Ydef

def Y (a b : ℝ) : Set ℝ := {y | a ≤ |y| ∧ |y| ≤ a + b}

The set {y | a ≤ |y| ≤ a + b}.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.Rdef

def R (a b : ℝ) : Set (ℝ × ℝ) :=
  {p | |p.1| ≤ 1 ∧ 0 ≤ |p.2| ∧ |p.2| ≤ a} ∪ {p | 0 ≤ p.1 ∧ p.1 ≤ 1 ∧ a ≤ |p.2| ∧ |p.2| ≤ a + b}

R_{a,b}, as in the report.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.Sdef

def S (a b : ℝ) : Set (ℝ × ℝ) := R a b ∪ σ '' R a b

S_{a,b} = R_{a,b} ∪ σ(R_{a,b}).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.image_σtheorem

theorem image_σ (A : Set (ℝ × ℝ)) : σ '' A = σ ⁻¹' A

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.Y_eqtheorem

theorem Y_eq {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) : Y a b = Icc (-(a + b)) (-a) ∪ Icc a (a + b)

Y as a union of two closed intervals.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.volume_Ytheorem

theorem volume_Y {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) :
  volume (Y a b) = ENNReal.ofReal (2 * b)

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.S_eqtheorem

theorem S_eq {a b : ℝ} (hb : 0 < b) :
  S a b = Icc (-1) 1 ×ˢ Icc (-(a + b)) (a + b)

S_{a,b} is the rectangle [−1, 1] × [−(a + b), a + b].

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.volume_Stheorem

theorem volume_S {a b : ℝ} (hb : 0 < b) :
  volume (S a b) = ENNReal.ofReal (4 * a + 4 * b)

**The area of S_{a,b} is 4a + 4b.**

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.R_eqtheorem

theorem R_eq (a b : ℝ) :
  R a b = Icc (-1) 1 ×ˢ Icc (-a) a ∪ Icc 0 1 ×ˢ Y a b

The two pieces of R_{a,b} as products.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.volume_Rtheorem

theorem volume_R {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) :
  volume (R a b) = ENNReal.ofReal (4 * a + 2 * b)

**The area of R_{a,b} is 4a + 2b.** The two pieces meet in the null set [0, 1] × {−a, a}.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.σ_Stheorem

theorem σ_S (a b : ℝ) : σ '' S a b = S a b

σ(S_{a,b}) = S_{a,b}: the symmetrization is "defined over W".

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.σ_R_netheorem

theorem σ_R_ne {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : σ '' R a b ≠ R a b

σ(R_{a,b}) ≠ R_{a,b}: the point (1, a + b) lies in R_{a,b}, its image (−1, a + b) does not. So R_{a,b} is "only defined over V".

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.μlogdef

noncomputable def μlog (A : Set (ℝ × ℝ)) : ℝ := Real.log (volume A).toReal

The log-volume μ^log (natural logarithm of the Lebesgue measure). It is meaningful only on sets of finite positive measure, which is the case of the two regions of the report for a, b > 0 (volume_R, volume_S). Outside that domain the definition is a totalization: through ENNReal.toReal and Real.log, both a set of measure 0 and a set of infinite measure get the value 0. Every lemma below about μlog assumes a, b > 0.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.μlog_Rtheorem

theorem μlog_R {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : μlog (R a b) = logVolR a b

The log-volumes agree with those taken as given in LVEx.lean.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.μlog_Stheorem

theorem μlog_S {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : μlog (S a b) = logVolS a b

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.μlog_R_lt_Stheorem

theorem μlog_R_lt_S {a b : ℝ} (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : μlog (R a b) < μlog (S a b)

The displayed inequality of (LVEx), now from Lebesgue measure: μ^log(S_{a,b}) > μ^log(R_{a,b}).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.admissible_existstheorem

theorem admissible_exists :
  ∃ a b lam : ℝ, 0 < a ∧ 0 < b ∧ 0 < μlog (S a b) ∧ lam < 0 ∧ μlog (R a b) < lam

Admissible data exist: at a = 1/8, b = 1/5 and λ = −1/20, μ^log(S) > 0 > λ > μ^log(R).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.gluingtheorem

theorem gluing {mS mR lam : ℝ} (hS : 0 < mS) (hlam : lam < 0) (hR : mR < lam) :
  1 < mR * lam⁻¹ ∧ mR / (mR * lam⁻¹) = lam ∧ lam < mS

With μ^log(S) > 0 > λ > μ^log(R): ρ = μ^log(R) · λ⁻¹ > 1, the gluing x ↦ x / ρ sends μ^log(R) to λ, and μ^log(S) > λ. The third conjunct λ < μ^log(S) follows from μ^log(S) > 0 > λ alone, without the gluing (logVolS_gt_lambda). So this comparison adds no evidence about the gluing: the "logical consistency" that the report describes is built into the admissibility conditions on λ.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVExMeasure.logVolS_gt_lambdatheorem

theorem logVolS_gt_lambda {mS lam : ℝ} (hS : 0 < mS) (hlam : lam < 0) : lam < mS

The inequality μ^log(S) > λ is a consequence of the hypotheses on λ alone; it does not use the gluing.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx

IUT/Audit/MochizukiResponses/SSIdEx.lean

Mochizuki's example (SSIdEx): two complex structures on ℝ²

Module documentation

Source: S. Mochizuki, Report on Discussions, Held During the Period March 15 to 20, 2018, Concerning Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory (IUTch), version dated February 2019, item (SSIdEx), pages 17 to 19. Read for this file: report pages 17 to 19; the formulas of (SSIdEx) were checked against the rendered pages 17 and 18. (The text extraction drops the slash of the ≠ in "(φ₁)_ℂ ∘ ι_U ≠ ι_W ∘ φ₂"; the rendered page 18 shows ≠.)

Whose argument. Mochizuki offers (SSIdEx) as an elementary model of the logical structure of his objection (SSIdFs) to the "id-version" (SSId) of the Θ-link that he attributes to Scholze and Stix: V₁ = ℝ e₁ plays the unit group portion, V₂ = ℝ e₂ the value group portion, the U-structure the q-holomorphic structure, the W-structure the Θ-holomorphic structure.

The example. V = ℝ², e₁ = (1,0), e₂ = (0,1), 0 < t < 1. The U-structure is i e₁ = e₂, i e₂ = −e₁; the W-structure is i e₁ = t e₂, i e₂ = −t⁻¹ e₁. The map φ : U → W is the identity of V. ι_U : U₂ → (U₁)_ℂ sends e₂ ↦ i e₁, and ι_W : W₂ → (W₁)_ℂ sends t e₂ ↦ i e₁; they are the restrictions of the ℂ-linear isomorphisms ζ_U : U → (U₁)_ℂ, ζ_W : W → (W₁)_ℂ that restrict to the identity on U₁, W₁.

Modeling choices. (U₁)_ℂ and (W₁)_ℂ are both modeled by ℂ with e₁ ↦ 1, so that ζ_U (x, y) = x + y i and ζ_W (x, y) = x + (y / t) i. This file proves that these are the ℂ-linear maps described in the report, and then checks:

  • (≇): there is no isomorphism of the data (U, U₁, U₂, e₁, e₂, ι_U) and (W, W₁, W₂, e₁, e₂, ι_W) when t ≠ 1 (not_iso_of_ne_one);
  • (Dilat): in the coordinates ζ_U, ζ_W, φ becomes z ↦ a z + b z̄ with b / a = (t − 1)/(t + 1) ≠ 0, while (φ₁)_ℂ is the identity of ℂ, with zero antiholomorphic part; the maximal dilatation of φ is 1/t.

What this file does not do. It checks the example, which is elementary linear algebra. Whether the id-version (SSId) of the Θ-link stands to the Θ-link of IUT as [Φ] stands to Φ here is the analogy Mochizuki asserts; it is not a statement about vector spaces and is not addressed. This file does not settle the dispute.

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.Vabbrev

abbrev V := ℝ × ℝ

The real vector space V = ℝ².

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.e₁def

def e₁ : V := (1, 0)

e₁ = (1, 0).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.e₂def

def e₂ : V := (0, 1)

e₂ = (0, 1).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JUdef

def JU : V →ₗ[ℝ] V where
  toFun v := (-v.2, v.1)
  map_add' _ _ := by ext <;> simp; ring
  map_smul' _ _ := by simp

Multiplication by i in the U-structure: (x, y) ↦ (−y, x).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JWdef

noncomputable def JW (t : ℝ) : V →ₗ[ℝ] V where
  toFun v := (-(v.2 / t), t * v.1)
  map_add' _ _ := by ext <;> simp <;> ring
  map_smul' _ _ := by ext <;> simp <;> ring

Multiplication by i in the W-structure: (x, y) ↦ (−y/t, t x).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JU_e₁theorem

theorem JU_e₁ : JU e₁ = e₂

The U-structure as stated: i e₁ = e₂, i e₂ = −e₁.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JU_e₂theorem

theorem JU_e₂ : JU e₂ = -e₁

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JW_e₁theorem

theorem JW_e₁ (t : ℝ) : JW t e₁ = t • e₂

The W-structure as stated: i e₁ = t e₂, i e₂ = −t⁻¹ e₁.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JW_e₂theorem

theorem JW_e₂ (t : ℝ) : JW t e₂ = -(t⁻¹ • e₁)

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JU_sqtheorem

theorem JU_sq (v : V) : JU (JU v) = -v

Both are complex structures: J² = −1 (for W, when t ≠ 0).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JW_sqtheorem

theorem JW_sq {t : ℝ} (ht : t ≠ 0) (v : V) : JW t (JW t v) = -v

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.JW_ne_JUtheorem

theorem JW_ne_JU {t : ℝ} (ht : t ≠ 1) : JW t ≠ JU

φ = id_V is ℂ-linear from U to W only if t = 1: the two structures differ.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ζUdef

noncomputable def ζU (v : V) : ℂ := (v.1 : ℂ) + (v.2 : ℂ) * I

ζ_U : U → (U₁)_ℂ, in the model (U₁)_ℂ = ℂ, e₁ ↦ 1.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ζWdef

noncomputable def ζW (t : ℝ) (v : V) : ℂ := (v.1 : ℂ) + ((v.2 / t : ℝ) : ℂ) * I

ζ_W : W → (W₁)_ℂ, in the model (W₁)_ℂ = ℂ, e₁ ↦ 1.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ζU_JUtheorem

theorem ζU_JU (v : V) : ζU (JU v) = I * ζU v

ζ_U is ℂ-linear for the U-structure.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ζW_JWtheorem

theorem ζW_JW {t : ℝ} (ht : t ≠ 0) (v : V) : ζW t (JW t v) = I * ζW t v

ζ_W is ℂ-linear for the W-structure.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ζU_e₁theorem

theorem ζU_e₁ : ζU e₁ = 1

Both restrict to the identity on the first factor: ζ (e₁) = 1.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ζW_e₁theorem

theorem ζW_e₁ (t : ℝ) : ζW t e₁ = 1

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ιU_e₂theorem

theorem ιU_e₂ : ζU e₂ = I

ι_U = ζ_U|_{U₂} maps e₂ ↦ i e₁.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.ιW_te₂theorem

theorem ιW_te₂ {t : ℝ} (ht : t ≠ 0) : ζW t (t • e₂) = I

ι_W = ζ_W|_{W₂} maps t e₂ ↦ i e₁.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.not_iso_of_ne_onetheorem

theorem not_iso_of_ne_one {t : ℝ} (ht : t ≠ 1) :
  ¬ ∃ (ψ : V →ₗ[ℝ] V) (χ : ℂ →ₗ[ℂ] ℂ), ψ e₁ = e₁ ∧ ψ e₂ = e₂ ∧ χ 1 = 1 ∧
    ∀ s : ℝ, χ (ζU (s • e₂)) = ζW t (ψ (s • e₂))

(≇): for t ≠ 1 there is no isomorphism between (U, U₁, U₂, e₁, e₂, ι_U) and (W, W₁, W₂, e₁, e₂, ι_W). An isomorphism consists of a real-linear ψ : V → V fixing the distinguished elements e₁, e₂ and a ℂ-linear χ : (U₁)_ℂ → (W₁)_ℂ extending ψ|_{U₁} (so χ 1 = 1), compatible with the ι's: χ (ι_U v) = ι_W (ψ v) on U₂.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.iso_of_eq_onetheorem

theorem iso_of_eq_one : ∃ (ψ : V →ₗ[ℝ] V) (χ : ℂ →ₗ[ℂ] ℂ), ψ e₁ = e₁ ∧ ψ e₂ = e₂ ∧ χ 1 = 1 ∧
  ∀ s : ℝ, χ (ζU (s • e₂)) = ζW 1 (ψ (s • e₂))

For t = 1 the identity is such an isomorphism, so the condition t ≠ 1 is needed.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.φcoorddef

noncomputable def φcoord (t : ℝ) (z : ℂ) : ℂ := (z.re : ℂ) + ((z.im / t : ℝ) : ℂ) * I

φ = id_V read in the coordinates ζ_U, ζ_W: the map ℂ → ℂ, x + y i ↦ x + (y / t) i.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.φcoord_ζUtheorem

theorem φcoord_ζU (t : ℝ) (v : V) : φcoord t (ζU v) = ζW t v

φcoord is indeed ζ_W ∘ φ ∘ ζ_U⁻¹.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.φcoord_eqtheorem

theorem φcoord_eq (t : ℝ) (z : ℂ) :
  φcoord t z = (((1 + t⁻¹) / 2 : ℝ) : ℂ) * z + (((1 - t⁻¹) / 2 : ℝ) : ℂ) * (starRingEnd ℂ) z

(Dilat), computation: φcoord z = a z + b z̄ with a = (1 + t⁻¹)/2, b = (1 − t⁻¹)/2.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.antiholo_ne_zerotheorem

theorem antiholo_ne_zero {t : ℝ} (ht : t ≠ 1) : (1 - t⁻¹) / 2 ≠ 0

The antiholomorphic coefficient b = (1 − t⁻¹)/2 vanishes only for t = 1; so for 0 < t < 1 the map φ is not holomorphic, while (φ₁)_ℂ = id_ℂ has b = 0.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.beltramitheorem

theorem beltrami {t : ℝ} (ht : 0 < t) : ((1 - t⁻¹) / 2) / ((1 + t⁻¹) / 2) = (t - 1) / (t + 1)

The Beltrami coefficient μ = b / a of φ equals (t − 1)/(t + 1).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.SSIdEx.dilatationtheorem

theorem dilatation {t : ℝ} (ht : 0 < t) (ht1 : t < 1) :
  (|(1 + t⁻¹) / 2| + |(1 - t⁻¹) / 2|) / (|(1 + t⁻¹) / 2| - |(1 - t⁻¹) / 2|) = 1 / t

The maximal dilatation K = (|a| + |b|)/(|a| − |b|) of φ equals 1/t for 0 < t < 1; it is 1 (conformal) only when t = 1.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels

IUT/Audit/MochizukiResponses/ToyModels.lean

Mochizuki's elementary models of the logical structure: (Smm) and the toy model

Module documentation

Sources:

  • S. Mochizuki, Report on Discussions, Held During the Period March 15 to 20, 2018, Concerning Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory (IUTch), version dated February 2019, item (Smm), page 2. Read for this file: report page 2, checked against the rendered page.
  • S. Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, May 2020 manuscript, Remark 3.12.2, (ii), items (a^toy) to (f^toy), pages 189 to 191. Pages 187 to 195 were read; pages 190 and 191 were checked against the rendered pages. The report cites these "toy models" in its item (LbLV), page 24.

Whose argument. Both are Mochizuki's. They are offered as "[very rough!]" models of the logical structure of the argument of IUT III, Corollary 3.12, Step (xi), and of what he calls the misunderstanding of Scholze and Stix.

What is proved. The arithmetic of the two models: in (Smm), the defining relation −2B = −A and the "theorem" −2B ≤ −2A + 1 give A ≤ 1, while adding A = B forces A = B = 0; in the toy model, the incompatibility −h ≠ −2h for h > 0 and the formal step −h ∈ ℝ_{≤ −2h + ε} ⟹ h ≤ ε.

Observation (assessment of this project). In both models every nontrivial input is a hypothesis: the "theorem" −2B ≤ −2A + 1 in (Smm), and the membership −h ∈ ℝ_{≤ −2h + ε} in (e^toy), which Remark 3.12.2 introduces with "suppose that one verifies". The corresponding assertions about IUT are IUT III, Theorem 3.11 and Corollary 3.12, Steps (xi-b) to (xi-f). These models do not test them, and this file does not settle the dispute.

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.smm_boundtheorem

theorem smm_bound {A B : ℝ} (hdef : -2 * B = -A) (hthm : -2 * B ≤ -2 * A + 1) : A ≤ 1

(Smm): from the defining relation −2B = −A (the Θ-link) and the "theorem" −2B ≤ −2A + 1 (the multiradial representation), A ≤ 1 (Corollary 3.12).

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.smm_identifytheorem

theorem smm_identify {A B : ℝ} (hdef : -2 * B = -A) (hAB : A = B) : A = 0 ∧ B = 0

(Smm): adding the identification A = B to the defining relation forces A = B = 0.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.smm_contradictiontheorem

theorem smm_contradiction {A B : ℝ} (hA : 0 < A) (hdef : -2 * B = -A) : A ≠ B

(Smm): hence A = B is incompatible with A > 0, the "initial assumption".

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.toy_incompatibletheorem

theorem toy_incompatible {h : ℝ} (hh : 0 < h) : -h ≠ -2 * h

(a^toy): without the labels q, Θ, the two assignments ∗ ↦ −h and ∗ ↦ −2h contradict each other: −h ≠ −2h for h > 0.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.toy_labeledtheorem

theorem toy_labeled (h : ℝ) :
  ∃ f : Bool → ℝ, f true = -h ∧ f false = -2 * h

With the labels kept, both assignments hold simultaneously: a function on the two labels.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.leSetdef

def leSet (c : ℝ) : Set ℝ := {x | x ≤ c}

ℝ_{≤ c} = {x ∈ ℝ | x ≤ c}.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.toy_boundtheorem

theorem toy_bound {h ε : ℝ} (hmem : -h ∈ leSet (-2 * h + ε)) : h ≤ ε

(f^toy): if −h ∈ ℝ_{≤ −2h + ε} (the hypothesis of (e^toy)), then h ≤ ε.

IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.ToyModels.stepXIf_formaltheorem

theorem stepXIf_formal {negLogQ negLogTheta : ℝ} :
  negLogQ ∈ leSet negLogTheta ↔ negLogQ ≤ negLogTheta

The analogue in the notation of IUT III, Corollary 3.12, Step (xi-f): the inclusion −|log(q)| ∈ ℝ_{≤ −|log(Θ)|} is the inequality −|log(q)| ≤ −|log(Θ)|.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Blurring

IUT/Audit/ScholzeStix/Blurring.lean · imports IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Section1_3, IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Diagram

Scholze–Stix, end of Section 2.2: the size of the "blurring"

Module documentation

Source: P. Scholze and J. Stix, Why abc is still a conjecture, manuscript dated July 16, 2018, Section 1.3 (page 4, displays (1.5), (1.6)) and the last paragraphs of Section 2.2 (page 10). Read in full; the formulas were checked against the rendered pages 4 and 10. Also S. Mochizuki, Comments on the Manuscript (2018-08 Version) by Scholze–Stix, September 2018, item (C14), page 4, read for this file in the extracted text.

Whose argument. Scholze and Stix report that Mochizuki "claimed that up to the 'blurring' given by certain indeterminacies the diagram does commute", and reply that "this statement means that the blurring must be by a factor of at least O(ℓ²) rendering the inequality thus obtained useless" (page 10). They do not spell out the computation.

Three claims kept apart.

  1. Arithmetic of the average (proved here, no hypothesis). With ℓ = 2ℓ* + 1, the average (1/ℓ*) ∑_{j ≤ ℓ*} j² equals ℓ(ℓ + 1)/12 (avgSq_eq), and the average (1.5) with the factor j² differs from the average (1.6) without it exactly by multiplying the deg(q_E) term by that average (withSquares_eq_withoutSquares_scaled).
  2. The hypothesis (Lin) (assumed here, disputed). Every identification of two copies of ℝ in the diagram of Section 2.2 is multiplication by a positive scalar; this is the structure IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.LinIdent. A "blurring by the factor B" is modeled by the choice ConsistentUpTo B: the product of the scalars around the cycle lies in [1/B, B]. Neither (Lin) nor this tolerance is derived from the papers. Under them, consistentUpTo_avgSq_iff evaluates the condition for a cycle carrying the averaged scalar on one edge: it holds exactly when 1/B ≤ ℓ(ℓ + 1)/12 ≤ B. The bound ℓ(ℓ + 1)/12 ≤ B is therefore the hypothesis ConsistentUpTo B itself, after the product of the scalars and the average have been computed; it is not derived from anything else.
  3. Mochizuki's objection (not modeled here). In his comments on the 2018-08 version, item (C14), page 4, Mochizuki states that linear relations do exist between the copies of ℝ arising via the Θ-link from the prime strips, but that the relation between log-volumes of regions subject to indeterminacies and regions not subject to them is "highly non-linear"; he refers to his report, items (MlLV), (LVEx), (DsInd). The example (LVEx) (IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.not_linear) is a nonlinear example in the plane; it is not a construction of the actual comparison of IUT III, Corollary 3.12.

What this file is not. It does not formalize Mochizuki's notion of indeterminacy or of a "blurred" diagram, and it does not show that the indeterminacies (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3) act on any copy of ℝ by a scalar. Whether a blurring of size ℓ(ℓ + 1)/12 makes the inequality of Corollary 3.12 useless, as Scholze and Stix assert, is a quantitative claim about how such a blurring would enter (1.4); this file does not supply that computation. It does not settle the dispute.

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.ConsistentUpTodef

def ConsistentUpTo (B : ℝ) (cycle : List LinIdent) : Prop :=
  1 / B ≤ (cycle.map (·.scalar)).prod ∧ (cycle.map (·.scalar)).prod ≤ B

A cycle of identifications closes up to a "blurring" by the factor B: the product of its scalars lies in [1/B, B].

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.consistentUpTo_one_ifftheorem

theorem consistentUpTo_one_iff (cycle : List LinIdent) :
  ConsistentUpTo 1 cycle ↔ Consistent cycle

ConsistentUpTo 1 is the same as Consistent.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.blur_boundtheorem

theorem blur_bound (s : LinIdent) (n : ℕ) (B : ℝ) :
  ConsistentUpTo B (s :: List.replicate n LinIdent.canonical) ↔ 1 / B ≤ s.scalar ∧ s.scalar ≤ B

One edge with scalar s, all others canonical: the cycle closes up to the factor B exactly when 1/B ≤ s ≤ B.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.avgSqdef

noncomputable def avgSq (lh : ℕ) : ℝ := (1 / (lh : ℝ)) * ∑ j ∈ Finset.Icc 1 lh, (j : ℝ) ^ 2

The average of j² over j = 1, …, ℓ*.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.avgSq_eqtheorem

theorem avgSq_eq {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) {l : ℝ} (hl : l = 2 * lh + 1) :
  avgSq lh = l * (l + 1) / 12

With ℓ = 2ℓ* + 1, the average of j² is ℓ(ℓ + 1)/12.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.consistentUpTo_avgSq_ifftheorem

theorem consistentUpTo_avgSq_iff {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) {l : ℝ} (hl : l = 2 * lh + 1) (n : ℕ)
  (B : ℝ) (hpos : 0 < avgSq lh) :
  ConsistentUpTo B (⟨avgSq lh, hpos⟩ :: List.replicate n LinIdent.canonical) ↔
    1 / B ≤ l * (l + 1) / 12 ∧ l * (l + 1) / 12 ≤ B

Under (Lin) and the tolerance model ConsistentUpTo: a cycle whose one edge carries the averaged scalar avgSq ℓ* and whose other edges are canonical closes up to the factor B exactly when 1/B ≤ ℓ(ℓ + 1)/12 ≤ B. This is an evaluation of the hypothesis ConsistentUpTo B, not a derivation of a lower bound on B from other premises.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.withSquares_eq_withoutSquares_scaledtheorem

theorem withSquares_eq_withoutSquares_scaled {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) (l d qE : ℝ) :
  thetaWithSquares lh l d qE = ((lh : ℝ) + 1) / 2 * d - avgSq lh * (qE / (2 * l)) ∧
    thetaWithoutSquares lh l d qE = ((lh : ℝ) + 1) / 2 * d - 1 * (qE / (2 * l))

The j-th summand form of (1.5) and (1.6) differ only in the q term, and after averaging the q term of (1.5) is the q term of (1.6) multiplied by avgSq ℓ*: thetaWithSquares = (ℓ* + 1)/2 · d − avgSq ℓ* · qE/(2ℓ) and thetaWithoutSquares = (ℓ* + 1)/2 · d − qE/(2ℓ).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Diagram

IUT/Audit/ScholzeStix/Diagram.lean

Scholze–Stix, Section 2.2: consistency of identifications of copies of ℝ

Module documentation

Source: Scholze and Stix, Why abc is still a conjecture (July 16, 2018), Sections 2.1.4 to 2.2, pages 7 to 10. Read in full.

The objection concerns six ordered one-dimensional real vector spaces arranged in a cycle: the spaces of the abstract Θ- and q-pilot objects, the spaces of the concrete pilot objects, and two copies of the standard real numbers. Scholze and Stix argue that a meaningful inequality needs all of them identified consistently, and that inserting the scalar j² on one edge makes the cycle inconsistent.

This file states the elementary algebra behind that argument and nothing more.

Hypothesis the objection rests on. Every identification between two of the copies is an ordered linear isomorphism, that is, multiplication by a positive scalar. Mochizuki rejects exactly this hypothesis, calling it "(Lin)" in his comments of September 2018, item (C14): he states that when indeterminacies are involved the relationship between log-volumes is not linear. See IUT.Audit.MochizukiResponses.LVEx. So the theorems below are conditional on (Lin) and do not settle the dispute.

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.LinIdentstructure

structure LinIdent where
  scalar : ℝ
  pos : 0 < scalar

An ordered linear identification of two copies of ℝ: multiplication by a positive scalar. This is the hypothesis "(Lin)".

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Consistentdef

def Consistent (cycle : List LinIdent) : Prop := (cycle.map (·.scalar)).prod = 1

A cycle of identifications is consistent when going around it returns every element to itself, that is, when the product of the scalars is 1.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.LinIdent.canonicaldef

def LinIdent.canonical : LinIdent := ⟨1, one_pos⟩

The canonical identification, scalar 1.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.consistent_canonicaltheorem

theorem consistent_canonical (n : ℕ) : Consistent (List.replicate n LinIdent.canonical)

The cycle with canonical identifications on every edge is consistent. Scholze and Stix: "There is one consistent choice of isomorphisms given by using the natural isomorphisms".

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.scalar_eq_one_of_consistenttheorem

theorem scalar_eq_one_of_consistent (c : LinIdent) (n : ℕ)
  (h : Consistent (c :: List.replicate n LinIdent.canonical)) : c.scalar = 1

If one edge carries the scalar c and all other edges are canonical, the cycle is consistent only if c = 1.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.not_consistent_squaretheorem

theorem not_consistent_square {j : ℕ} (hj : 2 ≤ j) (n : ℕ) :
  ¬ Consistent (⟨(j : ℝ) ^ 2, by positivity⟩ :: List.replicate n LinIdent.canonical)

The case at issue: the scalar j² for j ≥ 2. Under (Lin), with the other edges canonical, the cycle has "monodromy j²" and is inconsistent.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Reduction

IUT/Audit/ScholzeStix/Reduction.lean

Scholze–Stix, Section 1.2: from Claim 6 to Vojta's inequality, as real analysis

Module documentation

Source: P. Scholze and J. Stix, Why abc is still a conjecture, manuscript dated July 16, 2018, Section 1.2, pages 2 and 3 (displays (1.2), (1.3), Claim 6, and the two displays after it). Read in full; the formulas were checked against the rendered pages 2 and 3.

Whose argument. This is the reduction that Scholze and Stix write down to explain why Claim 6 (their summary of IUT IV, Theorem 1.10) would imply the uniform Vojta inequality for elliptic curves, and hence abc. Neither side disputes this step. Mochizuki's comments on the 2018-08 version, item (C1), pages 1 and 2, concern the justification of Remark 5 (page 3), which Scholze and Stix invoke for the final O(1).

What this file is not. It is not a formalization of Claim 6, of IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, of the Belyi reduction, of heights, or of Faltings' theorem. Every arithmetic quantity (h(P), deg(q_E), d(P) + n¹_∞(P), ℓ(P)) is an arbitrary real number or natural number attached to an index P, and (1.2), (1.3), Claim 6 are hypotheses. The file checks only the real analysis that turns those hypotheses into the conclusion. It does not settle the dispute, which concerns whether Claim 6 is proved.

Hypotheses, stated exactly. For an index type ι (the curves E/k "as above"), with hgt P = h(P), Q P = (1/6) deg(q_E), N P = d(P) + n¹_∞(P), ℓ P = ℓ(P):

  • (1.2), upper half, as Scholze and Stix use it: h ≤ Q + c (h^{1/2} + 1) with c > 0;
  • (1.3): h^{1/2} ≤ ℓ ≤ h^{1/2} (c₁ + c₂ log h) with c₁, c₂ > 0;
  • Claim 6: Q ≤ (1 + α(ℓ)) N + β(ℓ) ℓ, with α(ℓ) → 0 and β bounded for large ℓ;
  • h > 0 and N ≥ 0. These two are not displayed in the manuscript. N ≥ 0 holds for the intended quantities (a log root discriminant and a log conductor are nonnegative) and is needed in the step "we conclude Vojta's inequality"; without it the step fails.

Two observations recorded by this file (assessment of this project, not of either party).

  1. The display after Claim 6 uses β_d(ℓ) ℓ ≤ β_d(ℓ) h^{1/2} (c₁ + c₂ log h), which needs β_d(ℓ) ≥ 0. The manuscript only says β_d(ℓ) = O_d(1). This is harmless: Claim 6 with β implies Claim 6 with |β|, and display_after_claim6_abs proves the display with |β|.
  2. The final O(1) does not need the finiteness statement of Remark 5. Once Vojta's inequality holds for h(P) ≥ H, the points with h(P) < H satisfy h(P) < H ≤ (1 + ε) N + H because N ≥ 0. See vojta_of_claim6, which uses no finiteness hypothesis: the only property of N = d(P) + n¹_∞(P) (the different plus conductor term) that it uses is the lower bound N ≥ 0. So the dispute in Mochizuki's comment (C1) on Remark 5 does not affect the validity of this reduction.
  3. Remark 5 asserts finiteness of the curves E/k "as above" (over the varying fields k with [k : ℚ] ≤ d) with h(P) ≤ b, via Faltings' theorem and finite fibers of the Weil restriction. Comment (C1) argues through the j-invariant and finiteness of algebraic numbers of bounded degree and bounded height (Northcott's theorem). That argument controls the moduli values j(E); it does not by itself give finiteness of curves, since infinitely many quadratic twists over ℚ share one j-invariant. Any claim about curves, or about the fibers of E/k ↦ R_{k/ℚ}(E), needs its own argument using the restrictions "as above". This file needs neither.
Public declarations

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.display_after_claim6theorem

theorem display_after_claim6 {h Q N ℓ α β c c₁ c₂ : ℝ} (hh : 0 < h) (hβ : 0 ≤ β)
  (h12 : h ≤ Q + c * (√h + 1)) (hC6 : Q ≤ (1 + α) * N + β * ℓ)
  (h13 : ℓ ≤ √h * (c₁ + c₂ * Real.log h)) :
  h * (1 - (c * (1 + (√h)⁻¹) + β * (c₁ + c₂ * Real.log h)) / √h) ≤ (1 + α) * N

The display after Claim 6 (page 3), for one point, assuming β ≥ 0: h · (1 − (c (1 + h^{−1/2}) + β (c₁ + c₂ log h)) / h^{1/2}) ≤ (1 + α) N. Hypotheses: h12 is (1.2) in the form Scholze and Stix use, hC6 is Claim 6, h13 is the upper half of (1.3).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.display_after_claim6_abstheorem

theorem display_after_claim6_abs {h Q N ℓ α β c c₁ c₂ : ℝ} (hh : 0 < h) (hℓ : 0 ≤ ℓ)
  (h12 : h ≤ Q + c * (√h + 1)) (hC6 : Q ≤ (1 + α) * N + β * ℓ)
  (h13 : ℓ ≤ √h * (c₁ + c₂ * Real.log h)) :
  h * (1 - (c * (1 + (√h)⁻¹) + |β| * (c₁ + c₂ * Real.log h)) / √h) ≤ (1 + α) * N

The same display with |β| in place of β, for arbitrary real β. Claim 6 with β implies Claim 6 with |β| because ℓ ≥ 0.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.vojta_pointwisetheorem

theorem vojta_pointwise {h N α ε M : ℝ} (hε : 0 < ε) (hN : 0 ≤ N)
  (hdisp : h * M ≤ (1 + α) * N) (hα : 0 < 1 + α) (hlt : 1 + α < M * (1 + ε)) :
  h ≤ (1 + ε) * N

The step "we conclude Vojta's inequality" (page 3), for one point: if h · M ≤ (1 + α) N and 0 < 1 + α < M (1 + ε) with ε > 0, and N ≥ 0, then h ≤ (1 + ε) N. Here M stands for the factor 1 − (…)/h^{1/2} of the previous display.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.vojta_of_claim6theorem

theorem vojta_of_claim6 {ι : Type*} (hgt Q N : ι → ℝ) (ℓ : ι → ℕ) (α β : ℕ → ℝ)
  {c c₁ c₂ : ℝ} (hpos : ∀ P, 0 < hgt P) (hN : ∀ P, 0 ≤ N P)
  (h12 : ∀ P, hgt P ≤ Q P + c * (√(hgt P) + 1))
  (h13lo : ∀ P, √(hgt P) ≤ ℓ P)
  (h13hi : ∀ P, (ℓ P : ℝ) ≤ √(hgt P) * (c₁ + c₂ * Real.log (hgt P)))
  (hC6 : ∀ P, Q P ≤ (1 + α (ℓ P)) * N P + β (ℓ P) * ℓ P)
  (hα : Tendsto α atTop (𝓝 0)) (hβ : ∃ B L₀, ∀ n ≥ L₀, |β n| ≤ B) :
  ∀ ε > 0, ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ P, hgt P ≤ (1 + ε) * N P + C

The reduction of Section 1.2. Scholze and Stix: "the inequalities of Claim 6 and (1.3) yield [the display]. … As soon as for large h(P) … we conclude Vojta's inequality h(P) ≤ (1 + ε)(d(P) + n¹_∞(P)) + O_{ε,d}(1)." Hypotheses, uniform over the index type ι: hgt P > 0, N P ≥ 0, (1.2) as used, (1.3), and Claim 6 with α ℓ → 0 and |β ℓ| ≤ B for ℓ ≥ L₀. Conclusion: for every ε > 0 there is a constant C with hgt P ≤ (1 + ε) N P + C for all P. No finiteness hypothesis (Remark 5) is used.

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Section1_3

IUT/Audit/ScholzeStix/Section1_3.lean

Scholze–Stix, Section 1.3: the arithmetic of displays (1.4), (1.5), (1.6)

Module documentation

Source: P. Scholze and J. Stix, Why abc is still a conjecture, manuscript dated July 16, 2018, Section 1.3, pages 3 and 4. Read in full.

Scholze and Stix summarize the passage from IUT III, Corollary 3.12, to IUT IV, Theorem 1.10, by an "essential approximation" of − |log(Θ)|. This file checks the real arithmetic of that summary. It takes no position on whether the approximation (1.5) or its variant (1.6) describes what IUT III proves; that is the disputed point, and it is not arithmetic.

Notation: l is the prime, lh = (l − 1)/2 is their ℓ^>, d is the log root discriminant d(P), qE is deg(q_E). Display (1.4) is − |log(q)| ≤ − |log(Θ)| with − |log(q)| = − qE / (2 l).

Public declarations

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.thetaWithSquaresdef

noncomputable def thetaWithSquares (lh : ℕ) (l d qE : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (1 / (lh : ℝ)) * ∑ j ∈ Finset.Icc 1 lh, ((j : ℝ) * d - (j : ℝ) ^ 2 * (qE / (2 * l)))

The average over j = 1, …, ℓ^> in display (1.5), with the factor j² on the q term: (1/ℓ^>) ∑_j (j·d − j²·qE/(2ℓ)).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.thetaWithoutSquaresdef

noncomputable def thetaWithoutSquares (lh : ℕ) (l d qE : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (1 / (lh : ℝ)) * ∑ j ∈ Finset.Icc 1 lh, ((j : ℝ) * d - qE / (2 * l))

The average in display (1.6), "with the factor j² missing": (1/ℓ^>) ∑_j (j·d − qE/(2ℓ)).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.display_1_5theorem

theorem display_1_5 {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) {l : ℝ} (hl : l = 2 * lh + 1) (d qE : ℝ) :
  thetaWithSquares lh l d qE = ((lh : ℝ) + 1) / 2 * (d - qE / 6)

Display (1.5): with l = 2·ℓ^> + 1, the average with squares equals (ℓ^> + 1)/2 · (d − qE/6).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.display_1_6theorem

theorem display_1_6 {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) (l d qE : ℝ) :
  thetaWithoutSquares lh l d qE = ((lh : ℝ) + 1) / 2 * d - qE / (2 * l)

Display (1.6): the average without squares equals (ℓ^> + 1)/2 · d − qE/(2ℓ).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.consequence_with_squarestheorem

theorem consequence_with_squares {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) {l : ℝ} (hl : l = 2 * lh + 1)
  {d qE : ℝ} (h14 : -(qE / (2 * l)) ≤ thetaWithSquares lh l d qE) :
  1 / 6 * qE * (1 - 12 / (l * (l + 1))) ≤ d

Substituting (1.5) into (1.4): (1/6)·qE·(1 − 12/(ℓ(ℓ+1))) ≤ d. Scholze and Stix write this as (1/6) deg(q_E) ≲ (1 − 12/(ℓ(ℓ+1)))⁻¹ · d(P).

IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.consequence_without_squarestheorem

theorem consequence_without_squares {lh : ℕ} (hlh : 1 ≤ lh) (l d qE : ℝ) :
  -(qE / (2 * l)) ≤ thetaWithoutSquares lh l d qE ↔ 0 ≤ d

Substituting (1.6) into (1.4): the inequality is equivalent to 0 ≤ d, which Scholze and Stix call "essentially free of content". The q terms cancel exactly.

Countermodels

Checked examples that refute simplified abstractions, and the positive results and lemmas recorded beside them in data/countermodels.json. None is a counterexample to a statement of the papers.

IUT.Countermodels.Basic

IUT/Countermodels/Basic.lean

Countermodels to simplified abstractions

Module documentation

Each statement here is a small, fully checked example showing that some general principle fails. None of them is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. They refute abstractions: principles that an argument might be tempted to use and that are false without further hypotheses. In the classification of the project they are all of the kind "counterexample to a simplified abstraction". For each one the docstring says what the example satisfies and which question about the papers it bears on.

Where a principle of the same flavor is true, the positive statement is proved next to the countermodel, so that legitimate identifications are not ruled out by accident.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.mul_iso_not_additivetheorem

theorem mul_iso_not_additive :
  ∃ f : ZMod 5 → ZMod 5, Function.Bijective f ∧ f 0 = 0 ∧ f 1 = 1 ∧
    (∀ x y, f (x * y) = f x * f y) ∧ ¬ ∀ x y, f (x + y) = f x + f y

On the field ZMod 5, cubing is a bijection that preserves 0, 1 and multiplication but not addition. So an isomorphism of multiplicative monoids need not be a ring isomorphism: data that keeps only the multiplicative structure of a ring does not determine its additive structure. Bears on: IUT III distinguishes links that preserve multiplicative data only (the Θ-link) from ring isomorphisms. The example shows the distinction is not vacuous already for finite fields. It says nothing about whether additive structure can be reconstructed from richer data such as a Galois action.

IUT.Countermodels.separate_witnesses_no_jointtheorem

theorem separate_witnesses_no_joint :
  ∃ P Q : Bool → Prop, (∃ x, P x) ∧ (∃ x, Q x) ∧ ¬ ∃ x, P x ∧ Q x

Two existence statements, each true, with no common witness. Existence of an object compatible with condition P and existence of an object compatible with condition Q do not give one object compatible with both. Bears on: the simultaneous validity of several compatibility conditions in IUT III, Theorem 3.11, must be proved as a joint statement. It cannot be inferred from the separate ones.

IUT.Countermodels.invariant_comptheorem

theorem invariant_comp {α β : Type*} (F : α → β) (g h : α → α)
  (hg : ∀ x, F (g x) = F x) (hh : ∀ x, F (h x) = F x) : ∀ x, F (g (h x)) = F x

The positive statement: a quantity invariant under each of two transformations is invariant under their composite. Invariance under (Ind1) and under (Ind2) separately does give invariance under the combined action, for a single-valued quantity.

IUT.Countermodels.separate_orbits_ne_joint_orbittheorem

theorem separate_orbits_ne_joint_orbit :
  let g : Equiv.Perm (Fin 3) := Equiv.swap 0 1
  let h : Equiv.Perm (Fin 3) := Equiv.swap 1 2
  (h (g 0) ∉ ({0, g 0, h 0} : Finset (Fin 3)))

The countermodel: the union of the orbits of a point under two transformations taken separately can be strictly smaller than its orbit under the group they generate. On Fin 3 with the transpositions (0 1) and (1 2), the point 0 has separate orbits {0, 1} and {0}, while the composite (1 2) ∘ (0 1) sends 0 to 2. Bears on: "the union of the possible images" in IUT III, Corollary 3.12, is taken under (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3) acting together. A region that contains the images under each indeterminacy separately need not contain the images under their combination, so a volume bound must be established for the combined action.

IUT.Countermodels.intervalHulldef

def intervalHull (s : Finset (Fin 3)) : Finset (Fin 3) :=
  Finset.univ.filter fun x => (∃ a ∈ s, a ≤ x) ∧ (∃ b ∈ s, x ≤ b)

The interval hull of a finite set of Fin 3: all points between its least and greatest elements. A stand-in for any hull operation defined by an order or a ring structure.

IUT.Countermodels.hull_transport_not_commutetheorem

theorem hull_transport_not_commute :
  let σ : Equiv.Perm (Fin 3) := Equiv.swap 1 2
  let s : Finset (Fin 3) := {0, 2}
  intervalHull (s.map σ.toEmbedding) ≠ (intervalHull s).map σ.toEmbedding

Hull formation does not commute with transport along a bijection that does not respect the structure defining the hull. With s = {0, 2} and σ = (1 2): the hull of σ(s) = {0, 1} is {0, 1}, while σ of the hull of s is everything. Bears on: in Corollary 3.12 the holomorphic hull is formed relative to one arithmetic holomorphic structure (labeled 1, ∘), after transporting data that originates in another. The order of "transport" and "form the hull" matters, and the proof must say which is used.

IUT.Countermodels.additive_iso_changes_volumetheorem

theorem additive_iso_changes_volume :
  (∀ x y : ℝ, 2 * (x + y) = 2 * x + 2 * y) ∧
    MeasureTheory.volume (Set.Icc (2 * 0 : ℝ) (2 * 1)) ≠ MeasureTheory.volume (Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1)

Doubling x ↦ 2x is additive on ℝ, and the interval [2 · 0, 2 · 1] has Lebesgue measure different from that of [0, 1]. (Doubling is an automorphism of the topological group ℝ; the theorem states only its additivity, not its bijectivity or continuity.) So an isomorphism of topological groups need not preserve a chosen normalization of Haar measure. Bears on: log-volumes in IUT III and IV are normalized. Transporting a volume along an isomorphism that preserves only a weaker structure requires a separate proof that the normalization is preserved.

IUT.Countermodels.log_volume_shifttheorem

theorem log_volume_shift {c μ : ℝ} (hc : 0 < c) (hμ : 0 < μ) :
  Real.log (c * μ) = Real.log c + Real.log μ

The arithmetic behind the corresponding statement for log-volumes: for c > 0 and μ > 0, log (c μ) = log c + log μ. So rescaling a measure by c > 0 shifts the log-volume of every set of finite positive measure by log c; for a set of measure 0 or ∞ the log-volume log (μ s).toReal is 0 by totalization and no shift occurs. Two normalizations give comparable log-volumes only after the shift is accounted for.

IUT.Countermodels.CombinedIndeterminacies

IUT/Countermodels/CombinedIndeterminacies.lean

Countermodels: separate indeterminacies versus their combined action

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, read for this file from the extracted text: Theorem 3.11 (i), (ii), pages 153 to 157 (the indeterminacies (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3)); the statement of Corollary 3.12, pages 173 and 174, where − |log(Θ)| is the log-volume of the holomorphic hull of the union of the possible images of a Θ-pilot object subject to (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3); and Step (x) of its proof, page 181, which states that the procession-normalized log-volumes are invariant with respect to (Ind1) and (Ind2) and convert (Ind3) into an inequality. No formula of the papers is transcribed here.

None of the statements below is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. They separate two principles that are easy to conflate:

  • for a single-valued quantity, invariance under each of several transformations gives invariance under every transformation they generate (invariant_closure, positive; the two-step case is IUT.Countermodels.invariant_comp in Basic.lean);
  • for a set-valued construction such as "the union of the possible images", the union of the images under each indeterminacy separately can be much smaller than the union under their combined action (separate_finite_joint_infinite, countermodel).

In the countermodel each of the two indeterminacies is a reflection of the real line, a group of order 2, and the union of the images of the unit interval under either one separately has volume at most 3. The two reflections together generate the infinite dihedral group, which contains all translations by even integers, and the union of the images under it has infinite volume. So a bound on the volume of the union of possible images must be established for the combined action. The example concerns volumes of unions, not the log-volumes of regions in the papers, which Step (x) on page 181 states to be invariant under (Ind1) and (Ind2).

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.Indet.invariant_set_closuretheorem

theorem invariant_set_closure {α : Type*} (g h : Equiv.Perm α) (T : Set α)
  (hg : g '' T = T) (hh : h '' T = T) :
  ∀ σ ∈ Subgroup.closure ({g, h} : Set (Equiv.Perm α)), σ '' T = T

Positive statement. A set mapped onto itself by each of two permutations is mapped onto itself by every element of the group they generate.

IUT.Countermodels.Indet.invariant_closuretheorem

theorem invariant_closure {α β : Type*} (F : α → β) (g h : Equiv.Perm α)
  (hg : ∀ x, F (g x) = F x) (hh : ∀ x, F (h x) = F x) :
  ∀ σ ∈ Subgroup.closure ({g, h} : Set (Equiv.Perm α)), ∀ x, F (σ x) = F x

Positive statement. A function invariant under each of two permutations is invariant under every element of the group they generate. This extends IUT.Countermodels.invariant_comp of Basic.lean from one composite to the whole group.

IUT.Countermodels.Indet.reflect0def

def reflect0 : Equiv.Perm ℝ := Equiv.neg ℝ

The reflection x ↦ −x of the real line.

IUT.Countermodels.Indet.reflect1def

def reflect1 : Equiv.Perm ℝ := Equiv.subLeft 2

The reflection x ↦ 2 − x of the real line.

IUT.Countermodels.Indet.pow_reflect_applytheorem

theorem pow_reflect_apply (k : ℕ) (y : ℝ) : ((reflect1 * reflect0) ^ k) y = y + 2 * k

The composite of the two reflections is the translation by 2; its powers are the translations by even integers.

IUT.Countermodels.Indet.separate_finite_joint_infinitetheorem

theorem separate_finite_joint_infinite :
  MeasurePreserving reflect0 volume volume ∧ MeasurePreserving reflect1 volume volume ∧
    IsCompact (Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) ∧ volume (Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) = 1 ∧
    reflect0 * reflect0 = 1 ∧ reflect1 * reflect1 = 1 ∧
    volume (Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 ∪ reflect0 '' Icc 0 1 ∪ reflect1 '' Icc 0 1) ≤ 3 ∧
    volume (⋃ σ ∈ Subgroup.closure ({reflect1, reflect0} : Set (Equiv.Perm ℝ)),
      σ '' Icc (0 : ℝ) 1) = ⊤

Countermodel. Let S = [0, 1], g x = −x, h x = 2 − x. Both preserve Lebesgue measure, S is compact of volume 1, and each of g, h is an involution, so the orbit of S under each indeterminacy separately is S ∪ g S, respectively S ∪ h S, and the union of all images under the indeterminacies taken separately has volume at most 3. The union of the images of S under the group generated by g and h together has infinite volume.

IUT.Countermodels.HaarNormalization

IUT/Countermodels/HaarNormalization.lean

Countermodels: canonical reconstruction and a chosen normalization of volume

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Proposition 3.9 (i) to (iv), pages 115 to 117, read for this file; page 115 was also read from the rendered page. There, the p_v-adic log-volume on a local packet is normalized in two ways at once: the integral structure 𝒪 has log-volume 0, and multiplying a region by p_v adds −log(p_v). Proposition 3.9 (ii) states that the log-volumes can be constructed by a functorial algorithm from mono-analytic data; Theorem 3.11 (ii) (a) (page 155) and Step (x) of the proof of Corollary 3.12 (page 181) use their compatibility with the relevant isomorphisms.

None of the statements below is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. They refute the simplified abstraction "if an object is reconstructed canonically (up to isomorphism), a chosen normalization of the volume on it is reconstructed as well". On the real line, which stands in for a local field here, a Haar measure is unique only up to a positive scalar, and the scalar is not fixed by the group structure, by the topology, or by the scaling law. It is fixed by an anchor: the volume of a chosen reference set. In Proposition 3.9 (i) the anchor is the log-volume 0 of 𝒪; whether the various isomorphisms of the papers preserve that anchor is exactly what the compatibility statements there assert, and these examples show that such a compatibility is a genuine additional property.

Here logVol μ s is log (μ s) for a set of finite positive measure.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.logVoldef

noncomputable def logVol (μ : Measure ℝ) (s : Set ℝ) : ℝ := Real.log (μ s).toReal

The log-volume of a set s for a measure μ on ℝ, meaningful when 0 < μ s < ∞.

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.haar_eq_smul_volumetheorem

theorem haar_eq_smul_volume (μ : Measure ℝ) [μ.IsAddHaarMeasure] :
  μ = μ.addHaarScalarFactor volume • volume

Positive statement. Every additive Haar measure on ℝ is a scalar multiple of Lebesgue measure (isAddLeftInvariant_eq_smul). So Haar measure is canonical up to exactly one real scalar.

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.haar_eq_volume_of_anchortheorem

theorem haar_eq_volume_of_anchor (μ : Measure ℝ) [μ.IsAddHaarMeasure]
  (h : μ (Icc 0 1) = 1) : μ = volume

Positive statement. An anchor fixes the scalar: an additive Haar measure on ℝ that gives the unit interval volume 1 is Lebesgue measure. This is the analog of the requirement "the log-volume of 𝒪 is zero" in IUT III, Proposition 3.9 (i), page 115.

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.rescaled_haartheorem

theorem rescaled_haar (c : ℝ) (hc : 0 < c) :
  (ENNReal.ofReal c • volume : Measure ℝ).IsAddHaarMeasure ∧
    (c ≠ 1 → (ENNReal.ofReal c • volume : Measure ℝ) ≠ volume) ∧
    ∀ s : Set ℝ, volume s ≠ 0 → volume s ≠ ⊤ →
      logVol (ENNReal.ofReal c • volume) s = Real.log c + logVol volume s

Countermodel. For every c > 0, the measure c · volume is again an additive Haar measure on ℝ, it differs from volume when c ≠ 1, and it shifts by log c the log-volume of every set s of finite positive Lebesgue measure (volume s ≠ 0, volume s ≠ ⊤). The hypothesis is needed: for s = ∅ and c = 2 both log-volumes are 0 by the totalization of logVol. So the property "is a Haar measure on the topological group ℝ" determines the log-volume only up to an additive constant.

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.scaling_law_all_normalizationstheorem

theorem scaling_law_all_normalizations (c : ℝ) (hc : 0 < c) (a : ℝ) (ha : a ≠ 0) (s : Set ℝ)
  (h0 : volume s ≠ 0) (htop : volume s ≠ ⊤) :
  logVol (ENNReal.ofReal c • volume) (a • s) =
    Real.log |a| + logVol (ENNReal.ofReal c • volume) s

Countermodel. The scaling law "multiplying a region by a adds log |a| to its log-volume" holds for every rescaled Haar measure c · volume, whatever c > 0 is, for every a ≠ 0 and every set s of finite positive Lebesgue measure. This law is the real analog of the packet-normalization rule "multiplication by p_v adds −log(p_v)" of IUT III, Proposition 3.9 (i), page 115. Together with rescaled_haar it shows that the scaling law alone does not fix the normalization; only the anchor of haar_eq_volume_of_anchor does.

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.transport_shifts_logVoltheorem

theorem transport_shifts_logVol (a : ℝ) (ha : a ≠ 0) (s : Set ℝ) (h0 : volume s ≠ 0)
  (htop : volume s ≠ ⊤) :
  logVol (map (a * ·) volume) s = -Real.log |a| + logVol volume s

Countermodel. For a ≠ 0, transporting Lebesgue measure along the automorphism x ↦ a x of the topological group ℝ gives |a|⁻¹ · volume, and so shifts by −log |a| the log-volume of every set of finite positive Lebesgue measure. If ℝ is reconstructed only up to isomorphism of topological groups, the normalization transported from one copy to another depends on the isomorphism used.

IUT.Countermodels.Haar.transport_preserves_ifftheorem

theorem transport_preserves_iff (a : ℝ) (ha : a ≠ 0) :
  map (a * ·) volume = volume ↔ |a| = 1

Positive statement. Transport along x ↦ a x preserves Lebesgue measure exactly when |a| = 1, that is, exactly when it preserves the anchor volume [0, 1] = 1. So the normalization is carried along precisely by the isomorphisms that respect the anchor.

IUT.Countermodels.HullTransport

IUT/Countermodels/HullTransport.lean

Countermodels: hull formation and transport

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, read for this file from the extracted text: the statement of Corollary 3.12 (pages 173 and 174, "holomorphic hull" of the union of the possible images) and Steps (xi-c), (xi-d) of its proof (pages 182 and 183), where the output data of the multiradial algorithm, expressed in tensor packets of mono-analytic log-shells, is enlarged to its holomorphic hull relative to the arithmetic holomorphic structure labeled 1, ◦, so as to be expressed through localizations of arithmetic vector bundles over the rings 𝒪_{K_v}. Remark 3.9.5, where the hull is defined, was not read; this file refers to the hull only through the pages above. No formula of the papers is transcribed here.

None of the statements below is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. They refute the simplified abstraction "forming a hull commutes with transport", when the hull is taken with respect to more structure than the transport map preserves. Two hulls are used, both from Mathlib: the ℂ-linear span (a stand-in for a hull relative to a holomorphic structure, transported along a map that is only ℝ-linear, that is, mono-analytic), and the ℤ[i]-submodule generated by a set (an integral hull, an 𝒪-submodule, transported along a map that is only ℤ-linear). IUT.Countermodels.hull_transport_not_commute in Basic.lean is the finite toy version with an order hull.

The positive statement hull_transport_semilinear records the valid principle: hull formation commutes with every map that preserves the structure the hull uses, up to a surjective ring map on the scalars.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.Hull.halfConjdef

def halfConj (x : ℂ × ℂ) : ℂ × ℂ := (x.1, conj x.2)

The map (z, w) ↦ (z, conj w) on ℂ². It is ℝ-linear and bijective, but it is neither ℂ-linear nor conjugate-linear.

IUT.Countermodels.Hull.halfConj_countermodeltheorem

theorem halfConj_countermodel :
  Function.Bijective halfConj ∧ (∀ x y, halfConj (x + y) = halfConj x + halfConj y) ∧
    (∀ (r : ℝ) x, halfConj ((r : ℂ) • x) = (r : ℂ) • halfConj x) ∧
    halfConj '' (Submodule.span ℂ {((1 : ℂ), (1 : ℂ))} : Set (ℂ × ℂ)) ≠
      (Submodule.span ℂ (halfConj '' {((1 : ℂ), (1 : ℂ))}) : Set (ℂ × ℂ))

Countermodel. halfConj is bijective, additive, and commutes with real scalars, but the image of the ℂ-line spanned by (1, 1) is not the ℂ-line spanned by the image of (1, 1): the point (i, −i) lies in the first set and not in the second. So the hull relative to the complex structure of the transported set differs from the transport of the hull.

IUT.Countermodels.Hull.sheardef

def shear (z : GaussianInt) : GaussianInt := ⟨z.re + z.im, z.im⟩

The shear a + b i ↦ (a + b) + b i of the Gaussian integers: a ℤ-linear bijection that is not ℤ[i]-linear.

IUT.Countermodels.Hull.shear_countermodeltheorem

theorem shear_countermodel :
  (∀ x y, shear (x + y) = shear x + shear y) ∧ Function.Bijective shear ∧
    shear '' (Submodule.span GaussianInt {(⟨1, 1⟩ : GaussianInt)} : Set GaussianInt) ≠
      (Submodule.span GaussianInt (shear '' {(⟨1, 1⟩ : GaussianInt)}) : Set GaussianInt)

Countermodel. The shear is additive and bijective. The image under the shear of the ℤ[i]-submodule generated by 1 + i (an ideal of index 2) is not the ℤ[i]-submodule generated by the image 2 + i (an ideal of norm 5): the element 2 = (1 − i)(1 + i) is fixed by the shear, but 2 is not a multiple of 2 + i, since 5 does not divide the norm 4 of 2. So the integral hull of the transported set differs from the transport of the integral hull.

IUT.Countermodels.Hull.hull_transport_semilineartheorem

theorem hull_transport_semilinear {R R₂ M M₂ : Type*} [Semiring R] [Semiring R₂]
  [AddCommMonoid M] [AddCommMonoid M₂] [Module R M] [Module R₂ M₂] {σ : R →+* R₂}
  [RingHomSurjective σ] (f : M →ₛₗ[σ] M₂) (s : Set M) :
  (Submodule.span R s).map f = Submodule.span R₂ (f '' s)

Positive statement. Hull formation commutes with transport along any map that preserves the structure used by the hull, up to a surjective ring map σ on the scalars: for a σ-semilinear map f, the image of the R-span of s is the R₂-span of the image of s (Submodule.map_span). In particular complex conjugation applied to both coordinates of ℂ² does commute with the complex hull; halfConj fails because it is conjugate-linear on one coordinate and linear on the other. The analog for convex hulls and affine maps is AffineMap.image_convexHull.

IUT.Countermodels.JointWitness

IUT/Countermodels/JointWitness.lean

Countermodels: separate witnesses without a common realization

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Step (iii) of the proof of Corollary 3.12, page 176, read for this file: it is "of crucial importance" to relate the unit portion and the value group portion of the Θ×μ_LGP-links simultaneously, via a single link, because relating them via different links runs into the non-commutativity of the log-theta-lattice. Also Step (i), page 175 (the link as a unit portion and a value group portion), and Theorem 3.11 (i), (ii), pages 153 to 157. No formula of the papers is transcribed here.

None of the statements below is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. They refute the simplified abstraction "if a transport map compatible with condition P exists, and a transport map compatible with condition Q exists, then a transport map compatible with both exists". IUT.Countermodels.separate_witnesses_no_joint in Basic.lean gives the bare logical form; here the conditions are natural compatibility conditions on transport maps.

The positive statement joint_witness_of_product records when the abstraction is valid: when the candidate transports form a product and each condition constrains a different factor. This is the situation of a poly-isomorphism that is a product of a unit portion and a value group portion with independent conditions on the two portions, which page 176 is careful to keep inside a single link.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.Joint.pow5def

def pow5 : ZMod 7 ≃* ZMod 7 where
  toFun x := x ^ 5
  invFun x := x ^ 5
  left_inv := by decide
  right_inv := by decide
  map_mul' x y := mul_pow x y 5

The automorphism x ↦ x^5 of the multiplicative monoid of ZMod 7. It is its own inverse because x^25 = x for every x in ZMod 7.

IUT.Countermodels.Joint.zmod7_separate_not_jointtheorem

theorem zmod7_separate_not_joint :
  (∃ f : ZMod 7 ≃* ZMod 7, f 3 = 3) ∧ (∃ f : ZMod 7 ≃* ZMod 7, f 2 = 4) ∧
    ¬ ∃ f : ZMod 7 ≃* ZMod 7, f 3 = 3 ∧ f 2 = 4

Countermodel with structure. Among automorphisms of the multiplicative monoid of the field ZMod 7, the condition "fixes the generator 3" is satisfiable (by the identity) and the condition "sends 2 to 4" is satisfiable (by x ↦ x^5), but no automorphism satisfies both: 2 = 3², so fixing 3 forces 2 ↦ 2. Read the two conditions as two Kummer-type compatibilities of one transport map, each imposed on a different element. Each one separately can be met; jointly they cannot, because the elements are related by the structure that the transport map must respect.

IUT.Countermodels.Joint.linear_separate_not_jointtheorem

theorem linear_separate_not_joint {θ q : ℝ} (hθ : 0 < θ) (hq : 0 < q) :
  (∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ c * 1 = 1) ∧ (∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ c * θ = q) ∧
    ((∃ c : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ c * 1 = 1 ∧ c * θ = q) ↔ θ = q)

Countermodel with real numbers. Among ordered linear self-maps x ↦ c x (c > 0) of the real line, the normalization condition "1 ↦ 1" is satisfiable, and for given θ, q > 0 the pilot condition "θ ↦ q" is satisfiable, but both hold for one map only if θ = q. This is the shape of the transport between copies of ℝ discussed in IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Diagram: a single positive scalar cannot at once respect a fixed normalization and send one prescribed real number to a different one.

IUT.Countermodels.Joint.joint_witness_of_producttheorem

theorem joint_witness_of_product {A B : Type*} (P : A → Prop) (Q : B → Prop)
  (hP : ∃ a, P a) (hQ : ∃ b, Q b) : ∃ x : A × B, P x.1 ∧ Q x.2

Positive statement. If the candidate transports form a product A × B and each condition constrains a different factor, separate witnesses do combine into a joint one. So conditions on the unit portion and on the value group portion of one poly-isomorphism can be met independently, provided they really constrain independent portions. The countermodels above fail exactly this independence.

IUT.Countermodels.LevelMismatch

IUT/Countermodels/LevelMismatch.lean

Countermodels: an isomorphism at one level and invariants at another

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, read for this file from the extracted text: Proposition 1.2 (iii), (v) (compatibility of log-links with log-volumes, compactness of log-shells; pages 31 and 32), Proposition 3.9 (i) to (iv) (pages 115 to 117), and Step (xii) of the proof of Corollary 3.12 (pages 185 and 186), where the Θ×μ_LGP-link is said to preserve only isomorphism classes of objects of local Frobenioids, so that one cannot tell an open submodule of a log-shell from its p^N multiple. No formula of the papers is transcribed here.

None of the statements below is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. Each refutes the simplified abstraction "an isomorphism of one structure (an additive group, a field) preserves an invariant defined with the help of further structure (a norm, a valuation, an archimedean absolute value)". The papers state such compatibilities as separate assertions (for instance Proposition 1.2 (iii) and Theorem 3.11 (ii) (a)), and the examples show why they are separate.

Positive statement: real_ringEquiv_isometry. For ℝ, the field structure alone forces every automorphism to be the identity, hence to preserve the absolute value.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.Level.mulPdef

noncomputable def mulP (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] : ℚ_[p] ≃+ ℚ_[p] where
  toFun x := p * x
  invFun x := (p : ℚ_[p])⁻¹ * x
  left_inv x := by
    have : (p : ℚ_[p]) ≠ 0 := by exact_mod_cast (Fact.out : p.Prime).ne_zero
    simp [this]
  right_inv x := by
    have : (p : ℚ_[p]) ≠ 0 := by exact_mod_cast (Fact.out : p.Prime).ne_zero
    simp [this]
  map_add' x y := mul_add _ x y

Multiplication by p as an additive automorphism of ℚ_p.

IUT.Countermodels.Level.mulP_countermodeltheorem

theorem mulP_countermodel (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] :
  Continuous (mulP p) ∧ Continuous (mulP p).symm ∧
    (∀ x, ‖mulP p x‖ = (p : ℝ)⁻¹ * ‖x‖) ∧ ‖(1 : ℚ_[p])‖ = 1 ∧ ‖mulP p 1‖ < 1

Countermodel. Multiplication by p is an automorphism of the additive topological group ℚ_p (continuous with continuous inverse), and it maps the unit 1 of ℤ_p to p, which lies in the maximal ideal: it multiplies every norm by p⁻¹. So an isomorphism of the additive topological group does not preserve the p-adic norm, the valuation, or the integral structure ℤ_p. It is analogous, in the simplest setting, to the indeterminacy "an open submodule versus its p^N multiple" described in IUT III, Step (xii) of the proof of Corollary 3.12 (pages 185 and 186).

IUT.Countermodels.Level.no_ringEquiv_valuation_two_threetheorem

theorem no_ringEquiv_valuation_two_three :
  ¬ ∃ σ : ℚ ≃+* ℚ, ∀ x : ℚ, padicValRat 3 (σ x) = padicValRat 2 x

Countermodel. The valued fields (ℚ, v₂) and (ℚ, v₃) have the same underlying field and value groups ℤ, yet no field automorphism of ℚ carries one valuation to the other: any ring automorphism fixes 2, and v₃(2) = 0 ≠ 1 = v₂(2). An isomorphism of fields does not by itself carry one valuation to another; the valuation is extra structure that a link must be shown to respect.

IUT.Countermodels.Level.realEmbdef

noncomputable def realEmb : ℤ√2 →+* ℝ :=
  Zsqrtd.lift ⟨Real.sqrt 2, by rw [Real.mul_self_sqrt (by norm_num)]; norm_num⟩

The real embedding a + b√2 ↦ a + b·√2 of ℤ[√2] into ℝ.

IUT.Countermodels.Level.realEmb_applytheorem

theorem realEmb_apply (a : ℤ√2) : realEmb a = a.re + a.im * Real.sqrt 2

The real embedding on coordinates: realEmb (a + b√2) = a + b·√2.

IUT.Countermodels.Level.conj_unboundedtheorem

theorem conj_unbounded :
  Function.Involutive (starRingEnd (ℤ√2)) ∧ ∀ M : ℝ, ∃ x : ℤ√2, |realEmb x| ≤ 1 ∧ M < |realEmb (starRingEnd (ℤ√2) x)|

Countermodel. Conjugation a + b√2 ↦ a − b√2 is a ring endomorphism of ℤ[√2] (starRingEnd) and an involution, hence a ring automorphism, but it is unbounded on the elements whose real embedding has absolute value at most 1: the elements (1 − √2)^n are small, their conjugates (1 + √2)^n are arbitrarily large. So an isomorphism of rings need not respect the archimedean absolute value induced by an embedding, and transporting a region defined by an archimedean bound along it can produce an unbounded region. Bears on the archimedean part of the log-volume compatibility of IUT III, Proposition 1.2 (iii) (page 31), which the passage read asserts for the specific maps there; the example shows that such a compatibility does not follow from ring structure alone in general. Only unboundedness is proved here; discontinuity in the induced topology is not formalized.

IUT.Countermodels.Level.real_ringEquiv_isometrytheorem

theorem real_ringEquiv_isometry (σ : ℝ ≃+* ℝ) (x : ℝ) : |σ x| = |x|

Positive statement. Every ring automorphism of ℝ is the identity, hence preserves the absolute value: for ℝ, the archimedean structure is determined by the field structure. The contrast with conj_unbounded is that ℤ[√2] carries two real embeddings, and the ring structure does not single out one of them.

IUT.Countermodels.MultiplicativeAdditive

IUT/Countermodels/MultiplicativeAdditive.lean

Countermodels: multiplicative data and additive structure

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III. The passages this file bears on were read for this file from the extracted text: Definition 1.1 and Proposition 1.2 (log-links, log-shells; pages 26 to 35), Theorem 3.11 (i), (ii) (pages 153 to 157), and Step (i) of the proof of Corollary 3.12 (page 175), where the Θ×μ_LGP-link is described as a poly-isomorphism with a "unit portion" and a "value group portion". No formula of the papers is transcribed here, so no page render was needed.

None of the statements below is a counterexample to a statement of the IUT papers. They refute a simplified abstraction: "a map that respects the multiplicative structure of a field respects its additive structure", together with variants in which some extra data (a Galois action, a topology, an order, the unit group) is also respected. In the classification of the project they are counterexamples to a simplified abstraction. The passages read do not assert that multiplicative data alone determines the ring; the point of the examples is to show which extra data does and does not close the gap, so that an argument that relies on such a reconstruction must name the data it uses.

The positive statement additive_of_mul_of_translate shows that very little additive data suffices: compatibility with the single translation x ↦ x + 1.

For the unit group ℤ_p^× itself, padicInt_units_inv_not_ringHom shows that inversion, a group automorphism of ℤ_p^×, is induced by no ring endomorphism of ℤ_p. Power maps u ↦ u^k on ℤ_p^× were not attempted.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.inv_comm_ringEquivtheorem

theorem inv_comm_ringEquiv {K : Type*} [Field K] (σ : K ≃+* K) (x : K) :
  σ x⁻¹ = (σ x)⁻¹

Inversion commutes with every ring automorphism of every field. So equivariance with respect to a group of field automorphisms (a "Galois action") cannot distinguish inversion, which is not additive, from the identity.

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.inv_not_ringHomtheorem

theorem inv_not_ringHom {K : Type*} [Field K] (h2 : (2 : K) ≠ 0) (h3 : (3 : K) ≠ 0) :
  ¬ ∃ σ : K →+* K, ∀ x : K, x ≠ 0 → σ x = x⁻¹

On a field in which 2 ≠ 0 and 3 ≠ 0, inversion agrees with no ring endomorphism on the nonzero elements: a ring map fixes 2, while 2⁻¹ = 2 would force 3 = 0.

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.padic_inv_countermodeltheorem

theorem padic_inv_countermodel (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] :
  let f : ℚ_[p] → ℚ_[p] := fun x => x⁻¹
  Function.Bijective f ∧ f 0 = 0 ∧ f 1 = 1 ∧ (∀ x y, f (x * y) = f x * f y) ∧
    (∀ σ : ℚ_[p] ≃+* ℚ_[p], ∀ x, f (σ x) = σ (f x)) ∧ ContinuousOn f {0}ᶜ ∧
    (∀ x : ℚ_[p], ‖x‖ = 1 → ‖f x‖ = 1) ∧ ‖f p‖ ≠ ‖(p : ℚ_[p])‖ ∧
    ¬ ∃ σ : ℚ_[p] →+* ℚ_[p], ∀ x, x ≠ 0 → σ x = f x

Local field countermodel. On ℚ_p, inversion (with 0⁻¹ = 0) is a bijection that preserves 0, 1 and multiplication, commutes with every field automorphism, is continuous on ℚ_p^×, and maps the unit group {‖x‖ = 1} onto itself. It is nevertheless induced by no ring endomorphism, and it does not preserve the absolute value: it sends p, of norm p⁻¹, to an element of norm p. So the multiplicative monoid of ℚ_p with its topology, its unit group and the action of its field automorphisms does not determine the additive structure, and an isomorphism that is the identity on units can still reverse the value group. Bears on: the description of the Θ×μ_LGP-link as a unit portion together with a value group portion (IUT III, page 175, Step (i) of the proof of Corollary 3.12) and the fact that log-shells are built from units only (IUT III, Remark 1.1.1 (i), page 28). The example says nothing about reconstructions from richer data, such as the absolute Galois group of ℚ_p used in the papers.

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.padicInt_units_inv_not_ringHomtheorem

theorem padicInt_units_inv_not_ringHom (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] :
  ¬ ∃ σ : ℤ_[p] →+* ℤ_[p], ∀ u : ℤ_[p]ˣ, σ u = ((u⁻¹ : ℤ_[p]ˣ) : ℤ_[p])

Countermodel on a unit group. Inversion is a group automorphism of ℤ_p^× (MulEquiv.inv), but no ring endomorphism of ℤ_p agrees with it on units: a ring map fixes the unit 1 + p, while (1 + p)² = 1 would give p (p + 2) = 0 in the domain ℤ_p. So an automorphism of the unit group of the ring of integers of a local field need not come from the ring.

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.squareValuedef

noncomputable def squareValue (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (x : ℚ_[p]) : ℚ_[p] :=
  x * (p : ℚ_[p]) ^ x.valuation

The map x ↦ x · p^{v(x)} on ℚ_p, where v is the p-adic valuation. It is the identity on units and doubles valuations: on x = p^n u it gives p^{2n} u.

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.squareValue_countermodeltheorem

theorem squareValue_countermodel (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] :
  squareValue p 0 = 0 ∧ squareValue p 1 = 1 ∧
    (∀ x y, squareValue p (x * y) = squareValue p x * squareValue p y) ∧
    Function.Injective (squareValue p) ∧
    (∀ x : ℚ_[p], x ≠ 0 → ‖x‖ = 1 → squareValue p x = x) ∧
    (∀ x : ℚ_[p], x ≠ 0 → (squareValue p x).valuation = 2 * x.valuation) ∧
    ¬ ∀ x y, squareValue p (x + y) = squareValue p x + squareValue p y

Countermodel shaped like a unit portion plus a value group portion. The map squareValue p preserves 0, 1 and multiplication, is injective, and is the identity on every element of norm 1 (the units of ℤ_p). It doubles the valuation of every nonzero element. It is not additive. So a multiplicative map that is the identity on the unit portion and acts on the value group portion by a homomorphism of value groups (here multiplication by 2) need not be compatible with addition. This is a model of the abstraction "identify the units, rescale the value group"; it is not a model of the Θ×μ_LGP-link, which is a poly-isomorphism of Frobenioid theoretic prime-strips rather than a map on a field (IUT III, page 175).

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.real_cube_countermodeltheorem

theorem real_cube_countermodel :
  let f : ℝ → ℝ := fun x => x ^ 3
  StrictMono f ∧ Continuous f ∧ Function.Bijective f ∧ f 0 = 0 ∧ f 1 = 1 ∧
    (∀ x y, f (x * y) = f x * f y) ∧ ¬ ∃ σ : ℝ →+* ℝ, ∀ x, σ x = f x

Countermodel with order and topology. Cubing on ℝ is a strictly monotone, continuous bijection that preserves 0, 1 and multiplication, and it is not additive. The only ring endomorphism of ℝ is the identity (Real.RingHom.unique), so cubing is induced by no ring map. Hence the multiplicative monoid of ℝ together with its order and its topology does not determine the additive structure.

IUT.Countermodels.MulAdd.additive_of_mul_of_translatetheorem

theorem additive_of_mul_of_translate {K : Type*} [Field K] (f : K → K)
  (hmul : ∀ x y, f (x * y) = f x * f y) (h0 : f 0 = 0) (h1 : ∀ x, f (x + 1) = f x + 1) :
  ∀ x y, f (x + y) = f x + f y

Positive statement. On any field, a map that preserves multiplication, sends 0 to 0, and commutes with the translation x ↦ x + 1 is additive (hence a ring endomorphism, since f 1 = f 0 + 1 = 1). For y ≠ 0: f (x + y) = f (y · (x/y + 1)) = f y · (f (x/y) + 1) = f x + f y. So multiplicative data together with even a very small piece of additive data determines the additive structure. The countermodels above show that this piece cannot be replaced by an order, a topology, the unit group, or equivariance under field automorphisms.

IUT.Countermodels.ReconstructionComparison

IUT/Countermodels/ReconstructionComparison.lean

Countermodel: a link plus per-copy reconstruction does not give a comparison

Module documentation

Source context: Mochizuki, Inter-universal Teichmüller Theory III, Corollary 3.12 and its proof, pages 173 to 186, read for this file from the extracted text; in particular Steps (xi-a) to (xi-g), pages 181 to 184, and Step (xii), pages 185 and 186. Also Theorem 3.11 (i), (ii), pages 153 to 157, and Proposition 3.9, pages 115 to 117 (page 115 also read from the rendered page). No formula of the papers is transcribed here.

What this file is

This is a model of an abstraction, not a model of IUT, and it refutes no statement of the papers. The abstraction is: "two copies of a structure, each of which reconstructs its own real-valued invariant, joined by a link that identifies only part of the structure, already determine a relation between the two invariants". The model:

  • a normalized line is the additive group ℝ equipped with a Haar measure c · volume (c > 0, the normalization) and a pilot region [0, a] (a > 0);
  • its invariant is the log-volume log ((c · volume) [0, a]) of the pilot region, computed from the line's own data;
  • per-copy reconstruction holds: the invariant is preserved by every measurable bijection that carries the measure to the measure and the pilot region to the pilot region (invariant_of_iso);
  • a link between two normalized lines is an automorphism of the additive group ℝ (here even continuous and order preserving) that carries the pilot region of the first onto the pilot region of the second. It identifies the additive structure and the pilot objects, and nothing about the normalizations.

Results:

  • link_and_reconstruction_no_relation: for any positive pilot lengths and any two real numbers α, β, there are normalizations such that a link exists and the invariants are α and β. So the link together with per-copy reconstruction implies no relation at all between the invariants: not =, not ≤, not ≥.
  • invariant_le_of_volume_compatible (positive): if the link is in addition volume compatible (it carries the first measure to the second) and carries the first pilot region into the second, then the first invariant is at most the second; with equality of regions it is an equality (invariant_of_iso).
Which hypotheses of the actual theorem the model does and does not satisfy

The model contains: two copies of a structure carrying a real-valued log-volume; a link that identifies a pilot object of one copy with a pilot object of the other; the internal computability of each copy's log-volume.

The model does not contain: Hodge theaters, Frobenioids, Kummer theory, the log-link, the log-Kummer correspondence, tensor packets, the multiradial algorithm of Theorem 3.11, the indeterminacies (Ind1), (Ind2), (Ind3), the holomorphic hull, or the properties (IPL), (SHE), (APT) of Remark 3.11.1 (not read for this file) on which Steps (xi-b) to (xi-f) rely. In the proof of Corollary 3.12 the two log-volumes are not compared through a bare link: Step (xi-b) to (xi-e) express both, via the multiradial representation and (SHE), relative to the single arithmetic holomorphic structure of the 1-column. The named extra compatibility of this model, volume compatibility of the link, is the toy counterpart of what those steps are meant to supply. The model therefore shows only that some such compatibility is indispensable; it does not show that the papers fail to supply it. The linearity of the link here also places the model inside the hypothesis "(Lin)" discussed in IUT.Audit.ScholzeStix.Diagram, which Mochizuki disputes for the situation of the papers.

Public declarations

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.NormalizedLinestructure

structure NormalizedLine where

A normalized line: the additive group ℝ with the measure scale · volume and the pilot region [0, pilotLength]. Only data; no property is assumed. The intended case is 0 < scale and 0 < pilotLength: then measure is a Haar measure and the pilot region has finite positive measure. The structure does not enforce this; for scale ≤ 0, measure is the zero measure (not a Haar measure) and invariant takes the value 0 by totalization. Every countermodel below produces lines with positive data, and invariant_eq assumes it.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.NormalizedLine.measuredef

noncomputable def measure (T : NormalizedLine) : Measure ℝ := ENNReal.ofReal T.scale • volume

The measure scale · volume of a normalized line: a Haar measure when 0 < scale, the zero measure when scale ≤ 0.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.NormalizedLine.pilotdef

def pilot (T : NormalizedLine) : Set ℝ := Icc 0 T.pilotLength

The pilot region [0, pilotLength].

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.NormalizedLine.invariantdef

noncomputable def invariant (T : NormalizedLine) : ℝ := Real.log (T.measure T.pilot).toReal

The invariant reconstructed from the line's own data: the log-volume of its pilot region. Meaningful when the pilot region has finite positive measure, that is, when scale and pilotLength are positive; otherwise it is 0 by totalization.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.NormalizedLine.invariant_eqtheorem

theorem invariant_eq (T : NormalizedLine) (hc : 0 < T.scale) (ha : 0 < T.pilotLength) :
  T.invariant = Real.log T.scale + Real.log T.pilotLength

For positive data, the invariant is log c + log a.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.invariant_of_isotheorem

theorem invariant_of_iso (T₀ T₁ : NormalizedLine) (e : ℝ ≃ᵐ ℝ)
  (hμ : Measure.map e T₀.measure = T₁.measure) (hP : e '' T₀.pilot = T₁.pilot) :
  T₀.invariant = T₁.invariant

Positive statement: reconstruction. The invariant is preserved by every measurable bijection that carries the measure of one normalized line to the measure of the other and the pilot region onto the pilot region.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.mulLinkdef

noncomputable def mulLink (t : ℝ) (ht : t ≠ 0) : ℝ ≃+ ℝ where
  toFun x := t * x
  invFun x := t⁻¹ * x
  left_inv x := by simp [ht]
  right_inv x := by simp [ht]
  map_add' x y := mul_add t x y

Multiplication by a nonzero real number as an automorphism of the additive group ℝ.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.link_and_reconstruction_no_relationtheorem

theorem link_and_reconstruction_no_relation (a₀ a₁ : ℝ) (h₀ : 0 < a₀) (h₁ : 0 < a₁)
  (α β : ℝ) :
  ∃ c₀ c₁ : ℝ, 0 < c₀ ∧ 0 < c₁ ∧
    (∃ φ : ℝ ≃+ ℝ, Continuous φ ∧ StrictMono φ ∧
      φ '' (NormalizedLine.mk c₀ a₀).pilot = (NormalizedLine.mk c₁ a₁).pilot) ∧
    (NormalizedLine.mk c₀ a₀).invariant = α ∧ (NormalizedLine.mk c₁ a₁).invariant = β

Countermodel. Fix any pilot lengths a₀, a₁ > 0 and any real numbers α, β. There are normalizations c₀, c₁ > 0 such that the normalized lines T₀ = (c₀, a₀), T₁ = (c₁, a₁) are joined by a link (a continuous, strictly increasing automorphism of the additive group ℝ carrying the pilot region of T₀ onto that of T₁), and T₀.invariant = α, T₁.invariant = β. Each invariant is reconstructed inside its own line (invariant_of_iso), yet the link plus the two reconstructions impose no relation between them.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.link_compatible_with_both_orderstheorem

theorem link_compatible_with_both_orders (a₀ a₁ : ℝ) (h₀ : 0 < a₀) (h₁ : 0 < a₁) :
  (∃ c₀ c₁ : ℝ, 0 < c₀ ∧ 0 < c₁ ∧
    (∃ φ : ℝ ≃+ ℝ, Continuous φ ∧ StrictMono φ ∧
      φ '' (NormalizedLine.mk c₀ a₀).pilot = (NormalizedLine.mk c₁ a₁).pilot) ∧
    (NormalizedLine.mk c₀ a₀).invariant < (NormalizedLine.mk c₁ a₁).invariant) ∧
  (∃ c₀ c₁ : ℝ, 0 < c₀ ∧ 0 < c₁ ∧
    (∃ φ : ℝ ≃+ ℝ, Continuous φ ∧ StrictMono φ ∧
      φ '' (NormalizedLine.mk c₀ a₀).pilot = (NormalizedLine.mk c₁ a₁).pilot) ∧
    (NormalizedLine.mk c₁ a₁).invariant < (NormalizedLine.mk c₀ a₀).invariant)

The countermodel in the form relevant to an inequality: a link and per-copy reconstruction are compatible both with T₀.invariant < T₁.invariant and with T₁.invariant < T₀.invariant, for the same positive pilot lengths.

IUT.Countermodels.Comparison.invariant_le_of_volume_compatibletheorem

theorem invariant_le_of_volume_compatible (T₀ T₁ : NormalizedLine) (e : ℝ ≃ᵐ ℝ)
  (hμ : Measure.map e T₀.measure = T₁.measure) (hP : e '' T₀.pilot ⊆ T₁.pilot)
  (hpos : T₀.measure T₀.pilot ≠ 0) (hfin : T₁.measure T₁.pilot ≠ ⊤) :
  T₀.invariant ≤ T₁.invariant

Positive statement: comparison from volume compatibility. If a link (any measurable bijection) is volume compatible, that is, carries the measure of T₀ to the measure of T₁, and carries the pilot region of T₀ into the pilot region of T₁, then T₀.invariant ≤ T₁.invariant, provided the pilot region of T₀ has positive measure and that of T₁ finite measure. Inclusion rather than equality is the toy counterpart of an upper semi-compatibility such as (Ind3) of IUT III, Theorem 3.11 (ii), page 156.
